<purpose>
This document contains the source code for the RiskQuant 'CREDIT_REDUCED' profile.
Use this structural map and source code as the ground-truth context for answering subsequent queries.
This is god-tier quantitative code, not typical Python scripts. Pay close attention to the structure and patterns used.
RiskQuant prioritizes Data-Oriented Design (DOD), Structures-of-Arrays (SoA), bare-metal memory sympathy, and absolute maximum throughput. The core philosophy of this codebase is: Human-readable at the edge, C-contiguous at the core. Strings must die early. Bare-metal performance is the north star.
</purpose>

```python
# src/riskquant/constants.py

"""
Common constants used across the riskquant codebase.

This module separates:
- typed sentinel values used in numeric payloads / arrays
- general configuration constants used by the portfolio engine
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from datetime import date

import numpy as np

# ==============================================================================
# Typed sentinel values for payload fields
# ==============================================================================

# Universal sentinel for "no valid ID".
# Used across instrument_ids, underlying_ids, volatility_ids, and discount_curve_ids.
NO_ID = np.int32(-999_999)

# Universal sentinel for missing dates (settlement, delivery, expiry).
# Using -1 allows for clean `date_ord >= 0` masking.
NO_DATE_ORD32 = np.int32(-99_999)

# Specific sentinel for FX: No conversion required (Underlying CCY == Settlement CCY)
NO_FX_RF_REQUIRED = np.int32(-1)

# Invalid UIX sentinel for any universe index (*_uix fields).
# Used exclusively in the Execution Engine (blocks/compilers), never in Ingest.
INVALID_UIX = np.int32(-999)

# NOT IMPLEMENTED sentinels for fields expected to be populated but aren't yet.
NOT_IMPLEMENTED_SENTINEL: np.int8 = np.int8(-99)

# ==============================================================================
# Option encoding
# ==============================================================================

# 1 for call options, -1 for put options, 0 for not-an-option.
# Allows for branchless payoff calculation: payoff * cp_mult
CALL_OPTION_MULT = np.int8(1)
PUT_OPTION_MULT = np.int8(-1)
NOT_OPTION_MULT = np.int8(0)

# ==============================================================================
# Date encoding
# ==============================================================================

DATE_EPOCH: date = date(1970, 1, 1)

# ==============================================================================
# Numeric configuration
# ==============================================================================

ROUND_DECIMALS_LEVEL = 8
ROUND_DECIMALS_QUANTITY = 8
ROUND_DECIMALS_STRIKE_PRICE = 8

POSITION_DUST_TOLERANCE = 1e-8
PD_DUST_TOLERANCE = 1e-10

# ============================================
# --- DEVELOPMENT-ONLY FALLBACK VALUES
# ============================================

FALLBACK_MOCK_VOLATILITY_DO_NOT_SHIP: float = 0.50

# ==============================================================================
# --- EWMA Volatility Calculation Constants
# ==============================================================================

EWMA_DECAY_FACTOR: float = 0.94
MAX_EWMA_VOL_RATIO: float = 10.0
MIN_EWMA_VARIANCE: float = 1e-12

# ==============================================================================
# --- Credit Risk Constants
# ==============================================================================

NO_PARENT_OBLIGOR: np.int64 = np.int64(-1)
NO_RATING_ID: np.int8 = np.int8(0)
NO_SECTOR_ID: np.int32 = np.int32(-1)
NO_COUNTRY_ID: np.int32 = np.int32(-1)

# ==============================================================================
# RANDOM SEED FOR TESTING
# ==============================================================================

RNG_SEED: int = 42
```

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\credit\_uid_join.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/credit/_uid_join.py
"""
Vectorized uid-join primitives for the credit package.

Every credit module that joins an int64 uid array against an ascending-
sorted master uid array — the compile-edge invariant: every compiled
master in this codebase has `uid` ascending sorted — shares one of
three patterns: strict lookup that raises on absence, soft lookup that
maps absences to a sentinel, and existence-only membership testing.
They are collapsed into three primitives here so the credit code has
exactly one place where the searchsorted / clip / equality dance lives.

`searchsorted_or_raise` returns int64 positions into the master for
every target uid; the caller indexes whichever master column it needs
(collateral, pd, lgd, obligor_uid, ...).

`searchsorted_or_sentinel` returns int32 positions with absences as
`NO_UID_MATCH_IDX_I32`; the caller masks on `>= 0` to filter rows.

`searchsorted_membership` returns a bool mask — the existence-only
variant used by `cross_master/validate.py`.

All three handle the empty-superset edge case explicitly: a missing
master is a join error in the strict variant and a uniform sentinel /
all-False in the soft variants.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Final

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

# Centralized preview limit for error-message diagnostics. Anything beyond
# the first ten offending uids is unreadable in a stacktrace.
_PREVIEW: Final[int] = 10

# Sentinel for "no match" in soft-lookup positions. int32 because positions
# index a target array bounded by the obligor / netting-set universe size,
# comfortably under int32 max.
NO_UID_MATCH_IDX_I32: Final[np.int32] = np.int32(-1)


def searchsorted_or_raise(
    target: npt.NDArray[np.int64],
    superset_sorted: npt.NDArray[np.int64],
    *,
    ctx: str,
) -> npt.NDArray[np.int64]:
    """
    Vectorized strict uid lookup: positions of `target` in `superset_sorted`.

    Parameters
    ----------
    target
        int64 uid array to look up. May contain duplicates.
    superset_sorted
        int64 uid array, ascending sorted, unique. Every compiled master
        in this codebase satisfies that invariant.
    ctx
        Caller-side label used in the error message (e.g.
        ``"compile_exposure: netting set uid(s)"``).

    Returns
    -------
    pos
        int64 positions such that ``superset_sorted[pos] == target``
        elementwise. Same shape as ``target``. Caller indexes master
        columns with these positions to materialize joined values.

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError
        If any ``target`` uid is absent from ``superset_sorted``, or if
        ``superset_sorted`` is empty while ``target`` is not.
    """
    n = superset_sorted.shape[0]
    if n == 0:
        if target.shape[0] == 0:
            return np.empty(0, dtype=np.int64)
        raise ValueError(
            f"{ctx}: superset is empty, {target.shape[0]} target uid(s) "
            f"cannot be resolved."
        )

    pos = np.searchsorted(superset_sorted, target)
    np.clip(pos, 0, n - 1, out=pos)
    matched = superset_sorted[pos] == target
    if not matched.all():
        bad = target[~matched][:_PREVIEW].tolist()
        n_bad = int((~matched).sum())
        raise ValueError(
            f"{ctx}: {n_bad} uid(s) absent from the superset. First {len(bad)}: {bad}"
        )
    return pos


def searchsorted_or_sentinel(
    target: npt.NDArray[np.int64],
    superset_sorted: npt.NDArray[np.int64],
) -> npt.NDArray[np.int32]:
    """
    Vectorized soft uid lookup: positions or ``NO_UID_MATCH_IDX_I32``.

    Use when absent target uids are a valid runtime case — for example,
    a netting set whose owning obligor has no row in the *target* JTD
    obligor universe and must therefore be dropped, not flagged.

    Returns
    -------
    pos
        int32 positions into ``superset_sorted``. Absent uids map to
        ``NO_UID_MATCH_IDX_I32``. Caller filters via ``keep = pos >= 0``.
    """
    n = superset_sorted.shape[0]
    if n == 0:
        return np.full(target.shape[0], NO_UID_MATCH_IDX_I32, dtype=np.int32)

    pos = np.searchsorted(superset_sorted, target)
    np.clip(pos, 0, n - 1, out=pos)
    hit = superset_sorted[pos] == target
    return np.where(hit, pos, NO_UID_MATCH_IDX_I32).astype(np.int32, copy=False)


def searchsorted_membership(
    target: npt.NDArray[np.int64],
    superset_sorted: npt.NDArray[np.int64],
) -> npt.NDArray[np.bool_]:
    """
    Vectorized existence check: bool mask of which ``target`` entries are
    present in ``superset_sorted``.

    Used by ``cross_master/validate.py`` where presence is the answer
    and no positions or values need to be materialized.
    """
    n = superset_sorted.shape[0]
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(target.shape[0], dtype=np.bool_)
    pos = np.searchsorted(superset_sorted, target)
    np.clip(pos, 0, n - 1, out=pos)
    return superset_sorted[pos] == target
```

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\credit\cross_master\validate.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/credit/cross_master/validate.py
"""
Layered cross-master validators for the credit package.

Single-master validators (`obligors/validate.py`, `netting_set/validate.py`)
enforce intra-table invariants — dtype, range, uniqueness, sortedness,
self-references. They are intentionally unaware of other masters: an
obligor master that imports the netting-set master, or vice versa, would
couple two edges that should compile independently.

Layered invariants — facts that hold *across* tables — live here. The
canonical layered invariant is the foreign-key relationship from
`CompiledNettingSetMaster.obligor_uid` into `CompiledObligorCredit.uid`:
every netting-set row must point at an obligor that exists on the master.

This package is consumed by the top-level pipeline orchestrator (a future
`compile_credit_pipeline(...)` entry point) and by any caller that holds
both masters and wants to fail fast before they reach a downstream join.
Pure vectorized predicates on already-compiled SoA — no row loops, no
pandas, no string lookups.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Final

from riskquant.credit._uid_join import searchsorted_membership
from riskquant.credit.netting_set.compiled import CompiledNettingSetMaster
from riskquant.credit.obligors.compiled import CompiledObligorCredit

_PREVIEW: Final[int] = 10


def validate_netting_set_obligor_uids_on_master(
    *,
    netting_set_master: CompiledNettingSetMaster,
    obligor_master: CompiledObligorCredit,
) -> None:
    """
    Check that every netting set's owning obligor exists on the obligor master.

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError
        If any `netting_set_master.obligor_uid` value is absent from
        `obligor_master.uid`. The error message previews up to ten
        offending (ns_uid, obligor_uid) pairs.
    """
    matched = searchsorted_membership(
        target=netting_set_master.obligor_uid,
        superset_sorted=obligor_master.uid,
    )
    if matched.all():
        return

    bad_pairs = list(
        zip(
            netting_set_master.uid[~matched][:_PREVIEW].tolist(),
            netting_set_master.obligor_uid[~matched][:_PREVIEW].tolist(),
            strict=True,
        )
    )
    n_bad = int((~matched).sum())
    raise ValueError(
        f"cross_master: {n_bad} netting set(s) reference obligor uid(s) "
        f"absent from the obligor master. First {len(bad_pairs)} "
        f"(ns_uid, obligor_uid) pair(s): {bad_pairs}"
    )


def validate_cross_masters(
    *,
    obligor_master: CompiledObligorCredit,
    netting_set_master: CompiledNettingSetMaster,
) -> None:
    """
    Single-call entry point: every cross-master invariant, fail-fast.

    Today: the netting-set -> obligor foreign key. Future layered checks
    (rating taxonomy join, sector taxonomy join, parent-obligor closure
    against an external corporate hierarchy) land here too.
    """
    validate_netting_set_obligor_uids_on_master(
        netting_set_master=netting_set_master,
        obligor_master=obligor_master,
    )
```

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\credit\exposure\compile.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/credit/exposure/compile.py

"""
Exposure compiler: raw trade -> netting-set CSR views.

PURE PROJECTION KERNEL.

-----------------------------------------------------------------------

HARD INVARIANTS (ENFORCED)

- Every trade MUST map to a netting set uid
- Every netting set uid MUST exist in the master
- No implicit singleton construction
- No partial joins
- No silent fallback logic

-----------------------------------------------------------------------

DESIGN

- Structure = CSR over netting sets
- Attributes = strict projection from master
- Join primitive = centralized (searchsorted_or_raise)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.constants import NO_NETTING_SET_ID
from riskquant.credit._uid_join import searchsorted_or_raise
from riskquant.credit.exposure.compiled import CompiledExposure
from riskquant.credit.exposure.views.replacement_cost import RcNettingSetView
from riskquant.credit.netting_set.compiled import CompiledNettingSetMaster

# =====================================================================
# Validation
# =====================================================================


def _validate_inputs(
    trade_uids: npt.NDArray[np.int64],
    trade_netting_set_id: npt.NDArray[np.int64],
) -> None:
    if trade_uids.dtype != np.int64:
        raise TypeError("trade_uids must be int64")

    if trade_netting_set_id.dtype != np.int64:
        raise TypeError("trade_netting_set_id must be int64")

    if trade_uids.ndim != 1 or trade_netting_set_id.ndim != 1:
        raise ValueError("inputs must be 1D")

    if trade_uids.shape[0] != trade_netting_set_id.shape[0]:
        raise ValueError("trade_uids and trade_netting_set_id must align")


def _validate_master(master: CompiledNettingSetMaster) -> None:
    if master.uid.dtype != np.int64:
        raise TypeError("netting_set_master.uid must be int64")

    if master.net_collateral.dtype != np.float64:
        raise TypeError("netting_set_master.net_collateral must be float64")

    if master.uid.ndim != 1:
        raise ValueError("netting_set_master.uid must be 1D")

    if master.uid.size >= 2 and not np.all(master.uid[:-1] < master.uid[1:]):
        raise ValueError("netting_set_master.uid must be strictly ascending and unique")


def _validate_no_missing_netting_sets(
    trade_netting_set_id: npt.NDArray[np.int64],
) -> None:
    if np.any(trade_netting_set_id == NO_NETTING_SET_ID):
        raise ValueError(
            "NO_NETTING_SET_ID detected. "
            "All trades must be assigned to a valid or singleton netting set upstream."
        )


# =====================================================================
# CSR construction (structure only)
# =====================================================================


def _build_csr(
    netting_set_per_row: npt.NDArray[np.int64],
) -> tuple[
    npt.NDArray[np.int64],  # netting_set_uids
    npt.NDArray[np.int64],  # offsets
    npt.NDArray[np.int64],  # mtm_row_perm
]:
    """
    Deterministic grouping of trades by netting set.

    Guarantees:
    - netting_set_uids sorted ascending
    - stable ordering within each netting set
    """

    order = np.argsort(netting_set_per_row, kind="stable")

    mtm_row_perm = np.ascontiguousarray(order, dtype=np.int64)
    sorted_ns = netting_set_per_row[order]

    netting_set_uids, counts = np.unique(sorted_ns, return_counts=True)
    netting_set_uids = np.ascontiguousarray(netting_set_uids, dtype=np.int64)

    offsets = np.empty(netting_set_uids.size + 1, dtype=np.int64)
    offsets[0] = 0
    np.cumsum(counts, out=offsets[1:])

    return netting_set_uids, offsets, mtm_row_perm


# =====================================================================
# Attribute projection (STRICT)
# =====================================================================


def _project_netting_set_attributes(
    netting_set_uids: npt.NDArray[np.int64],
    master: CompiledNettingSetMaster,
) -> tuple[
    npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # collateral
    npt.NDArray[np.bool_],  # is_collateralized
]:
    """
    Strict projection from master.

    Invariant:
    - Every netting_set_uid MUST exist in master.uid
    """

    pos = searchsorted_or_raise(
        target=netting_set_uids,
        superset_sorted=master.uid,
        ctx="compile_exposure: netting_set_uids",
    )

    collateral = np.ascontiguousarray(
        master.net_collateral[pos],
        dtype=np.float64,
    )

    is_collateralized = collateral > 0.0

    return collateral, is_collateralized


# =====================================================================
# Public API
# =====================================================================


def compile_exposure(
    *,
    trade_uids: npt.NDArray[np.int64],
    trade_netting_set_id: npt.NDArray[np.int64],
    netting_set_master: CompiledNettingSetMaster,
) -> CompiledExposure:
    """
    Compile exposure into a Replacement Cost netting-set CSR view.

    This function is a PURE projection kernel:
    - no mutation
    - no inference
    - no fallback logic

    Upstream responsibilities:
    - assign singleton netting sets
    - ensure full master coverage
    """

    # ---- Validate inputs ----
    _validate_inputs(trade_uids, trade_netting_set_id)
    _validate_master(netting_set_master)
    _validate_no_missing_netting_sets(trade_netting_set_id)

    # ---- Build structure ----
    netting_set_uids, offsets, mtm_row_perm = _build_csr(trade_netting_set_id)

    # ---- Project attributes ----
    collateral, is_collateralized = _project_netting_set_attributes(
        netting_set_uids,
        netting_set_master,
    )

    # ---- Construct view ----
    rc_view = RcNettingSetView(
        netting_set_uids=netting_set_uids,
        netting_set_offsets=offsets,
        mtm_row_perm=mtm_row_perm,
        collateral=collateral,
        is_collateralized=is_collateralized,
    )

    return CompiledExposure(replacement_cost=rc_view)
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\credit\exposure\compiled.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/credit/exposure/compiled.py
"""
Compiled exposure container.

`CompiledExposure` is the EAD-layer analogue of `CompiledPortfolio`: an ordered
set of per-model netting-set views. Each populated view contributes one
contiguous row-band to the eventual (n_netting_sets x n_scenarios) EAD matrix;
`view_offsets[i]` is its first row. `netting_set_uids` aggregates every view's
uids into one flat int64 array in band order — the join key into the JTD layer.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Iterator
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import TypeAlias

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.credit.exposure.views.replacement_cost import RcNettingSetView

ViewT: TypeAlias = RcNettingSetView  # | SaccrNettingSetView | ...  (future)


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class CompiledExposure:
    """EAD-ready exposure: an ordered set of per-model netting-set views."""

    # VIEWS: One per exposure model. Each has its own netting set membership, collateral, etc.
    replacement_cost: RcNettingSetView | None = None

    # DERIVED FIELDS: Populated at init by __post_init__. These are the fields the engine dispatches on.
    _views: tuple[ViewT, ...] = field(init=False, repr=False)
    _view_offsets: npt.NDArray[np.int64] = field(init=False, repr=False)
    _total_views: int = field(init=False, repr=False)
    _total_netting_sets: int = field(init=False, repr=False)
    _netting_set_uids: npt.NDArray[np.int64] = field(init=False, repr=False)

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        ordered: tuple[ViewT | None, ...] = (self.replacement_cost,)
        views: tuple[ViewT, ...] = tuple(v for v in ordered if v is not None)
        total_views = len(views)

        # 1. Calculate the total size needed
        total_ns = sum(v.num_netting_sets for v in views)

        # 2. Allocate uninitialized arrays (faster than zeros)
        view_offsets = np.empty(total_views, dtype=np.int64)
        netting_set_uids = np.empty(total_ns, dtype=np.int64)

        # 3. Populate both arrays in a single, clean pass
        offset = 0
        for i, v in enumerate(views):
            view_offsets[i] = offset
            n = v.num_netting_sets
            netting_set_uids[offset : offset + n] = v.netting_set_uids
            offset += n

        object.__setattr__(self, "_views", views)
        object.__setattr__(self, "_view_offsets", view_offsets)
        object.__setattr__(self, "_total_views", total_views)
        object.__setattr__(self, "_total_netting_sets", total_ns)
        object.__setattr__(self, "_netting_set_uids", netting_set_uids)

    @property
    def views(self) -> tuple[ViewT, ...]:
        return self._views

    @property
    def view_offsets(self) -> npt.NDArray[np.int64]:
        return self._view_offsets

    @property
    def total_views(self) -> int:
        return self._total_views

    @property
    def total_netting_sets(self) -> int:
        return self._total_netting_sets

    @property
    def netting_set_uids(self) -> npt.NDArray[np.int64]:
        return self._netting_set_uids

    def iter_views(self) -> Iterator[ViewT]:
        yield from self._views
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\credit\exposure\engine.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/credit/exposure/engine.py
"""
Exposure engine: model-id -> evaluator dispatch and the EAD resolution loop.

`EAD_EVALUATORS` is the EAD-layer analogue of `valuation.engine.BLOCK_EVALUATORS`:
a frozen tuple indexed by `ExposureModel` id. `run_exposure` reads each view's
`EXPOSURE_MODEL_ID` class constant, indexes the table, and dispatches — no
`type()`, no isinstance ladder. Adding SA-CCR is: one enum member, one view,
one evaluator, one `_register` line. The import-time guard rejects any model
that ships unwired.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Any, Final, Protocol, TypeVar, cast, runtime_checkable

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.credit.exposure.compiled import CompiledExposure
from riskquant.credit.exposure.evaluators.replacement_cost import (
    evaluate_replacement_cost,
)
from riskquant.credit.exposure.models import (
    NUM_EXPOSURE_MODELS,
    REPLACEMENT_COST_ID,
    ExposureModel,
)
from riskquant.credit.exposure.results import EadResult
from riskquant.credit.exposure.views.base import _NettingSetView

V_contra = TypeVar("V_contra", bound=_NettingSetView, contravariant=True)


@runtime_checkable
class EadEvaluator(Protocol[V_contra]):
    """Structural contract for all EAD-model evaluators."""

    def __call__(
        self,
        *,
        view: V_contra,
        mtm: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
        out: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    ) -> None: ...


_EAD_EVALUATORS_MUT: list[EadEvaluator[Any] | None] = [None] * NUM_EXPOSURE_MODELS


def _register(model_id: int, evaluator: EadEvaluator[Any]) -> None:
    if not isinstance(evaluator, EadEvaluator):
        raise TypeError(
            f"Evaluator for model_id={model_id} does not satisfy the "
            f"EadEvaluator protocol: {evaluator!r}"
        )
    _EAD_EVALUATORS_MUT[model_id] = evaluator


_register(REPLACEMENT_COST_ID, evaluate_replacement_cost)

EAD_EVALUATORS: Final[tuple[EadEvaluator[Any], ...]] = cast(
    tuple[EadEvaluator[Any], ...], tuple(_EAD_EVALUATORS_MUT)
)

_unregistered = [
    ExposureModel(i).name for i, ev in enumerate(EAD_EVALUATORS) if ev is None
]
if _unregistered:
    raise RuntimeError(
        f"No evaluator registered for exposure models: {_unregistered}. "
        f"Register them in exposure/engine.py."
    )


def run_exposure(
    *,
    exposure: CompiledExposure,
    mtm: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
) -> EadResult:
    """
    Resolve EAD across all model views from a portfolio MtM matrix.

    Walks the compiled views in band order, dispatches each on its
    `EXPOSURE_MODEL_ID`, and writes into contiguous bands of one pre-allocated
    (n_netting_sets x n_scenarios) output. Mirror of `run_valuation`.
    """
    n_sets = exposure.total_netting_sets
    n_scenarios = mtm.shape[1]

    ead: npt.NDArray[np.float64] = np.empty(
        (n_sets, n_scenarios), dtype=np.float64, order="C"
    )

    views = exposure.views
    offsets = exposure.view_offsets
    evaluators_table = EAD_EVALUATORS

    for i in range(exposure.total_views):
        view = views[i]
        start = int(offsets[i])
        end = start + view.num_netting_sets

        evaluator = evaluators_table[view.EXPOSURE_MODEL_ID]
        evaluator(view=view, mtm=mtm, out=ead[start:end, :])

    return EadResult(uid=exposure.netting_set_uids, ead=ead)
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\credit\exposure\evaluators\replacement_cost.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/credit/exposure/evaluators/replacement_cost.py
"""
Replacement Cost (RC) EAD model: kernel + evaluator.

    V  = sum of MtM over the netting set's member trades
    RC = max(V - C, 0)

`_kernel_replacement_cost` computes RC for all netting sets and scenarios
in one parallel pass. The kernel uses positional-or-keyword arguments
(NOT keyword-only): Numba's parsing of the `*,` marker is unreliable
across versions and inconsistent with every other JTD kernel in the
codebase. The evaluator wrapper materializes the SoA fields from the
view before crossing the JIT boundary.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import numba as nb  # type: ignore
import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.credit.exposure.views.replacement_cost import RcNettingSetView


@nb.njit(
    parallel=True,
    fastmath={"contract", "reassoc", "nsz"},
    nogil=True,
    boundscheck=False,
    error_model="numpy",
    cache=True,
)  # type: ignore[misc]
def _kernel_replacement_cost(
    out: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (N, S)
    mtm: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (M_total, S)
    mtm_row_perm: npt.NDArray[np.int64],  # (M_kept,)
    ns_offsets: npt.NDArray[np.int64],  # (N + 1,)
    collateral: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (N,)
) -> None:
    n_net_sets, s_scenarios = out.shape
    for n in nb.prange(n_net_sets):
        start = ns_offsets[n]
        end = ns_offsets[n + 1]
        coll = collateral[n]
        out_row = out[n]
        if end > start:
            row0 = mtm[mtm_row_perm[start]]
            for s in range(s_scenarios):
                out_row[s] = row0[s]
            for i in range(start + 1, end):
                row = mtm[mtm_row_perm[i]]
                for s in range(s_scenarios):
                    out_row[s] += row[s]
        else:
            for s in range(s_scenarios):
                out_row[s] = 0.0
        # Branchless floor: RC = max(V - C, 0).
        for s in range(s_scenarios):
            v = out_row[s] - coll
            out_row[s] = v * (v > 0.0)


def evaluate_replacement_cost(
    *,
    view: RcNettingSetView,
    mtm: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    out: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
) -> None:
    """Unpack the view SoA fields and dispatch to the JIT kernel."""
    _kernel_replacement_cost(
        out,
        mtm,
        view.mtm_row_perm,
        view.netting_set_offsets,
        view.collateral,
    )
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/exposure/models.py
"""
Exposure-model dispatch identifiers.

`ExposureModel` is the EAD-layer analogue of `portfolio.instruments.Instrument`:
the single integer the exposure engine dispatches on. It indexes directly into
`EAD_EVALUATORS`. The enum holds only IMPLEMENTED models — it grows by one
member each time a model is wired, and the import-time guard in `engine.py`
fails loudly if a member ships without an evaluator.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from enum import IntEnum
from typing import Final


class ExposureModel(IntEnum):
    REPLACEMENT_COST = 0  # RC(NS) = MAX(MTM(NS) - COLLATERAL(NS), 0)


NUM_EXPOSURE_MODELS: Final[int] = len(ExposureModel)

REPLACEMENT_COST_ID: Final[int] = ExposureModel.REPLACEMENT_COST.value
```

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/exposure/results.py

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class EadResult:
    """
    Exposure-at-default, resolved per effective netting set.

    Row i of every array describes the netting set identified by ``uid[i]``.
    ``uid`` is the join key into the downstream credit layer (the ``uid``
    column consumed by the deterministic / copula JTD ingest cases).
    """

    uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64]  # (N,)     effective-netting-set identifier
    ead: npt.NDArray[np.float64]  # (N, S)   EAD per netting set, per scenario

    def print(
        self,
        *,
        max_rows: int = 10,
        precision: int = 0,
        summary: bool = True,
    ) -> None:
        """
        Pretty-print the EAD result.

        Parameters
        ----------
        max_rows
            Maximum number of rows to display.
        precision
            Number of decimal places for floats.
        summary
            Whether to print summary statistics.
        """
        n_rows, n_scenarios = self.ead.shape

        print(f"EadResult: {n_rows} netting sets × {n_scenarios} scenarios")

        display_rows = min(n_rows, max_rows)

        print(f"\n{'UID':>12} | EAD (per scenario)")
        print("-" * 50)

        # Local print-options scope: never mutate numpy globals.
        with np.printoptions(precision=precision, suppress=True):
            for i in range(display_rows):
                uid_str = f"{self.uid[i]:>12_.0f}"
                ead_str = np.array2string(
                    self.ead[i],
                    separator=", ",
                    formatter={"float_kind": lambda x: f"{x:>18_.{precision}f}"},
                )
                print(f"{uid_str} | {ead_str}")

        if display_rows < n_rows:
            print(f"... ({n_rows - display_rows} more rows)")

        if summary:
            print("\nSummary statistics (across all netting sets & scenarios):")
            print(f"  Mean : {self.ead.mean():>18_.{precision}f}")
            print(f"  Std  : {self.ead.std():>18_.{precision}f}")
            print(f"  Min  : {self.ead.min():>18_.{precision}f}")
            print(f"  Max  : {self.ead.max():>18_.{precision}f}")
```

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\credit\exposure\views\base.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/credit/exposure/views/base.py
"""
Base class for compiled netting-set views.

`_NettingSetView` carries only what is universal to every EAD model: the CSR
band layout that groups portfolio-order MtM rows into contiguous per-netting-set
spans. All model-specific SoA arrays (collateral, notionals, supervisory
factors, ...) live on the concrete subclasses.

`EXPOSURE_MODEL_ID` is the integer the exposure engine dispatches on — it
indexes `EAD_EVALUATORS` directly. The `_UNSET` sentinel is rejected at
class-creation time, a compile-time guard against adding a model view without
wiring its id.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import ClassVar

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

_UNSET_EXPOSURE_MODEL_ID: int = -999


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class _NettingSetView:
    """Universal CSR base for compiled netting-set views."""

    EXPOSURE_MODEL_ID: ClassVar[int] = _UNSET_EXPOSURE_MODEL_ID

    # Effective-netting-set identifiers, one per band. dtype: int64
    netting_set_uids: npt.NDArray[np.int64]  # (N,)

    # Exclusive prefix-sum band boundaries into the reordered MtM matrix. int64
    netting_set_offsets: npt.NDArray[np.int64]  # (N + 1,)

    # Permutation: global portfolio MtM rows -> NS-contiguous order. int64
    mtm_row_perm: npt.NDArray[np.int64]  # (M_view,)

    def __init_subclass__(cls, **kwargs: object) -> None:
        is_abstract_base = cls.__name__.startswith("_")
        if not is_abstract_base and (cls.EXPOSURE_MODEL_ID == _UNSET_EXPOSURE_MODEL_ID):
            raise TypeError(
                f"Netting-set view '{cls.__name__}' must set EXPOSURE_MODEL_ID "
                f"(its ExposureModel id) for engine dispatch."
            )

    @property
    def num_netting_sets(self) -> int:
        return self.netting_set_uids.shape[0]

    @property
    def num_trades(self) -> int:
        return self.mtm_row_perm.shape[0]
```

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/exposure/views/replacement_cost.py

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import ClassVar

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.credit.exposure.models import REPLACEMENT_COST_ID
from riskquant.credit.exposure.views.base import _NettingSetView


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class RcNettingSetView(_NettingSetView):
    """Replacement Cost netting-set view: CSR grouping + net collateral."""

    EXPOSURE_MODEL_ID: ClassVar[int] = REPLACEMENT_COST_ID

    collateral: npt.NDArray[np.float64]
    is_collateralized: npt.NDArray[np.bool_]

    def print(
        self,
        *,
        max_rows: int = 10,
        precision: int = 2,
        show_collateral: bool = True,
        add_space_before_table: bool = True,
    ) -> None:
        """
        Pretty-print the RcNettingSetView.

        Parameters
        ----------
        max_rows
            Maximum number of netting sets to display.
        precision
            Number of decimal places for floats.
        show_collateral
            Whether to display the collateral column.
        add_space_before_table
            Insert a blank line before the table header.
        """
        n_sets = self.num_netting_sets
        n_trades = self.num_trades

        if add_space_before_table:
            print()

        print(f"RcNettingSetView: {n_sets} netting sets, {n_trades} trades")

        display_rows = min(n_sets, max_rows)

        header = f"{'Netting Set UID':>16}"
        if show_collateral:
            header += f" | {'Collateral':>12}"
        header += f" | {'Netting Set Offsets':>20}"
        print()
        print(header)
        print("-" * 80)

        with np.printoptions(precision=precision, suppress=True):
            for i in range(display_rows):
                uid_str = f"{self.netting_set_uids[i]:>16_.0f}"

                collateral_str = ""
                if show_collateral:
                    collateral_str = f" | {self.collateral[i]:>12_.{precision}f}"

                offsets = self.netting_set_offsets[i : i + 2]
                offsets_str = f" | [{offsets[0]:>6}, {offsets[1]:>6})"

                print(f"{uid_str}{collateral_str}{offsets_str}")

            if display_rows < n_sets:
                print(f"... ({n_sets - display_rows} more netting sets)")

            print(f"\nSummary statistics (across {n_sets} netting sets):")
            print(f"  Total collateral: {self.collateral.sum():>16_.{precision}f}")
            print(f"  Mean collateral : {self.collateral.mean():>16_.{precision}f}")
            print(f"  Std collateral  : {self.collateral.std():>16_.{precision}f}")
            print(f"  Min collateral  : {self.collateral.min():>16_.{precision}f}")
            print(f"  Max collateral  : {self.collateral.max():>16_.{precision}f}")

            print(f"\nPermutation (first {min(10, n_trades)} rows):")
            print(f"  mtm_row_perm[:10] = {self.mtm_row_perm[:10]}")
            if n_trades > 10:
                print(f"  ... ({n_trades - 10} more)")
```

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\credit\factors\simulate\base.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/credit/factors/simulate/base.py
"""
Credit-factor scenario generation contract.

A credit-factor batch holds LATENT draws (systemic Z, idiosyncratic eps,
chi-square mixing W) — not market shocks. There is no base level, shift type,
or shock scale: latent factors are a different sample space, so this is a
credit-native protocol, deliberately NOT `simlab`'s `ScenarioGenerator`.

`build_credit_factor_matrix` assembles streamed batches into the C-contiguous
`(num_credit_factors, num_scenarios)` `CreditFactorMatrix` the JTD kernels read.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Iterable, Iterator
from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Protocol

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.credit.factors.state import CreditFactorMatrix


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class CreditFactorBatch:
    """
    A streamed batch of latent credit-factor draws.

    `latent_factors` is `(batch_size, F)` — scenario-major, the streaming-
    friendly layout. `build_credit_factor_matrix` transposes it into the
    factor-major `CreditFactorMatrix`.
    """

    latent_factors: npt.NDArray[np.float64]  # (batch_size, F)
    scenario_offset: int
    scenario_count_total: int


class CreditFactorGenerator(Protocol):
    """Streaming-first credit-factor generation."""

    total_scenarios: int
    available_factor_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int32]

    def iter_batches(self, *, batch_size: int) -> Iterator[CreditFactorBatch]: ...


def build_credit_factor_matrix(
    batches: Iterable[CreditFactorBatch],
    *,
    num_factors: int,
    num_scenarios: int,
) -> CreditFactorMatrix:
    """
    Assemble streamed `(batch, F)` batches into a `(F, S)` C-contiguous matrix.

    The transpose happens once, at the edge, during assembly — the JTD kernels
    then index factor rows with the scenario axis contiguous.
    """
    latent: npt.NDArray[np.float64] = np.empty(
        (num_factors, num_scenarios), dtype=np.float64, order="C"
    )
    for batch in batches:
        off = batch.scenario_offset
        bs = batch.latent_factors.shape[0]
        latent[:, off : off + bs] = batch.latent_factors.T

    return CreditFactorMatrix(latent_factors=latent)
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\credit\factors\simulate\parametric_copula_gaussian.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/credit/factors/simulate/parametric_copula_gaussian.py
"""Gaussian-copula latent-factor generator (zero-allocation, streaming).

Emits independent standard-normal draws for the full credit-factor universe.
All dependence — single-factor `beta` mixing or multi-factor CSR loadings —
is applied downstream in the JTD kernels, never here.

CONTRACT: yielded `latent_factors` are views into a reused C-contiguous
buffer; consumers treat them as ephemeral (read-only) or copy.

LAYOUT: the buffer is C-order ``(batch_size, n_factors)``. The partial-
batch slice ``buffer[:bs, :]`` is therefore C-contiguous for any
``bs <= batch_size`` — required by ``rng.standard_normal(out=...)``,
which rejects non-contiguous output arrays. (An F-order layout would
break here on the final partial batch.)
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Iterator
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import cast

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.credit.factors.simulate.base import (
    CreditFactorBatch,
    CreditFactorGenerator,
)


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class GaussianCopulaGenerator(CreditFactorGenerator):
    """Zero-allocation independent standard-normal latent-factor generator."""

    available_factor_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int32]
    total_scenarios: int
    seed: int | None = field(default=None)

    def iter_batches(self, *, batch_size: int) -> Iterator[CreditFactorBatch]:
        if batch_size <= 0:
            raise ValueError("batch_size must be positive")

        n_factors = int(self.available_factor_ids.shape[0])
        rng = np.random.default_rng(self.seed)

        # C-order: buffer[:bs, :] is C-contiguous for every bs <= batch_size,
        # the precondition `rng.standard_normal(out=...)` requires.
        buffer = np.empty((batch_size, n_factors), dtype=np.float64, order="C")

        scenario_offset = 0
        total = self.total_scenarios

        while scenario_offset < total:
            bs = min(batch_size, total - scenario_offset)
            view = buffer[:bs, :]

            rng.standard_normal(out=cast(np.ndarray, view))

            yield CreditFactorBatch(
                latent_factors=view,
                scenario_offset=scenario_offset,
                scenario_count_total=total,
            )
            scenario_offset += bs
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\credit\factors\simulate\parametric_copula_t.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/credit/factors/simulate/parametric_copula_t.py
"""Student-t-copula latent-factor generator (zero-allocation, streaming).

Emits the RAW latent universe: standard-normal columns for systemic / idio
factors and chi-square columns for the mixing variables W. The Student-t
transform ``sqrt(nu_i / W)`` is applied ONCE, per obligor, inside
``kernel_sf_t_copula_jtd`` — never pre-applied here.

`nu_by_chi` is a per-chi-factor SoA array, not a hidden scalar default.
Chi-square draws use ``standard_gamma`` (which supports ``out=``);
``chisquare`` does not. There is no Cholesky here — systemic correlation
belongs to multi-factor loadings, and single-factor idiosyncratic draws
are independent by definition.

CONTRACT: yielded `latent_factors` are views into a reused buffer.

LAYOUT
------
- Main buffer is C-order ``(batch_size, n_factors)`` so the partial-batch
  slice ``buffer[:bs, :]`` is C-contiguous — required by
  ``rng.standard_normal(out=...)``.
- A separate 1D ``chi_scratch`` buffer of length ``batch_size`` is reused
  for each chi-square fill. The scratch is contiguous; the strided write
  ``buffer[:bs, chi_cols[c]] = chi_scratch[:bs]`` happens after the gamma
  draw, NOT during it. Filling chi columns directly in the main C-order
  buffer would require a non-contiguous ``out=``, which numpy rejects.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Iterator
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import cast

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.credit.factors.simulate.base import (
    CreditFactorBatch,
    CreditFactorGenerator,
)


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class TCopulaGenerator(CreditFactorGenerator):
    """
    Zero-allocation Student-t-copula latent-factor generator.

    `chi_factor_columns` lists the factor-axis positions that carry a
    chi-square mixing variable W; `nu_by_chi` is the degrees-of-freedom
    array aligned to it. Every other column is a standard-normal latent
    factor.
    """

    available_factor_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int32]
    total_scenarios: int
    chi_factor_columns: npt.NDArray[np.int32]  # (n_chi,) positions in the F axis
    nu_by_chi: npt.NDArray[np.float64]  # (n_chi,) dof, aligned
    seed: int | None = field(default=None)

    def iter_batches(self, *, batch_size: int) -> Iterator[CreditFactorBatch]:
        if batch_size <= 0:
            raise ValueError("batch_size must be positive")
        if self.chi_factor_columns.shape != self.nu_by_chi.shape:
            raise ValueError("chi_factor_columns and nu_by_chi must be aligned")
        if self.nu_by_chi.size and not np.all(self.nu_by_chi > 0.0):
            raise ValueError("nu_by_chi must be strictly positive")

        n_factors = int(self.available_factor_ids.shape[0])
        chi_cols = self.chi_factor_columns
        chi_shape_halved = self.nu_by_chi * 0.5  # chi2(nu) = 2 * Gamma(nu/2)
        n_chi = int(chi_cols.shape[0])

        rng = np.random.default_rng(self.seed)

        # Main buffer: C-order so [:bs, :] is C-contiguous for any bs.
        buffer = np.empty((batch_size, n_factors), dtype=np.float64, order="C")
        # 1D scratch: contiguous, used as `out=` for the chi gamma draw.
        chi_scratch = np.empty(batch_size, dtype=np.float64)

        scenario_offset = 0
        total = self.total_scenarios

        while scenario_offset < total:
            bs = min(batch_size, total - scenario_offset)
            view = buffer[:bs, :]

            # Fill the whole batch with standard normals...
            rng.standard_normal(out=cast(np.ndarray, view))

            # ...then overwrite the chi columns with raw chi-square draws.
            # Draw into a 1D contiguous scratch, then write across (the
            # destination is a strided 1D view, but the write itself is
            # not the `out=` target).
            for c in range(n_chi):
                chi_view = chi_scratch[:bs]
                rng.standard_gamma(chi_shape_halved[c], out=cast(np.ndarray, chi_view))
                chi_view *= 2.0
                buffer[:bs, chi_cols[c]] = chi_view

            yield CreditFactorBatch(
                latent_factors=view,
                scenario_offset=scenario_offset,
                scenario_count_total=total,
            )
            scenario_offset += bs
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/factors/state.py
"""
Latent-factor container for copula credit models.

`CreditFactorMatrix` is the credit-layer analogue of `MarketDataMatrix`: a
single C-contiguous `(num_credit_factors, num_scenarios)` array holding every
simulated latent driver — systemic Z, idiosyncratic eps, chi-squared mixing W —
in one integer-indexed universe. Copula blocks index it by `*_uix`.

It is deliberately separate from `MarketDataMatrix`: latent copula draws and
market prices are different sample spaces. Separation also lets the credit
Monte Carlo run an independent scenario count `S_credit != S_market`.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class CreditFactorMatrix:
    """
    Vectorized latent-factor container for copula JTD models.

    Dimension 0 is the credit-factor UIX space; dimension -1 is the scenario
    index. Z, eps and W share one UIX space, so a block's systemic/idio/chi
    indices all address the same array.
    """

    # (num_credit_factors, num_scenarios) — all latent drivers, scenario-major.
    latent_factors: npt.NDArray[np.float64]

    @property
    def num_credit_factors(self) -> int:
        return self.latent_factors.shape[0]

    @property
    def num_scenarios(self) -> int:
        return self.latent_factors.shape[1]
```

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\credit\jtd\blocks\base.py">
```python
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass, fields
from typing import ClassVar

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

_UNSET_JTD_MODEL_ID: int = -999


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class _JtdBlock:
    """Model-agnostic base for compiled obligor-keyed JTD blocks."""

    # ===== Class contract =====

    JTD_MODEL_ID: ClassVar[int] = _UNSET_JTD_MODEL_ID
    _ABSTRACT: ClassVar[bool] = True  # opt-in per class (not inherited)

    # ===== Core data (assumed valid) =====

    obligor_uids: npt.NDArray[np.int64]
    lgd: npt.NDArray[np.float64]

    # ===== Class construction guard =====

    def __init_subclass__(cls, **kwargs: object) -> None:
        super().__init_subclass__(**kwargs)

        # Only consider attributes defined *on this class*
        is_abstract = cls.__dict__.get("_ABSTRACT", False) or cls.__name__.startswith(
            "_"
        )

        if is_abstract:
            return

        if cls.JTD_MODEL_ID == _UNSET_JTD_MODEL_ID:
            raise TypeError(f"JTD block '{cls.__name__}' must set JTD_MODEL_ID.")

    # ===== Lightweight API =====

    @property
    def num_obligors(self) -> int:
        return self.obligor_uids.shape[0]

    def print_block(self, max_items: int | None = 10) -> None:
        print(f"{type(self).__name__}:")
        for f in fields(self):
            v = getattr(self, f.name)
            if isinstance(v, np.ndarray):
                print(
                    f"  {f.name:<36} | shape: {v.shape!s:>10} | "
                    f"dtype: {v.dtype!s:>8} | values: {v[:max_items]}"
                )
            else:
                print(f"  {f.name:<36} {v}")
        print()
```

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\credit\jtd\blocks\copula.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/credit/jtd/blocks/copula.py
"""
Copula JTD blocks — single-factor (Gaussian, Student-t) and multi-factor.

Single-factor blocks carry a scalar `beta` per obligor and one systemic / one
idiosyncratic `_uix`. The multi-factor block stores its loadings in CSR layout
(`factor_offsets`, flat `betas`, flat `systemic_uix`) — NEVER a padded
`(N, F_max)` array — so a 3-factor obligor next to a 30-factor obligor wastes
no cache lines and the kernel walks `factor_offsets[i]:factor_offsets[i+1]`.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import ClassVar

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.credit.jtd.blocks.base import _JtdBlock
from riskquant.credit.jtd.models import (
    COPULA_SF_GAUSSIAN_ID,
    COPULA_SF_T_ID,
)


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class _SingleFactorCopulaBlock(_JtdBlock):
    """Single-factor copula family base: one systemic factor, scalar loading."""

    _ABSTRACT: ClassVar[bool] = True

    # Threshold per obligor: A_i < t_i implies default. float64
    thresholds: npt.NDArray[np.float64]

    beta: npt.NDArray[np.float64]  # (N,) factor loading
    systemic_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32]  # (N,) index into CreditFactorMatrix
    idiosyncratic_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32]  # (N,)


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class GaussianCopulaJtdBlock(_SingleFactorCopulaBlock):
    """A_i = beta_i * Z + sqrt(1 - beta_i^2) * eps_i ; default if A_i < t_i."""

    JTD_MODEL_ID: ClassVar[int] = COPULA_SF_GAUSSIAN_ID


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class TCopulaJtdBlock(_SingleFactorCopulaBlock):
    """
    A_i = sqrt(nu_i / W) * (beta_i * Z + sqrt(1 - beta_i^2) * eps_i).

    `nu` is a real per-obligor SoA field, not a hidden default argument. The
    chi-square mixing variable W is read from the latent matrix via
    `chi_squared_uix`; the kernel — not the generator — owns the t-transform.
    """

    JTD_MODEL_ID: ClassVar[int] = COPULA_SF_T_ID

    chi_squared_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32]  # (N,) index into mixing variable W
    nu: npt.NDArray[np.float64]  # (N,) degrees of freedom
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/jtd/blocks/deterministic.py
"""
Deterministic JTD block.

The deterministic model is scenario-independent: it ignores the
`CreditFactorMatrix` entirely and assumes default with certainty, so the block
carries no copula or factor state — only the `_JtdBlock` base terms.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import ClassVar

from riskquant.credit.jtd.blocks.base import _JtdBlock
from riskquant.credit.jtd.models import DETERMINISTIC_ID


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class DeterministicJtdBlock(_JtdBlock):
    """Worst-case JTD: loss_i = ead_i * lgd_i (default indicator forced to 1)."""

    JTD_MODEL_ID: ClassVar[int] = DETERMINISTIC_ID
```

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\credit\jtd\bridge.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/credit/jtd/bridge.py
"""
Exposure -> Credit bridge.

Aggregates per-netting-set EAD (the output of `run_exposure`) up to per-obligor
EAD, then reindexes the result onto a target obligor-uid order. Pure SoA joins
plus one CSR segment-sum kernel — no row loops in Python, no jagged arrays.

The two joins differ in semantics:

  1. netting-set uid -> obligor uid is a FAIL-FAST lookup: an EadResult row
     that points at an unknown netting set is a bug in the upstream pipeline.

  2. obligor_per_ns -> target obligor index is a SOFT lookup: a netting set
     whose obligor has no row in `obligor_uids_target` is dropped (e.g. when
     the JTD portfolio only models a subset of the obligor universe).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import numba as nb  # type: ignore
import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.credit._uid_join import (
    searchsorted_or_raise,
    searchsorted_or_sentinel,
)
from riskquant.credit.exposure.results import EadResult
from riskquant.credit.netting_set.compiled import CompiledNettingSetMaster


@nb.njit(
    parallel=True,
    fastmath={"contract", "reassoc", "nsz"},
    nogil=True,
    boundscheck=False,
    error_model="numpy",
    cache=True,
)
def _kernel_aggregate_ead(
    out: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (n_target, S)
    ead: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (n_ns,    S)
    ead_perm: npt.NDArray[np.int32],  # (n_ns,)   ead row -> target band order
    target_offsets: npt.NDArray[np.int64],  # (n_target + 1,)
) -> None:
    n_target, s_scenarios = out.shape
    for g in nb.prange(n_target):
        start = target_offsets[g]
        end = target_offsets[g + 1]
        out_row = out[g]
        if end == start:
            for s in range(s_scenarios):
                out_row[s] = 0.0
            continue
        src0 = ead[ead_perm[start]]
        for s in range(s_scenarios):
            out_row[s] = src0[s]
        for i in range(start + 1, end):
            src = ead[ead_perm[i]]
            for s in range(s_scenarios):
                out_row[s] += src[s]


def aggregate_ead_to_obligor(
    *,
    ead_result: EadResult,
    netting_set_master: CompiledNettingSetMaster,
    obligor_uids_target: npt.NDArray[np.int64],
) -> npt.NDArray[np.float64]:
    """
    Aggregate per-netting-set EAD up to per-obligor EAD in target-row order.

    Parameters
    ----------
    ead_result
        Output of `run_exposure`; rows aligned to its `uid` (netting-set
        uid) array.
    netting_set_master
        Provides the netting-set uid -> obligor uid linkage. `uid`
        ascending sorted.
    obligor_uids_target
        Target obligor-uid order (typically `jtd_portfolio.obligor_uids`).
        Rows in the output align to this; obligors absent here are
        dropped from the aggregation.
    """
    # Strict: ns uid -> obligor uid.
    ns_pos = searchsorted_or_raise(
        target=ead_result.uid,
        superset_sorted=netting_set_master.uid,
        ctx="aggregate_ead_to_obligor: netting-set uid(s)",
    )
    obligor_per_ns = netting_set_master.obligor_uid[ns_pos]

    # Soft: target obligor index per ns; absences -> NO_UID_MATCH_IDX_I32 (-1).
    target_idx = searchsorted_or_sentinel(
        target=obligor_per_ns,
        superset_sorted=obligor_uids_target,
    )

    # Drop ns rows whose obligor isn't in the target universe.
    keep = target_idx >= 0
    target_idx = target_idx[keep]
    kept_ead_rows = np.flatnonzero(keep).astype(np.int32, copy=False)

    # CSR build: sort kept ns rows by target obligor, emit offsets.
    order = np.argsort(target_idx, kind="stable")
    ead_perm = np.ascontiguousarray(kept_ead_rows[order], dtype=np.int32)
    target_offsets = np.zeros(obligor_uids_target.shape[0] + 1, dtype=np.int64)
    counts = np.bincount(target_idx, minlength=obligor_uids_target.shape[0])
    np.cumsum(counts.astype(np.int64, copy=False), out=target_offsets[1:])

    s = ead_result.ead.shape[1]
    out = np.empty((obligor_uids_target.shape[0], s), dtype=np.float64, order="C")
    _kernel_aggregate_ead(
        out=out,
        ead=ead_result.ead,
        ead_perm=ead_perm,
        target_offsets=target_offsets,
    )
    return out
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\credit\jtd\compiled.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/credit/jtd/compiled.py
"""
Compiled JTD portfolio container.

`CompiledJtdPortfolio` is the credit-layer analogue of `CompiledExposure`: an
ordered set of per-model obligor blocks. Each populated block contributes one
contiguous row-band to the eventual (n_obligors x n_scenarios) JTD-loss matrix;
`block_offsets[i]` is its first row. `obligor_uids` aggregates every block's
uids into one flat int64 array in band order — the row order the engine's
`ead_by_obligor` argument must match.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Iterator
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import TypeAlias

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.credit.jtd.blocks.copula import (
    GaussianCopulaJtdBlock,
    TCopulaJtdBlock,
)
from riskquant.credit.jtd.blocks.deterministic import DeterministicJtdBlock

JtdBlockT: TypeAlias = GaussianCopulaJtdBlock | TCopulaJtdBlock | DeterministicJtdBlock


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class CompiledJtdPortfolio:
    """JTD-ready credit portfolio: an ordered set of compiled obligor blocks."""

    # Compiled blocks
    gaussian_copula_jtd: GaussianCopulaJtdBlock | None = None
    t_copula_jtd: TCopulaJtdBlock | None = None
    deterministic_jtd: DeterministicJtdBlock | None = None

    # Internal fields derived from the above and used by the engine.
    _blocks: tuple[JtdBlockT, ...] = field(init=False, repr=False)
    _block_offsets: npt.NDArray[np.int64] = field(init=False, repr=False)
    _total_blocks: int = field(init=False, repr=False)
    _total_obligors: int = field(init=False, repr=False)
    _obligor_uids: npt.NDArray[np.int64] = field(init=False, repr=False)

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        ordered: tuple[JtdBlockT | None, ...] = (
            self.gaussian_copula_jtd,
            self.t_copula_jtd,
            self.deterministic_jtd,
        )
        blocks: tuple[JtdBlockT, ...] = tuple(b for b in ordered if b is not None)
        total_blocks = len(blocks)

        block_offsets = np.zeros(total_blocks, dtype=np.int64)
        counts = np.fromiter(
            (b.num_obligors for b in blocks), dtype=np.int64, count=total_blocks
        )
        total_obligors = int(counts.sum())
        if total_blocks > 1:
            np.cumsum(counts[:-1], out=block_offsets[1:])

        obligor_uids = np.empty(total_obligors, dtype=np.int64)
        offset = 0
        for b in blocks:
            n = b.num_obligors
            obligor_uids[offset : offset + n] = b.obligor_uids
            offset += n

        object.__setattr__(self, "_blocks", blocks)
        object.__setattr__(self, "_block_offsets", block_offsets)
        object.__setattr__(self, "_total_blocks", total_blocks)
        object.__setattr__(self, "_total_obligors", total_obligors)
        object.__setattr__(self, "_obligor_uids", obligor_uids)

    @property
    def blocks(self) -> tuple[JtdBlockT, ...]:
        return self._blocks

    @property
    def block_offsets(self) -> npt.NDArray[np.int64]:
        return self._block_offsets

    @property
    def total_blocks(self) -> int:
        return self._total_blocks

    @property
    def total_obligors(self) -> int:
        return self._total_obligors

    @property
    def obligor_uids(self) -> npt.NDArray[np.int64]:
        return self._obligor_uids

    def iter_blocks(self) -> Iterator[JtdBlockT]:
        yield from self._blocks

    def summary(self) -> None:
        """Print a console-friendly overview of block layout and obligor counts."""
        print("=" * 90)
        print(f"{'COMPILED PORTFOLIO SUMMARY':^90}")
        print("=" * 90)
        print(f"Total Obligors: {self._total_obligors:,}")
        print(f"Total Blocks: {self._total_blocks}")
        print("-" * 90)
        print(f"{'Block Type':<32} | {'Obligors':<10} | {'Offset'}")
        print("-" * 90)

        if self._total_blocks == 0:
            print(f"{'Empty Portfolio':^90}")
        else:
            for i, block in enumerate(self._blocks):
                print(
                    f"{type(block).__name__:<32} | "
                    f"{block.num_obligors:<10,} | {self._block_offsets[i]}"
                )
        print("=" * 90 + "\n")

    def print_block_details(self, max_items: int | None = 10) -> None:
        """Cascade into each block and print its raw SoA arrays."""
        print("=" * 90)
        print(f"{f'BLOCK DETAILS (MAX {max_items} ITEMS PER BLOCK)':^90}")
        print("=" * 90 + "\n")
        for block in self._blocks:
            title = f"Block: {type(block).__name__}"
            print("-" * len(title))
            print(title)
            print("-" * len(title))
            block.print_block(max_items=max_items)
        print("=" * 90 + "\n")
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\credit\jtd\engine.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/credit/jtd/engine.py
"""
JTD engine: model-id -> evaluator dispatch and the loss-resolution loop.

`JTD_EVALUATORS` is the credit-layer analogue of `EAD_EVALUATORS`: a frozen
tuple indexed by `JtdModel` id. `run_jtd` walks the compiled obligor blocks in
band order, dispatches each on its `JTD_MODEL_ID`, and writes into contiguous
bands of one pre-allocated (n_obligors x n_scenarios) output. The import-time
guard rejects any model that ships without an evaluator.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Any, Final, cast

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.credit.factors.state import CreditFactorMatrix
from riskquant.credit.jtd.compiled import CompiledJtdPortfolio
from riskquant.credit.jtd.evaluators.base import JtdEvaluator
from riskquant.credit.jtd.evaluators.copula import (
    evaluate_gaussian_copula_jtd,
    evaluate_t_copula_jtd,
)
from riskquant.credit.jtd.evaluators.deterministic import (
    evaluate_deterministic_jtd,
)
from riskquant.credit.jtd.models import (
    COPULA_SF_GAUSSIAN_ID,
    COPULA_SF_T_ID,
    DETERMINISTIC_ID,
    NUM_JTD_MODELS,
    JtdModel,
)
from riskquant.credit.jtd.results import JtdLossResult

_JTD_EVALUATORS_MUT: list[JtdEvaluator[Any] | None] = [None] * NUM_JTD_MODELS


def _register(model_id: int, evaluator: JtdEvaluator[Any]) -> None:
    if not isinstance(evaluator, JtdEvaluator):
        raise TypeError(
            f"Evaluator for JTD model_id={model_id} does not satisfy the "
            f"JtdEvaluator protocol: {evaluator!r}"
        )
    _JTD_EVALUATORS_MUT[model_id] = evaluator


_register(COPULA_SF_GAUSSIAN_ID, evaluate_gaussian_copula_jtd)
_register(COPULA_SF_T_ID, evaluate_t_copula_jtd)
_register(DETERMINISTIC_ID, evaluate_deterministic_jtd)

JTD_EVALUATORS: Final[tuple[JtdEvaluator[Any], ...]] = cast(
    tuple[JtdEvaluator[Any], ...], tuple(_JTD_EVALUATORS_MUT)
)

_unregistered = [JtdModel(i).name for i, ev in enumerate(JTD_EVALUATORS) if ev is None]
if _unregistered:
    raise RuntimeError(
        f"No evaluator registered for JTD models: {_unregistered}. "
        f"Register them in credit/jtd/engine.py."
    )


def run_jtd(
    *,
    jtd_portfolio: CompiledJtdPortfolio,
    ead_by_obligor: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    credit_factors: CreditFactorMatrix,
) -> JtdLossResult:
    """
    Resolve jump-to-default loss across all JTD models.

    Parameters
    ----------
    jtd_portfolio
        Compiled obligor blocks; `obligor_uids` defines the output row order.
    ead_by_obligor
        (n_obligors, S) EAD, rows aligned to `jtd_portfolio.obligor_uids` —
        the output of `aggregate_ead_to_obligor`.
    credit_factors
        Latent-factor matrix; `num_scenarios` must match `ead_by_obligor`.
        Ignored by the deterministic model, which is scenario-independent.
    """
    n_obligors = jtd_portfolio.total_obligors
    n_scenarios = credit_factors.num_scenarios

    if ead_by_obligor.shape != (n_obligors, n_scenarios):
        raise ValueError(
            f"ead_by_obligor shape {ead_by_obligor.shape} != expected "
            f"{(n_obligors, n_scenarios)}."
        )

    loss: npt.NDArray[np.float64] = np.empty(
        (n_obligors, n_scenarios), dtype=np.float64, order="C"
    )

    blocks = jtd_portfolio.blocks
    offsets = jtd_portfolio.block_offsets
    evaluators_table = JTD_EVALUATORS

    for i in range(jtd_portfolio.total_blocks):
        block = blocks[i]
        start = int(offsets[i])
        end = start + block.num_obligors

        evaluator = evaluators_table[block.JTD_MODEL_ID]
        evaluator(
            block=block,
            ead=ead_by_obligor[start:end, :],
            credit_factors=credit_factors,
            out=loss[start:end, :],
        )

    return JtdLossResult(uid=jtd_portfolio.obligor_uids, loss=loss)
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/jtd/evaluators/base.py
"""
JTD evaluator dispatch contract.

`JtdEvaluator` is deliberately NOT the valuation `Evaluator` protocol. A pricer
maps (block, market data) -> PV; a JTD evaluator maps (block, EAD, latent
factors) -> loss. Every evaluator — copula or deterministic — honors the same
`(n_obligors, S)` `out` contract so the engine table stays homogeneous.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Protocol, TypeVar, runtime_checkable

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

if TYPE_CHECKING:
    from riskquant.credit.factors.state import CreditFactorMatrix
    from riskquant.credit.jtd.blocks.base import _JtdBlock

B_contra = TypeVar("B_contra", bound="_JtdBlock", contravariant=True)


@runtime_checkable
class JtdEvaluator(Protocol[B_contra]):
    """Structural contract for all JTD-model evaluators."""

    def __call__(
        self,
        *,
        block: B_contra,
        ead: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
        credit_factors: CreditFactorMatrix,
        out: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    ) -> None: ...
```

```python
from __future__ import annotations

from typing import TYPE_CHECKING

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.credit.jtd.kernels.copula import (
    kernel_sf_gaussian_copula_jtd,
    kernel_sf_t_copula_jtd,
)

if TYPE_CHECKING:
    from riskquant.credit.factors.state import CreditFactorMatrix
    from riskquant.credit.jtd.blocks.copula import (
        GaussianCopulaJtdBlock,
        TCopulaJtdBlock,
    )


def evaluate_gaussian_copula_jtd(
    *,
    block: GaussianCopulaJtdBlock,
    ead: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    credit_factors: CreditFactorMatrix,
    out: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
) -> None:
    kernel_sf_gaussian_copula_jtd(
        out=out,
        latent_factors=credit_factors.latent_factors,
        ead=ead,
        lgd=block.lgd,
        beta=block.beta,
        thresholds=block.thresholds,
        sys_uix=block.systemic_uix,
        idio_uix=block.idiosyncratic_uix,
    )


def evaluate_t_copula_jtd(
    *,
    block: TCopulaJtdBlock,
    ead: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    credit_factors: CreditFactorMatrix,
    out: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
) -> None:
    kernel_sf_t_copula_jtd(
        out=out,
        latent_factors=credit_factors.latent_factors,
        ead=ead,
        lgd=block.lgd,
        beta=block.beta,
        thresholds=block.thresholds,
        nu=block.nu,
        sys_uix=block.systemic_uix,
        idio_uix=block.idiosyncratic_uix,
        chi_uix=block.chi_squared_uix,
    )
```

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\credit\jtd\ingest.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/credit/jtd/ingest.py
"""
Credit-positions DataFrame + obligor master -> CompiledJtdPortfolio.

The credit edge: a human-facing per-obligor JTD frame is grouped by
`JtdModel` and collapsed into obligor-keyed SoA blocks. PD and LGD live
on the obligor master (`CompiledObligorCredit`) and are joined onto the
JTD rows by uid here — they are NEVER carried inline on the JTD frame.
This is what makes the obligor master the single source of truth for
obligor-level credit attributes.

Strings (factor names) die here, mapped to int32 indices into the
`CreditFactorMatrix` UIX space via vectorized ``pd.Series.map``. EAD is
intentionally absent — it is realized per scenario by the exposure
engine and bridged in at `run_jtd` time.

Single-factor models compile from one row per obligor. Multi-factor
models would carry multiple rows per obligor (one per loading) once
implemented; the compiler currently expects exactly one row per obligor.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt
import pandas as pd
from scipy.special import ndtri

from riskquant.constants import PD_DUST_TOLERANCE
from riskquant.credit._uid_join import searchsorted_or_raise
from riskquant.credit.jtd.blocks.copula import (
    GaussianCopulaJtdBlock,
    TCopulaJtdBlock,
)
from riskquant.credit.jtd.blocks.deterministic import DeterministicJtdBlock
from riskquant.credit.jtd.compiled import CompiledJtdPortfolio
from riskquant.credit.jtd.models import JtdModel
from riskquant.credit.obligors.compiled import CompiledObligorCredit

_PREVIEW = 10


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------
# Vectorized primitives
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------


def _resolve_factor_uix(
    names: npt.NDArray[np.object_],
    factor_name_to_uix: dict[str, int],
    *,
    ctx: str,
) -> npt.NDArray[np.int32]:

    out = np.empty(names.shape, dtype=np.int32)
    mapping_get = factor_name_to_uix.get

    missing = set()
    sentinel = -999  # must not collide with valid UIX

    for i, name in enumerate(names):
        val = mapping_get(name, sentinel)
        if val == sentinel:
            missing.add(name)
        else:
            out[i] = val

    if missing:
        raise ValueError(f"Unknown credit factor(s) for '{ctx}': {sorted(missing)}")

    return out


def _gaussian_thresholds(pd_arr: npt.NDArray[np.float64]) -> npt.NDArray[np.float64]:
    """t_i = Phi^{-1}(PD_i) with dust-clipping to keep ndtri finite."""
    out = np.empty_like(pd_arr)
    np.clip(pd_arr, PD_DUST_TOLERANCE, 1.0 - PD_DUST_TOLERANCE, out=out)
    return ndtri(out, out=out)


def _i64(df: pd.DataFrame, col: str) -> npt.NDArray[np.int64]:
    return np.ascontiguousarray(df[col].to_numpy(dtype=np.int64))


def _f64(df: pd.DataFrame, col: str) -> npt.NDArray[np.float64]:
    return np.ascontiguousarray(df[col].to_numpy(dtype=np.float64))


def _join_pd_lgd_from_master(
    obligor_uids: npt.NDArray[np.int64],
    obligor_master: CompiledObligorCredit,
) -> tuple[npt.NDArray[np.float64], npt.NDArray[np.float64]]:
    """
    Look up PD and LGD on the obligor master in one ``searchsorted``.

    Delegates the actual lookup to the centralized
    ``_uid_join.searchsorted_or_raise`` — every credit-package join goes
    through the same primitive, never inline searchsorted/clip/match.
    """
    pos = searchsorted_or_raise(
        target=obligor_uids,
        superset_sorted=obligor_master.uid,
        ctx="compile_jtd_portfolio: JTD obligor uid(s)",
    )
    pd_arr = np.ascontiguousarray(obligor_master.pd[pos], dtype=np.float64)
    lgd_arr = np.ascontiguousarray(obligor_master.lgd[pos], dtype=np.float64)
    return pd_arr, lgd_arr


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------
# Per-model field validators
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------


def _validate_beta_range(
    obligor_uids: npt.NDArray[np.int64], beta: npt.NDArray[np.float64]
) -> None:
    bad = ~((beta >= -1.0) & (beta <= 1.0))
    if bad.any():
        raise ValueError(
            f"compile_jtd_portfolio: beta outside [-1, 1] for obligor(s) "
            f"{obligor_uids[bad][:_PREVIEW].tolist()}."
        )


def _validate_nu_positive(
    obligor_uids: npt.NDArray[np.int64], nu: npt.NDArray[np.float64]
) -> None:
    bad = ~(nu > 0.0)  # also catches NaN
    if bad.any():
        raise ValueError(
            f"compile_jtd_portfolio: nu must be > 0 (finite) for obligor(s) "
            f"{obligor_uids[bad][:_PREVIEW].tolist()}."
        )


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------
# Per-model block builders
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------


def _build_gaussian_block(
    df: pd.DataFrame,
    obligor_master: CompiledObligorCredit,
    factor_name_to_uix: dict[str, int],
) -> GaussianCopulaJtdBlock:
    obligor_uids = _i64(df, "uid")
    pd_arr, lgd_arr = _join_pd_lgd_from_master(obligor_uids, obligor_master)
    beta = _f64(df, "beta")
    _validate_beta_range(obligor_uids, beta)

    return GaussianCopulaJtdBlock(
        obligor_uids=obligor_uids,
        lgd=lgd_arr,
        thresholds=_gaussian_thresholds(pd_arr),
        beta=beta,
        systemic_uix=_resolve_factor_uix(
            df["systemic_factor"].to_numpy(copy=False),
            factor_name_to_uix,
            ctx="systemic_factor",
        ),
        idiosyncratic_uix=_resolve_factor_uix(
            df["idiosyncratic_factor"].to_numpy(copy=False),
            factor_name_to_uix,
            ctx="idiosyncratic_factor",
        ),
    )


def _build_t_block(
    df: pd.DataFrame,
    obligor_master: CompiledObligorCredit,
    factor_name_to_uix: dict[str, int],
) -> TCopulaJtdBlock:
    if "nu" not in df.columns:
        raise ValueError("COPULA_SF_T: missing required column 'nu'.")
    if df["nu"].isna().any():
        bad = df.loc[df["nu"].isna(), "uid"].tolist()[:_PREVIEW]
        raise ValueError(f"COPULA_SF_T: nu missing for obligor(s) {bad}")

    obligor_uids = _i64(df, "uid")
    pd_arr, lgd_arr = _join_pd_lgd_from_master(obligor_uids, obligor_master)

    beta = _f64(df, "beta")
    _validate_beta_range(obligor_uids, beta)

    nu = _f64(df, "nu")
    _validate_nu_positive(obligor_uids, nu)

    return TCopulaJtdBlock(
        obligor_uids=obligor_uids,
        lgd=lgd_arr,
        thresholds=_gaussian_thresholds(pd_arr),
        beta=beta,
        systemic_uix=_resolve_factor_uix(
            df["systemic_factor"].to_numpy(copy=False),
            factor_name_to_uix,
            ctx="systemic_factor",
        ),
        idiosyncratic_uix=_resolve_factor_uix(
            df["idiosyncratic_factor"].to_numpy(copy=False),
            factor_name_to_uix,
            ctx="idiosyncratic_factor",
        ),
        chi_squared_uix=_resolve_factor_uix(
            df["chi_squared_factor"].to_numpy(copy=False),
            factor_name_to_uix,
            ctx="chi_squared_factor",
        ),
        nu=nu,
    )


def _build_deterministic_block(
    df: pd.DataFrame,
    obligor_master: CompiledObligorCredit,
) -> DeterministicJtdBlock:
    """
    Deterministic JTD: loss = ead * lgd, default forced to 1. The kernel
    never reads PD, so the master join is for LGD only.
    """
    obligor_uids = _i64(df, "uid")
    _, lgd_arr = _join_pd_lgd_from_master(obligor_uids, obligor_master)
    return DeterministicJtdBlock(obligor_uids=obligor_uids, lgd=lgd_arr)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------
# Public API
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------


def compile_jtd_portfolio_from_df(
    *,
    credit_df: pd.DataFrame,
    obligor_master: CompiledObligorCredit,
    factor_name_to_uix: dict[str, int],
) -> CompiledJtdPortfolio:
    """
    Compile a validated credit-positions frame into a ``CompiledJtdPortfolio``.

    Parameters
    ----------
    credit_df
        One row per obligor. Carries ``uid``, ``instrument`` (a ``JtdModel``
        member name), plus model-specific fields. Single-factor copula
        rows carry ``beta``, ``systemic_factor``, ``idiosyncratic_factor``;
        t-copula adds ``chi_squared_factor`` and ``nu``. **PD and LGD are
        NOT on this frame** — they are joined from ``obligor_master``.
    obligor_master
        Compiled obligor master; the single source of truth for PD and
        LGD. ``uid`` ascending sorted. The JTD ``uid`` column must be a
        subset of the master.
    factor_name_to_uix
        Credit-factor name -> int32 UIX into the ``CreditFactorMatrix``
        universe.
    """
    if credit_df.empty:
        raise ValueError("compile_jtd_portfolio: cannot compile an empty frame.")

    if "instrument" not in credit_df.columns:
        raise ValueError("compile_jtd_portfolio: missing required column 'instrument'.")

    if "uid" not in credit_df.columns:
        raise ValueError("compile_jtd_portfolio: missing required column 'uid'.")

    if credit_df["uid"].duplicated().any():
        dups = credit_df.loc[credit_df["uid"].duplicated(), "uid"].tolist()[:_PREVIEW]
        raise ValueError(f"compile_jtd_portfolio: duplicate obligor uid(s): {dups}.")

    # Fail-fast: an unknown instrument name is a typo, NOT a silent drop.
    known_models = {m.name for m in JtdModel}
    present = set(credit_df["instrument"].astype(str).unique().tolist())
    unknown = present - known_models
    if unknown:
        raise ValueError(
            f"compile_jtd_portfolio: unknown JTD model(s) in 'instrument' column: "
            f"{sorted(unknown)}. Known: {sorted(known_models)}."
        )

    # Typed locals preserve each block's static type into the constructor;
    # a `dict[str, object]` + `**fields` unpack collapses everything to
    # `object` and breaks `CompiledJtdPortfolio.__init__`'s overload resolution.
    gaussian_block: GaussianCopulaJtdBlock | None = None
    t_block: TCopulaJtdBlock | None = None
    det_block: DeterministicJtdBlock | None = None

    for model in JtdModel:
        group = credit_df[credit_df["instrument"] == model.name]
        if group.empty:
            continue
        group = group.reset_index(drop=True)

        if model is JtdModel.COPULA_SF_GAUSSIAN:
            gaussian_block = _build_gaussian_block(
                group,
                obligor_master=obligor_master,
                factor_name_to_uix=factor_name_to_uix,
            )
        elif model is JtdModel.COPULA_SF_T:
            t_block = _build_t_block(
                group,
                obligor_master=obligor_master,
                factor_name_to_uix=factor_name_to_uix,
            )
        elif model is JtdModel.DETERMINISTIC:
            det_block = _build_deterministic_block(
                group,
                obligor_master=obligor_master,
            )
        else:
            # Defensive: the unknown-instrument guard above already filters
            # this, but a new enum member added without a builder here is a
            # programmer error that should fail loudly, not silently drop.
            raise NotImplementedError(
                f"compile_jtd_portfolio: no builder wired for JtdModel.{model.name}."
            )

    if gaussian_block is None and t_block is None and det_block is None:
        raise ValueError(
            "compile_jtd_portfolio: no rows matched a known JtdModel "
            f"({[m.name for m in JtdModel]})."
        )

    return CompiledJtdPortfolio(
        gaussian_copula_jtd=gaussian_block,
        t_copula_jtd=t_block,
        deterministic_jtd=det_block,
    )
```
