<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\credit\jtd\kernels\copula.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/credit/jtd/kernels/copula.py
"""
Bare-metal copula JTD loss kernels — single-factor and multi-factor.

Zero-allocation, parallel, branchless. Loss is realized per scenario as
`ead * lgd * default_indicator`, so a scenario-dependent EAD flows straight
through with no static precompute. Latent factors are read `(F, S)` with the
scenario axis contiguous.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import numba as nb  # type: ignore
import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

_NB = dict(
    parallel=True,
    fastmath={"contract", "reassoc", "nsz"},
    nogil=True,
    boundscheck=False,
    error_model="numpy",
    cache=True,
)


@nb.njit(**_NB)  # type: ignore[misc]
def kernel_sf_gaussian_copula_jtd(
    out: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (M, S)
    latent_factors: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (F, S)
    ead: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (M, S)
    lgd: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (M,)
    beta: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (M,)
    thresholds: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (M,)
    sys_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32],  # (M,)
    idio_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32],  # (M,)
) -> None:
    m_, s_scenarios = out.shape

    for i in nb.prange(m_):
        t_i = thresholds[i]
        beta_i = beta[i]
        sqrt_1mb = np.sqrt(1.0 - beta_i * beta_i)
        lgd_i = lgd[i]

        z_row = latent_factors[sys_uix[i]]
        eps_row = latent_factors[idio_uix[i]]
        ead_row = ead[i]
        out_row = out[i]

        for s in range(s_scenarios):
            a_ = beta_i * z_row[s] + sqrt_1mb * eps_row[s]
            out_row[s] = ead_row[s] * lgd_i * (t_i > a_)


@nb.njit(**_NB)  # type: ignore[misc]
def kernel_sf_t_copula_jtd(
    out: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (M, S)
    latent_factors: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (F, S)
    ead: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (M, S)
    lgd: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (M,)
    beta: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (M,)
    thresholds: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (M,)
    nu: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (M,)
    sys_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32],  # (M,)
    idio_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32],  # (M,)
    chi_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32],  # (M,)
) -> None:
    m_, s_scenarios = out.shape

    for i in nb.prange(m_):
        t_i = thresholds[i]
        beta_i = beta[i]
        sqrt_1mb = np.sqrt(1.0 - beta_i * beta_i)
        lgd_i = lgd[i]
        nu_i = nu[i]

        z_row = latent_factors[sys_uix[i]]
        eps_row = latent_factors[idio_uix[i]]
        w_row = latent_factors[chi_uix[i]]
        ead_row = ead[i]
        out_row = out[i]

        for s in range(s_scenarios):
            # W is a raw chi-square draw, strictly positive (cleaned at
            # factor-generation time); the t-transform is applied ONCE, here.
            m_factor = np.sqrt(nu_i / w_row[s])
            a_ = m_factor * (beta_i * z_row[s] + sqrt_1mb * eps_row[s])
            out_row[s] = ead_row[s] * lgd_i * (t_i > a_)
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\credit\jtd\kernels\deterministic.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/credit/jtd/kernels/deterministic.py
"""
Deterministic JTD loss kernel.

Scenario-independent: default is certain, so loss is `ead * lgd` with the
per-obligor `lgd` scalar broadcast across the scenario axis. The latent-factor
matrix is never read. Worst-case stress is simply `S = 1`.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import numba as nb  # type: ignore
import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt


@nb.njit(
    parallel=True,
    fastmath={"contract", "reassoc", "nsz"},
    nogil=True,
    boundscheck=False,
    error_model="numpy",
    cache=True,
)  # type: ignore[misc]
def kernel_deterministic_jtd(
    out: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (M, S)
    ead: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (M, S)
    lgd: npt.NDArray[np.float64],  # (M,)
) -> None:
    m_, s_scenarios = out.shape

    for i in nb.prange(m_):
        lgd_i = lgd[i]
        ead_row = ead[i]
        out_row = out[i]
        for s in range(s_scenarios):
            out_row[s] = ead_row[s] * lgd_i
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\credit\jtd\models.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/credit/jtd/models.py
"""
JTD model dispatch identifiers.

`JtdModel` is the credit-layer analogue of `ExposureModel`: a compact integer
identifier used to dispatch into `JTD_EVALUATORS`.

Design principles
-----------------
- Values are contiguous in [0, N-1] → safe for direct indexing.
- Enum contains ONLY implemented models.
- Ordering reflects increasing model complexity:
    deterministic → single-factor → multi-factor
  (NOT production priority).
- The enum is the single source of truth for model IDs.

Any addition MUST:
    1. Append at the end (preserve ordering + contiguity)
    2. Provide a corresponding evaluator
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from enum import IntEnum
from typing import Final


class JtdModel(IntEnum):
    # ---------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Deterministic model
    # ---------------------------------------------------------------------
    DETERMINISTIC = 0  # scenario-independent worst-case JTD

    # ---------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Single-factor copula models
    # ---------------------------------------------------------------------
    COPULA_SF_GAUSSIAN = 1  # single-factor Gaussian copula
    COPULA_SF_T = 2  # single-factor Student-t copula

    # ---------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Multi-factor copula models
    # ---------------------------------------------------------------------
    # TODO


# -------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Derived constants (single source of truth = enum)
# -------------------------------------------------------------------------

NUM_JTD_MODELS: Final[int] = len(JtdModel)


# -------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Fast-path integer aliases (optional, performance-oriented)
# -------------------------------------------------------------------------
# These exist to avoid enum dereferencing in tight loops / NumPy code.
# Safe because they derive directly from the enum.

# Deterministic model
DETERMINISTIC_ID: Final[int] = int(JtdModel.DETERMINISTIC)

# Single-factor copula models
COPULA_SF_GAUSSIAN_ID: Final[int] = int(JtdModel.COPULA_SF_GAUSSIAN)
COPULA_SF_T_ID: Final[int] = int(JtdModel.COPULA_SF_T)
```

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/netting_set/compiled.py
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class CompiledNettingSetMaster:
    """
    Compiled netting-set master data.

    This only contains information relevant to netting-set-level collateral, which is
    all that's needed for exposure calculation. The master is keyed by `uid`, which
    is the netting-set id ingested from the batch and joined onto the portfolio's
    `trade_uids` to align collateral to priced trades.
    """

    # --- NETTING SET UID ---
    # ingest column netting_set_id; sorted ascending, unique
    uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64]

    # --- OBLIGOR UID ---
    # Obligor uid of the netting set; aligned to `uid`
    obligor_uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64]

    # --- COLLATERAL PER NETTING SET ---
    # net collateral per netting set; aligned to `uid`
    net_collateral: npt.NDArray[np.float64]
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\credit\netting_set\ingest.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/credit/netting_set/ingest.py
"""
Netting-set master DataFrame -> `CompiledNettingSetMaster`.

The netting-set edge: a human-facing master frame is cast to strict dtypes,
sorted by netting-set uid ascending, validated, and collapsed into a single
immutable SoA container. The ascending uid invariant is what
`compile_exposure._resolve_collateral` relies on for its `searchsorted`
join from `netting_set_uids` -> `(obligor_uid, net_collateral)`.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt
import pandas as pd

from riskquant.credit.netting_set.compiled import CompiledNettingSetMaster
from riskquant.credit.netting_set.validate import (
    validate_netting_set_master_arrays,
)

_REQUIRED_COLS: tuple[str, ...] = ("uid", "obligor_uid", "net_collateral")


def _check_required_columns(df: pd.DataFrame) -> None:
    missing = [c for c in _REQUIRED_COLS if c not in df.columns]
    if missing:
        raise ValueError(
            f"compile_netting_set_master: missing required column(s) {missing}."
        )


def _i64(df: pd.DataFrame, col: str) -> npt.NDArray[np.int64]:
    arr = df[col].to_numpy(dtype=np.int64)
    return np.ascontiguousarray(arr)


def _f64(df: pd.DataFrame, col: str) -> npt.NDArray[np.float64]:
    arr = df[col].to_numpy(dtype=np.float64)
    if np.isnan(arr).any():
        raise ValueError(
            f"compile_netting_set_master: NaN in non-nullable column '{col}'."
        )
    return np.ascontiguousarray(arr)


def compile_netting_set_master_from_df(
    netting_set_df: pd.DataFrame,
) -> CompiledNettingSetMaster:
    """
    Compile a validated netting-set master frame into `CompiledNettingSetMaster`.

    Parameters
    ----------
    netting_set_df
        One row per effective netting set. Required columns:
        - `uid` (int64): netting-set identifier; matches the
          `netting_set_id` column on the portfolio ingest frame.
        - `obligor_uid` (int64): obligor that owns the netting set; the
          join key from the exposure result into the obligor space.
        - `net_collateral` (float64): haircut-adjusted collateral, >= 0.

    Returns
    -------
    CompiledNettingSetMaster
        `uid` sorted ascending; other columns reordered by the same
        permutation to stay row-aligned.
    """
    if netting_set_df.empty:
        raise ValueError("compile_netting_set_master: cannot compile an empty frame.")

    _check_required_columns(netting_set_df)

    df = netting_set_df.sort_values("uid", kind="stable").reset_index(drop=True)

    uid = _i64(df, "uid")
    obligor_uid = _i64(df, "obligor_uid")
    net_collateral = _f64(df, "net_collateral")

    validate_netting_set_master_arrays(
        uid=uid,
        obligor_uid=obligor_uid,
        net_collateral=net_collateral,
    )

    return CompiledNettingSetMaster(
        uid=uid,
        obligor_uid=obligor_uid,
        net_collateral=net_collateral,
    )
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/credit/netting_set/loader.py
"""
Netting-set master loader entry points.

`loader.py` is the external-source bridge — CSVs, the DWH, S3, whatever
concrete production format is wired in. Each loader reads the raw source
into a canonical `pd.DataFrame` with columns `uid`, `obligor_uid`,
`net_collateral`, then hands off to `ingest.compile_netting_set_master_from_df`
for dtype coercion, validation and SoA materialization.

This module is intentionally a thin shell: it owns I/O only, never
validation logic. Validation lives in `validate.py`; SoA materialization
lives in `ingest.py`. Adding a loader is one function that returns a
DataFrame and one call site that hands the frame to the ingest.
```

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\credit\netting_set\validate.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/credit/netting_set/validate.py
"""
Netting-set master validators.

Mirrors `obligors/validate.py`: vectorized predicates on post-ingest SoA,
no row loops, no pandas. Cross-master validation (obligor uids present on
the obligor master) is deliberately out of scope here — it's a layered
check that belongs at the top-level pipeline orchestrator, where both
masters are visible.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

_PREVIEW = 10


def _bad_uids(uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64], mask: npt.NDArray[np.bool_]) -> list[int]:
    return uid[mask][:_PREVIEW].tolist()


def validate_uid_sorted_unique(uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64]) -> None:
    """`uid` (netting-set id) must be strictly ascending."""
    if uid.shape[0] == 0:
        raise ValueError("netting set master: empty uid array.")
    if not (np.diff(uid) > 0).all():
        unique, counts = np.unique(uid, return_counts=True)
        dups = unique[counts > 1][:_PREVIEW].tolist()
        if dups:
            raise ValueError(
                f"netting set master: duplicate netting-set uid(s) {dups}."
            )
        raise ValueError("netting set master: uid is not sorted ascending.")


def validate_obligor_uid_nonnegative(
    uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64],
    obligor_uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64],
) -> None:
    """Every netting set must point at a non-negative obligor uid (no sentinel)."""
    bad = obligor_uid < 0
    if bad.any():
        raise ValueError(
            f"netting set master: negative obligor_uid for netting set(s) "
            f"{_bad_uids(uid, bad)}."
        )


def validate_net_collateral(
    uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64], net_collateral: npt.NDArray[np.float64]
) -> None:
    """Net collateral must be non-negative and finite."""
    bad = ~(np.isfinite(net_collateral) & (net_collateral >= 0.0))
    if bad.any():
        raise ValueError(
            f"netting set master: net_collateral negative / non-finite for "
            f"netting set(s) {_bad_uids(uid, bad)}."
        )


def validate_netting_set_master_arrays(
    *,
    uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64],
    obligor_uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64],
    net_collateral: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
) -> None:
    """Single-call entry point: every validator, fail-fast."""
    validate_uid_sorted_unique(uid)
    validate_obligor_uid_nonnegative(uid, obligor_uid)
    validate_net_collateral(uid, net_collateral)
```
</file>

```python
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class CompiledObligorCredit:
    """
    Universal obligor credit data.
    Consumed by ALL credit kernels (Deterministic JTD, Expected Loss, Copula).
    """

    uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64]  # obligor ID; sorted ascending, unique
    pd: npt.NDArray[np.float64]
    lgd: npt.NDArray[np.float64]
    rating_id: npt.NDArray[np.int8]    # int enum from config/enums/credit_ratings
    sector_id: npt.NDArray[np.int32]   # int enum, future
    country_id: npt.NDArray[np.int32]   # int enum, future
    parent_uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64]   # NO_PARENT_OBLIGOR sentinel for ultimates
```

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\credit\obligors\ingest.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/credit/obligors/ingest.py
"""
Obligor master DataFrame -> `CompiledObligorCredit`.

The obligor edge: a human-facing master frame is cast to strict dtypes,
sorted by uid ascending, validated as one self-consistent universe, and
collapsed into a single immutable SoA container.

`uid` ascending sorted is a structural invariant the downstream layers
rely on for vectorized joins (`searchsorted` in `compile_jtd_portfolio_from_df`,
`aggregate_ead_to_obligor`, and `_resolve_collateral`). The same ordering
permutation is applied to every column so the SoA stays aligned row-wise.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import cast

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt
import pandas as pd

from riskquant.constants import NO_PARENT_OBLIGOR
from riskquant.credit.obligors.compiled import CompiledObligorCredit
from riskquant.credit.obligors.validate import validate_obligor_master_arrays

_REQUIRED_COLS: tuple[str, ...] = (
    "uid",
    "pd",
    "lgd",
    "rating_id",
    "sector_id",
    "country_id",
    "parent_uid",
)


def _check_required_columns(df: pd.DataFrame) -> None:
    missing = [c for c in _REQUIRED_COLS if c not in df.columns]
    if missing:
        raise ValueError(
            f"compile_obligor_master: missing required column(s) {missing}."
        )


def _i64(df: pd.DataFrame, col: str) -> npt.NDArray[np.int64]:
    return np.ascontiguousarray(df[col].to_numpy(dtype=np.int64))


def _f64(df: pd.DataFrame, col: str) -> npt.NDArray[np.float64]:
    arr = df[col].to_numpy(dtype=np.float64)
    if np.isnan(arr).any():
        raise ValueError(f"compile_obligor_master: NaN in non-nullable column '{col}'.")
    return np.ascontiguousarray(arr)


def _i8(df: pd.DataFrame, col: str) -> npt.NDArray[np.int8]:
    return np.ascontiguousarray(df[col].to_numpy(dtype=np.int8))


def _i32(df: pd.DataFrame, col: str) -> npt.NDArray[np.int32]:
    return np.ascontiguousarray(df[col].to_numpy(dtype=np.int32))


def _parent_uid(df: pd.DataFrame) -> npt.NDArray[np.int64]:
    """NA / None -> NO_PARENT_OBLIGOR; numeric values pass through as int64."""
    filled = (
        df["parent_uid"]
        .astype("Int64")  # pandas nullable integer
        .fillna(NO_PARENT_OBLIGOR)
    )
    return cast(
        npt.NDArray[np.int64],
        np.ascontiguousarray(filled.to_numpy(dtype=np.int64)),
    )


def compile_obligor_master_from_df(
    obligor_df: pd.DataFrame,
) -> CompiledObligorCredit:
    """
    Compile a validated obligor-master frame into `CompiledObligorCredit`.

    Parameters
    ----------
    obligor_df
        One row per obligor. Required columns: uid, pd, lgd, rating_id,
        sector_id, country_id, parent_uid. `parent_uid` is nullable —
        NA/None marks an ultimate (no parent) and is mapped to
        `NO_PARENT_OBLIGOR`.

    Returns
    -------
    CompiledObligorCredit
        `uid` sorted ascending; all other columns reordered by the same
        permutation to stay row-aligned.
    """
    if obligor_df.empty:
        raise ValueError("compile_obligor_master: cannot compile an empty frame.")

    _check_required_columns(obligor_df)

    # Sort once by uid; one stable permutation applied to every column. Sorting
    # the frame (not the arrays) keeps the parent_uid fillna semantics intact.
    df = obligor_df.sort_values("uid", kind="stable").reset_index(drop=True)

    uid = _i64(df, "uid")
    pd_ = _f64(df, "pd")
    lgd = _f64(df, "lgd")
    rating_id = _i8(df, "rating_id")
    sector_id = _i32(df, "sector_id")
    country_id = _i32(df, "country_id")
    parent_uid = _parent_uid(df)

    validate_obligor_master_arrays(
        uid=uid,
        pd=pd_,
        lgd=lgd,
        rating_id=rating_id,
        sector_id=sector_id,
        country_id=country_id,
        parent_uid=parent_uid,
    )

    return CompiledObligorCredit(
        uid=uid,
        pd=pd_,
        lgd=lgd,
        rating_id=rating_id,
        sector_id=sector_id,
        country_id=country_id,
        parent_uid=parent_uid,
    )
```
</file>

```python
This module is intentionally a thin shell: it owns I/O only, never
```

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\credit\obligors\validate.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/credit/obligors/validate.py
"""
Obligor master validators.

Pure vectorized predicates on the post-ingest SoA arrays. Each function
either passes silently or raises `ValueError` with the (bounded) list of
offending uids. No row loops, no pandas, no string lookups — these run
once at ingest time, on float64/int64/int32/int8 arrays already cast to
their compiled dtypes.

The validators are split (uid uniqueness, range checks, sentinel checks)
rather than fused so any concrete failure points at exactly one invariant.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.constants import (
    NO_COUNTRY_ID,
    NO_PARENT_OBLIGOR,
    NO_RATING_ID,
    NO_SECTOR_ID,
)

_PREVIEW = 10
_PD_LO = 0.0
_PD_HI = 1.0
_LGD_LO = 0.0
_LGD_HI = 1.0


def _bad_uids(uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64], mask: npt.NDArray[np.bool_]) -> list[int]:
    return uid[mask][:_PREVIEW].tolist()


def validate_uid_sorted_unique(uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64]) -> None:
    """`uid` must be strictly ascending — uniqueness + sorted in one check."""
    if uid.shape[0] == 0:
        raise ValueError("obligor master: empty uid array.")
    if not (np.diff(uid) > 0).all():
        # Surface duplicates first (the common bug); sortedness second.
        unique, counts = np.unique(uid, return_counts=True)
        dups = unique[counts > 1][:_PREVIEW].tolist()
        if dups:
            raise ValueError(f"obligor master: duplicate uid(s) {dups}.")
        raise ValueError("obligor master: uid is not sorted ascending.")


def validate_pd_range(uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64], pd: npt.NDArray[np.float64]) -> None:
    """PD in [0, 1]; NaN is also rejected (np.isnan short-circuits the compare)."""
    bad = ~((pd >= _PD_LO) & (pd <= _PD_HI))
    if bad.any():
        raise ValueError(
            f"obligor master: PD outside [{_PD_LO}, {_PD_HI}] for uid(s) "
            f"{_bad_uids(uid, bad)}."
        )


def validate_lgd_range(
    uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64], lgd: npt.NDArray[np.float64]
) -> None:
    """LGD in [0, 1]; NaN is also rejected."""
    bad = ~((lgd >= _LGD_LO) & (lgd <= _LGD_HI))
    if bad.any():
        raise ValueError(
            f"obligor master: LGD outside [{_LGD_LO}, {_LGD_HI}] for uid(s) "
            f"{_bad_uids(uid, bad)}."
        )


def validate_rating_id(
    uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64], rating_id: npt.NDArray[np.int8]
) -> None:
    """
    `rating_id` must be either the unknown sentinel (`NO_RATING_ID == 0`) or
    a positive int8. The full rating-id taxonomy lives in
    `config/enums/credit_ratings`; this validator only enforces the dtype-
    level invariant. The taxonomy check is deferred until the rating enum
    is promoted to an IntEnum and a canonical {AAA..D} -> int8 mapping
    lands.
    """
    bad = rating_id < np.int8(0)
    if bad.any():
        raise ValueError(
            f"obligor master: rating_id < 0 (must be NO_RATING_ID={NO_RATING_ID} "
            f"or a positive int8) for uid(s) {_bad_uids(uid, bad)}."
        )


def validate_sector_id(
    uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64], sector_id: npt.NDArray[np.int32]
) -> None:
    bad = (sector_id < np.int32(0)) & (sector_id != NO_SECTOR_ID)
    if bad.any():
        raise ValueError(
            f"obligor master: sector_id < 0 and not NO_SECTOR_ID for uid(s) "
            f"{_bad_uids(uid, bad)}."
        )


def validate_country_id(
    uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64], country_id: npt.NDArray[np.int32]
) -> None:
    bad = (country_id < np.int32(0)) & (country_id != NO_COUNTRY_ID)
    if bad.any():
        raise ValueError(
            f"obligor master: country_id < 0 and not NO_COUNTRY_ID for uid(s) "
            f"{_bad_uids(uid, bad)}."
        )


def validate_parent_uid(
    uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64], parent_uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64]
) -> None:
    """
    Each `parent_uid` is either `NO_PARENT_OBLIGOR` or an obligor uid present
    on this same master. The check is one `searchsorted` on the ascending
    `uid` array — no row loop.
    """
    needs_lookup = parent_uid != NO_PARENT_OBLIGOR
    if not needs_lookup.any():
        return

    targets = parent_uid[needs_lookup]
    pos = np.searchsorted(uid, targets)
    pos = np.clip(pos, 0, uid.shape[0] - 1)
    matched = uid[pos] == targets
    if not matched.all():
        bad_children = uid[needs_lookup][~matched][:_PREVIEW].tolist()
        bad_parents = targets[~matched][:_PREVIEW].tolist()
        raise ValueError(
            f"obligor master: parent_uid(s) {bad_parents} (children "
            f"{bad_children}) absent from the obligor master."
        )

    # Self-parenting check: not allowed.
    self_parent = uid[needs_lookup] == targets
    if self_parent.any():
        bad = uid[needs_lookup][self_parent][:_PREVIEW].tolist()
        raise ValueError(
            f"obligor master: obligor(s) {bad} list themselves as parent_uid."
        )


def validate_obligor_master_arrays(
    *,
    uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64],
    pd: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    lgd: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    rating_id: npt.NDArray[np.int8],
    sector_id: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
    country_id: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
    parent_uid: npt.NDArray[np.int64],
) -> None:
    """Single-call entry point: every validator, fail-fast on first violation."""
    validate_uid_sorted_unique(uid)
    validate_pd_range(uid, pd)
    validate_lgd_range(uid, lgd)
    validate_rating_id(uid, rating_id)
    validate_sector_id(uid, sector_id)
    validate_country_id(uid, country_id)
    validate_parent_uid(uid, parent_uid)
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\exceptions.py">
```python
# riskquant/exceptions.py


class PrettyKeyError(KeyError):
    """
    KeyError variant that prints the message using str(...) instead of repr(...),
    allowing multi-line, human-readable diagnostics while preserving KeyError
    semantics.
    """

    def __str__(self) -> str:
        return str(self.args[0])


class RiskQuantError(Exception):
    """Base exception for all custom riskquant errors."""

    pass


class ConfigurationError(RiskQuantError):
    """
    Raised when static configuration, ID namespaces, or
    market data mappings violate the engine's strict contracts.
    """

    pass
```

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\market_data\builder.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/market_data/builder.py
"""
Market-data matrix builder.

Sits on the producer side of the valuation contract. Takes raw
scenario-shocked levels plus the `UixMapper` describing the run's
global universe, and emits a frozen `MarketDataMatrix` whose every
axis is aligned with the per-trade `_uix` coordinates carried by the
compiled blocks.

Key responsibilities
--------------------
1. FX effective matrix. Reads `UixMapper.fx_effective_pairs` to learn
   which `(fx_uix, invert)` pairs are needed across the portfolio,
   then materialises `fx_spots_effective: (K, S)` from the raw
   `(F, S)` direct-quote scenario matrix. Inverted rows are resolved
   via vectorised `np.reciprocal(out=...)`; direct rows are a single
   block copy. The pricers see zero inversion math.

2. Discount-factor grid. Log-linear interpolation in time, flat
   extrapolation. Routed through the zero-allocation Numba kernel
   with the pre-allocated output buffer.

3. Price-curve grid. Same shape as discount, but interpolation
   method varies per curve and is dispatched via `CURVE_MATH_REGISTRY`
   on `(interp_method, extrap_method)` group masks.

4. Time-to-expiry grid. Act/365.25 day count from `as_of_date` to
   each unique portfolio date, floored at 1e-9 to keep `sqrt(tte)`
   well-defined for same-day expiries.

5. Vol surfaces. Time-slice interpolation onto the unique-date
   universe. Output layout is `[surface, expiry, scenario, pillar]`
   so the strike-axis interpolation in the option-curve kernel can
   walk a contiguous pillar run per (trade, scenario).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from datetime import date
from typing import TYPE_CHECKING

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.market_data.curves.interp_defs import (
    interp_log_linear_flat_extrap_3d_numba_kernel,
)
from riskquant.market_data.curves.interp_dispatch import CURVE_MATH_REGISTRY
from riskquant.market_data.state import MarketDataMatrix
from riskquant.market_data.surfaces.y_axes_types import (
    SURFACE_Y_AXIS_TYPE_DELTA_CALL,
)
from riskquant.utils.dates import date_to_ord32

if TYPE_CHECKING:
    from riskquant.portfolio.engine import UixMapper

_ACT_365_25: float = 365.25
_TTE_FLOOR: float = 1e-9


# ==============================================================================
# FX EFFECTIVE
# ==============================================================================


def _build_fx_spots_effective(
    *,
    raw_fx_spots: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    fx_effective_pairs: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
    num_scenarios: int,
) -> npt.NDArray[np.float64]:
    """
    Materialise the `(K, S)` effective FX matrix from the dedup pair table.

    `fx_effective_pairs[k] == (fx_uix, invert)` — `fx_uix` is a row index
    into `raw_fx_spots` (or `< 0` for an identity-row sentinel), `invert`
    is `0` for direct and `1` for inverted (i.e. `1 / rate`).
    """
    k_pairs = fx_effective_pairs.shape[0]
    out = np.empty((k_pairs, num_scenarios), dtype=np.float64)

    if k_pairs == 0:
        return out

    if fx_effective_pairs.shape[1] != 2:
        raise ValueError(
            f"fx_effective_pairs must have shape (K, 2); got {fx_effective_pairs.shape}"
        )

    fx_uixs = fx_effective_pairs[:, 0]
    invs = fx_effective_pairs[:, 1]

    sentinel_mask = fx_uixs < 0
    direct_mask = (~sentinel_mask) & (invs == 0)
    invert_mask = (~sentinel_mask) & (invs == 1)

    if sentinel_mask.any():
        out[sentinel_mask, :] = 1.0

    if direct_mask.any():
        np.copyto(out[direct_mask, :], raw_fx_spots[fx_uixs[direct_mask]])

    if invert_mask.any():
        np.reciprocal(
            raw_fx_spots[fx_uixs[invert_mask]],
            out=out[invert_mask, :],
        )

    return out


# ==============================================================================
# CURVE GRIDS
# ==============================================================================


def _build_price_curve_grid(
    *,
    raw_tenors: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    raw_values: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    target_dates: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
    interp_methods: npt.NDArray[np.int8],
    extrap_methods: npt.NDArray[np.int8],
    num_scenarios: int,
) -> npt.NDArray[np.float64]:
    """
    Group-dispatched 3-D curve interpolation onto the unique-date grid.

    Curves sharing the same `(interp_method, extrap_method)` pair are
    gathered into a contiguous scratch buffer, fed to the dispatched
    kernel, then scatter-assigned back into `out_grid`.

    Scatter contract
    ----------------
    `out_grid[idx]` on the LHS of an assignment is NumPy fancy-index
    setitem and is in-place. `out_grid[idx]` on the RHS (the kernel's
    original signature) would have been a fresh COPY — the kernel
    would have written into the copy, the copy would have been
    discarded, and `out_grid` would have been left as `np.empty()`
    garbage. The scratch + scatter pattern is the correct fix.
    """
    c_curves = raw_values.shape[0]
    d_dates = target_dates.shape[0]

    out_grid = np.empty((c_curves, d_dates, num_scenarios), dtype=np.float64)

    if d_dates == 0 or c_curves == 0:
        return out_grid

    for (interp_id, extrap_id), kernel in CURVE_MATH_REGISTRY.items():
        mask = (interp_methods == interp_id) & (extrap_methods == extrap_id)
        if not mask.any():
            continue

        idx = np.where(mask)[0]
        n_in_group = idx.shape[0]

        # Gather to a contiguous scratch buffer the kernel can write to.
        scratch = np.empty(
            (n_in_group, d_dates, num_scenarios),
            dtype=np.float64,
        )
        kernel(
            np.ascontiguousarray(raw_tenors[idx]),
            np.ascontiguousarray(raw_values[idx]),
            target_dates,
            scratch,
        )
        # Scatter back. Fancy-indexed ASSIGNMENT is in-place.
        out_grid[idx] = scratch

    return out_grid


def _build_discount_factor_grid(
    *,
    raw_tenors: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    raw_ln_dfs: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    target_dates: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
    num_scenarios: int,
) -> npt.NDArray[np.float64]:
    """
    Discount-factor grid: log-linear interpolation in time, flat extrap.

    Domain-convention: every discount curve in the system is quoted as
    log(DF) at pillar dates and interpolated linearly on log(DF), which
    is equivalent to linear interpolation on zero rates between pillars.
    The flat extrapolation prevents pathological exponentials at the long
    end and matches the front-office convention.
    """
    c_curves = raw_ln_dfs.shape[0]
    d_dates = target_dates.shape[0]

    out_grid = np.empty((c_curves, d_dates, num_scenarios), dtype=np.float64)

    if d_dates == 0 or c_curves == 0:
        return out_grid

    interp_log_linear_flat_extrap_3d_numba_kernel(
        raw_tenors,
        raw_ln_dfs,
        target_dates,
        out_grid,
    )
    return out_grid


# ==============================================================================
# VOL SURFACES
# ==============================================================================


def _build_vol_surfaces(
    *,
    raw_vol_surface_tenors: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    raw_vol_surface_strikes: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    raw_vol_surface_values: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    surface_interp_x_methods: npt.NDArray[np.int8],
    surface_extrap_x_methods: npt.NDArray[np.int8],
    surface_y_axis_type_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int8],
    target_dates: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
    num_scenarios: int,
) -> npt.NDArray[np.float64]:
    """
    Time-slice every vol surface onto the portfolio's unique-date axis
    and emit the canonical kernel-facing layout.

    Input  : `raw_vol_surface_values` is `(C_surf, M_tenors, K_strikes, S_scn)`.
    Output : `(C_surf, D_dates, S_scn, K_strikes)` — pillar in trailing axis.
    """
    if (surface_y_axis_type_ids == SURFACE_Y_AXIS_TYPE_DELTA_CALL).any():
        raise ValueError(
            "FATAL: Delta-quoted Volatility Surfaces detected in runtime builder. "
            "DELTA_CALL surfaces must be pre-processed and converted to STRIKE or "
            "MONEYNESS by the Calibration module before being passed to the "
            "MarketDataMatrix."
        )

    c_surf = raw_vol_surface_tenors.shape[0] if raw_vol_surface_tenors.ndim > 0 else 0
    k_strikes = (
        raw_vol_surface_strikes.shape[1] if raw_vol_surface_strikes.ndim > 1 else 0
    )
    d_dates = target_dates.shape[0]

    if d_dates == 0 or raw_vol_surface_values.size == 0 or c_surf == 0:
        return np.empty((c_surf, d_dates, num_scenarios, k_strikes), dtype=np.float64)

    c_in, m_tenors, k_in, s_in = raw_vol_surface_values.shape
    if c_in != c_surf or k_in != k_strikes or s_in != num_scenarios:
        raise ValueError(
            f"raw_vol_surface_values shape {raw_vol_surface_values.shape} is "
            f"inconsistent with tenors/strikes/scenarios "
            f"({c_surf}, {m_tenors}, {k_strikes}, {num_scenarios})."
        )

    # Step 1. Fold (pillar, scenario) into one trailing axis and feed
    # the time-interp kernels as a 3-D (c, m_tenors, k * s) array.
    flat_in = raw_vol_surface_values.reshape(
        c_surf, m_tenors, k_strikes * num_scenarios
    )
    time_sliced_flat = _build_price_curve_grid(
        raw_tenors=raw_vol_surface_tenors,
        raw_values=np.ascontiguousarray(flat_in),
        target_dates=target_dates,
        interp_methods=surface_interp_x_methods,
        extrap_methods=surface_extrap_x_methods,
        num_scenarios=k_strikes * num_scenarios,
    )

    # Step 2. Reshape back to (c, d, k, s) and swap to land in the kernel-
    # facing (c, d, s, k) layout. Force C-contiguous because the kernel
    # signature pins `[:, :, :, ::1]`.
    canonical = time_sliced_flat.reshape(
        c_surf, d_dates, k_strikes, num_scenarios
    ).swapaxes(2, 3)
    return np.ascontiguousarray(canonical)


# ==============================================================================
# TOP-LEVEL ENTRY POINT
# ==============================================================================


def build_market_data_matrix(
    *,
    as_of_date: date,
    uix_mapper: UixMapper,
    num_scenarios: int,
    # ---- Raw scalar matrices (F/P/Fwd/Vol, scenarios) ----
    raw_fx_spots: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    raw_prices: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    raw_forward_nodes: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    raw_vol_nodes: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    # ---- Curve configs ----
    raw_discount_tenors: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    raw_discount_ln_dfs: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    raw_price_curve_tenors: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    raw_price_curve_values: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    curve_interp_methods: npt.NDArray[np.int8],
    curve_extrap_methods: npt.NDArray[np.int8],
    # ---- Surface configs ----
    raw_vol_surface_tenors: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    raw_vol_surface_strikes: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    raw_vol_surface_values: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    surface_interp_x_methods: npt.NDArray[np.int8],
    surface_extrap_x_methods: npt.NDArray[np.int8],
    surface_y_axis_type_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int8],
) -> MarketDataMatrix:
    """
    Assemble a frozen `MarketDataMatrix` aligned to the run's UIX universes.

    Parameters
    ----------
    uix_mapper
        Source of truth for the FX-effective pair table and the unique-
        date axis. Built once per run by `build_uix_mapper`.

    Returns
    -------
    MarketDataMatrix
        Valuation-ready container; pricers consume directly.
    """
    unique_dates_ord32 = uix_mapper.date_ord32_universe
    d_dates = unique_dates_ord32.shape[0]

    # ---- 1. FX effective matrix ------------------------------------------
    fx_spots_effective = _build_fx_spots_effective(
        raw_fx_spots=raw_fx_spots,
        fx_effective_pairs=uix_mapper.fx_effective_pairs,
        num_scenarios=num_scenarios,
    )

    # ---- 2. Discount factor grid -----------------------------------------
    discount_factor_grid = _build_discount_factor_grid(
        raw_tenors=raw_discount_tenors,
        raw_ln_dfs=raw_discount_ln_dfs,
        target_dates=unique_dates_ord32,
        num_scenarios=num_scenarios,
    )

    # ---- 3. Price curve grid ---------------------------------------------
    price_curve_grid = _build_price_curve_grid(
        raw_tenors=raw_price_curve_tenors,
        raw_values=raw_price_curve_values,
        target_dates=unique_dates_ord32,
        interp_methods=curve_interp_methods,
        extrap_methods=curve_extrap_methods,
        num_scenarios=num_scenarios,
    )

    # ---- 4. TTE grid (act/365.25) ----------------------------------------
    as_of_ord32 = date_to_ord32(as_of_date)
    if d_dates == 0:
        tte_grid = np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        tte_grid = np.maximum(
            (unique_dates_ord32.astype(np.float64) - float(as_of_ord32)) / _ACT_365_25,
            _TTE_FLOOR,
        )

    # ---- 5. Vol surfaces (canonical [c, d, s, k] layout) -----------------
    vol_surfaces = _build_vol_surfaces(
        raw_vol_surface_tenors=raw_vol_surface_tenors,
        raw_vol_surface_strikes=raw_vol_surface_strikes,
        raw_vol_surface_values=raw_vol_surface_values,
        surface_interp_x_methods=surface_interp_x_methods,
        surface_extrap_x_methods=surface_extrap_x_methods,
        surface_y_axis_type_ids=surface_y_axis_type_ids,
        target_dates=unique_dates_ord32,
        num_scenarios=num_scenarios,
    )

    return MarketDataMatrix(
        num_scenarios=num_scenarios,
        fx_spots_effective=fx_spots_effective,
        prices=raw_prices,
        forward_nodes=raw_forward_nodes,
        vol_nodes=raw_vol_nodes,
        discount_factor_grid=discount_factor_grid,
        price_curve_grid=price_curve_grid,
        tte_grid=tte_grid,
        vol_surface_strikes=raw_vol_surface_strikes,
        vol_surfaces=vol_surfaces,
    )
```

```python
# src\riskquant\market_data\curves\extrap_methods.py

from __future__ import annotations

from enum import IntEnum

import numpy as np


class ExtrapMethod(IntEnum):
    FLAT = 1
    LINEAR = 2


# compiler-friendly int8 constants
EXTRAP_FLAT: np.int8 = np.int8(ExtrapMethod.FLAT)
EXTRAP_LINEAR: np.int8 = np.int8(ExtrapMethod.LINEAR)


# Extrap methods
EXTRAP_METHOD_NAMES: tuple[str, ...] = tuple(
    method.name.upper() for method in ExtrapMethod
)

# Central registry
EXTRAP_METHOD_REGISTRY: dict[str, ExtrapMethod] = {
    "LINEAR": ExtrapMethod.LINEAR,
    "FLAT": ExtrapMethod.FLAT,
}


def validate_extrapolation_methods_dict(
    extrapolation_methods_dict: dict[str, int],
) -> None:
    expected = {
        name: int(member) for name, member in ExtrapMethod.__members__.items()
    }
    got = {str(k): int(v) for k, v in extrapolation_methods_dict.items()}

    if got == expected:
        return

    missing = {k: expected[k] for k in expected.keys() - got.keys()}
    extra = {k: got[k] for k in got.keys() - expected.keys()}
    different = {
        k: {"expected": expected[k], "got": got[k]}
        for k in (expected.keys() & got.keys())
        if expected[k] != got[k]
    }

    raise ValueError(
        "extrapolation_methods sheet does not match riskquant ExtrapMethod enum.\n"
        f"missing={missing}\n"
        f"extra={extra}\n"
        f"different={different}\n"
        "Fix the Excel extrapolation_methods table or update riskquant.market_data.curves.extrap_methods.ExtrapMethod."
    )
```

```python
# src/riskquant/market_data/curves/interp_defs.py
from __future__ import annotations

import math

import numba as nb
import numpy as np

# ==============================================================================
# --- LINEAR INTERPOLATION WITH FLAT EXTRAPOLATION ---
# ==============================================================================


def interp_linear_flat_extrap_3d(
    tenors: np.ndarray,
    values: np.ndarray,
    targets: np.ndarray,
) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Vectorized 3D Linear Interpolation with Flat Extrapolation.
    Shapes: tenors (c_curves, m_pillars), values (c_curves, m_pillars, s_scenarios), targets (d_targets,)
    Returns: (c_curves, d_targets, s_scenarios)
    """
    c_curves, m_pillars = tenors.shape
    idx1 = np.clip(
        np.sum(tenors[:, None, :] <= targets[None, :, None], axis=2),
        1,
        m_pillars - 1,
    )
    idx0 = idx1 - 1

    c_idx = np.arange(c_curves)[:, None]
    t0, t1 = tenors[c_idx, idx0], tenors[c_idx, idx1]
    y0, y1 = values[c_idx, idx0, :], values[c_idx, idx1, :]

    dt = np.where((t1 - t0) == 0, 1.0, t1 - t0)
    w = (targets[None, :] - t0) / dt

    # FLAT EXTRAPOLATION: Clamp weights to [0.0, 1.0]
    w = np.clip(w, 0.0, 1.0)

    return y0 + w[:, :, None] * (y1 - y0)


@nb.njit(
    parallel=True,
    fastmath={"contract", "reassoc", "nsz"},
    nogil=True,
    boundscheck=False,
    error_model="numpy",
    cache=True,
)
def interp_linear_flat_extrap_3d_numba_kernel(
    tenors: np.ndarray,
    values: np.ndarray,
    targets: np.ndarray,
    out: np.ndarray,
) -> None:
    """Zero-allocation 3D Linear Interpolator with Flat Extrapolation."""
    c_curves, m_pillars = tenors.shape
    d_target_dates = targets.shape[0]
    s_scenarios = values.shape[2]

    for c in nb.prange(c_curves):
        for d in range(d_target_dates):
            target = targets[d]

            idx = np.searchsorted(tenors[c], target, side="right")
            idx1 = min(max(idx, 1), m_pillars - 1)
            idx0 = idx1 - 1

            t0 = tenors[c, idx0]
            t1 = tenors[c, idx1]

            dt = t1 - t0
            if dt == 0.0:
                dt = 1.0

            w = (target - t0) / dt
            w = min(max(w, 0.0), 1.0)

            for s in range(s_scenarios):
                y0 = values[c, idx0, s]
                y1 = values[c, idx1, s]
                out[c, d, s] = y0 + w * (y1 - y0)


# ==============================================================================
# --- LINEAR INTERPOLATION WITH LINEAR EXTRAPOLATION ---
# ==============================================================================


def interp_linear_linear_extrap_3d(
    tenors: np.ndarray,
    values: np.ndarray,
    targets: np.ndarray,
) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Vectorized 3D Linear Interpolation with Linear Extrapolation.
    Shapes: tenors (c_curves, m_pillars), values (c_curves, m_pillars, s_scenarios), targets (d_targets,)
    Returns: (c_curves, d_targets, s_scenarios)
    """
    c_curves, m_pillars = tenors.shape
    idx1 = np.clip(
        np.sum(tenors[:, None, :] <= targets[None, :, None], axis=2),
        1,
        m_pillars - 1,
    )
    idx0 = idx1 - 1

    c_idx = np.arange(c_curves)[:, None]
    t0, t1 = tenors[c_idx, idx0], tenors[c_idx, idx1]
    y0, y1 = values[c_idx, idx0, :], values[c_idx, idx1, :]

    dt = np.where((t1 - t0) == 0, 1.0, t1 - t0)
    w = (targets[None, :] - t0) / dt

    # LINEAR EXTRAPOLATION: Do not clamp weights
    return y0 + w[:, :, None] * (y1 - y0)


@nb.njit(
    parallel=True,
    fastmath={"contract", "reassoc", "nsz"},
    nogil=True,
    boundscheck=False,
    error_model="numpy",
    cache=True,
)
def interp_linear_linear_extrap_3d_numba_kernel(
    tenors: np.ndarray,
    values: np.ndarray,
    targets: np.ndarray,
    out: np.ndarray,
) -> None:
    """Zero-allocation 3D Linear Interpolator with Linear Extrapolation."""
    c_curves, m_pillars = tenors.shape
    d_target_dates = targets.shape[0]
    s_scenarios = values.shape[2]

    for c in nb.prange(c_curves):
        for d in range(d_target_dates):
            target = targets[d]

            idx = np.searchsorted(tenors[c], target, side="right")
            idx1 = min(max(idx, 1), m_pillars - 1)
            idx0 = idx1 - 1

            t0 = tenors[c, idx0]
            t1 = tenors[c, idx1]

            dt = t1 - t0
            if dt == 0.0:
                dt = 1.0

            w = (target - t0) / dt
            # LINEAR EXTRAPOLATION: Deliberately do not clamp w

            for s in range(s_scenarios):
                y0 = values[c, idx0, s]
                y1 = values[c, idx1, s]
                out[c, d, s] = y0 + w * (y1 - y0)


# ==============================================================================
# --- LOG-LINEAR INTERPOLATION WITH FLAT EXTRAPOLATION ---
# ==============================================================================


def interp_log_linear_flat_extrap_3d(
    tenors: np.ndarray,
    values: np.ndarray,
    targets: np.ndarray,
) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Vectorized 3D Log-Linear Interpolation with Constant Forward Extrapolation.
    """
    c_curves, m_pillars = tenors.shape
    mask = tenors[:, None, :] <= targets[None, :, None]
    idx1 = np.sum(mask, axis=2)
    idx1 = np.clip(idx1, 1, m_pillars - 1)
    idx0 = idx1 - 1

    c_idx = np.arange(c_curves)[:, None]
    t0 = tenors[c_idx, idx0]
    t1 = tenors[c_idx, idx1]

    y0 = values[c_idx, idx0, :]
    y1 = values[c_idx, idx1, :]

    dt = t1 - t0
    dt = np.where(dt == 0, 1.0, dt)
    w = (targets[None, :] - t0) / dt

    ln_interp = y0 + w[:, :, None] * (y1 - y0)
    return np.exp(ln_interp)


@nb.njit(
    parallel=True,
    fastmath={"contract", "reassoc", "nsz"},
    nogil=True,
    boundscheck=False,
    error_model="numpy",
    cache=True,
)
def interp_log_linear_flat_extrap_3d_numba_kernel(
    tenors: np.ndarray,
    values: np.ndarray,
    targets: np.ndarray,
    out: np.ndarray,
) -> None:
    """
    Zero-allocation 3D Log-Linear Interpolator.
    Mathematically equivalent to linear interpolation on Zero Rates.
    """
    c_curves, m_pillars = tenors.shape
    d_target_dates = targets.shape[0]
    s_scenarios = values.shape[2]

    for c in nb.prange(c_curves):
        for d in range(d_target_dates):
            target = targets[d]

            idx = np.searchsorted(tenors[c], target, side="right")
            idx1 = min(max(idx, 1), m_pillars - 1)
            idx0 = idx1 - 1

            t0 = tenors[c, idx0]
            t1 = tenors[c, idx1]

            dt = t1 - t0
            if dt == 0.0:
                dt = 1.0

            w = (target - t0) / dt

            for s in range(s_scenarios):
                y0 = values[c, idx0, s]
                y1 = values[c, idx1, s]
                ln_interp = y0 + w * (y1 - y0)
                out[c, d, s] = math.exp(ln_interp)


# ==============================================================================
# --- LOG-LINEAR INTERPOLATION WITH LINEAR EXTRAPOLATION ---
# ==============================================================================


def interp_log_linear_linear_extrap_3d(
    tenors: np.ndarray,
    values: np.ndarray,
    targets: np.ndarray,
) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Vectorized 3D Log-Linear Interpolation with Linear Extrapolation.
    """
    c_curves, m_pillars = tenors.shape
    idx1 = np.clip(
        np.sum(tenors[:, None, :] <= targets[None, :, None], axis=2),
        1,
        m_pillars - 1,
    )
    idx0 = idx1 - 1

    c_idx = np.arange(c_curves)[:, None]
    t0, t1 = tenors[c_idx, idx0], tenors[c_idx, idx1]
    y0, y1 = values[c_idx, idx0, :], values[c_idx, idx1, :]

    dt = np.where((t1 - t0) == 0, 1.0, t1 - t0)
    w = (targets[None, :] - t0) / dt

    ln_interp = y0 + w[:, :, None] * (y1 - y0)
    return np.exp(ln_interp)


@nb.njit(
    parallel=True,
    fastmath={"contract", "reassoc", "nsz"},
    nogil=True,
    boundscheck=False,
    error_model="numpy",
    cache=True,
)
def interp_log_linear_linear_extrap_3d_numba_kernel(
    tenors: np.ndarray,
    values: np.ndarray,
    targets: np.ndarray,
    out: np.ndarray,
) -> None:
    """
    Zero-allocation 3D Log-Linear Interpolator with Linear Extrapolation.
    Mathematically identical to the flat forward logic in log-space.
    """
    c_curves, m_pillars = tenors.shape
    d_target_dates = targets.shape[0]
    s_scenarios = values.shape[2]

    for c in nb.prange(c_curves):
        for d in range(d_target_dates):
            target = targets[d]

            idx = np.searchsorted(tenors[c], target, side="right")
            idx1 = min(max(idx, 1), m_pillars - 1)
            idx0 = idx1 - 1

            t0 = tenors[c, idx0]
            t1 = tenors[c, idx1]

            dt = t1 - t0
            if dt == 0.0:
                dt = 1.0

            w = (target - t0) / dt

            for s in range(s_scenarios):
                y0 = values[c, idx0, s]
                y1 = values[c, idx1, s]
                ln_interp = y0 + w * (y1 - y0)
                out[c, d, s] = math.exp(ln_interp)


# ==============================================================================
# --- STEP LEFT (LOCF) INTERPOLATION WITH FLAT EXTRAPOLATION ---
# ==============================================================================


def interp_step_left_flat_extrap_3d(
    tenors: np.ndarray,
    values: np.ndarray,
    targets: np.ndarray,
) -> np.ndarray:
    """Vectorized 3D Step Left (LOCF) Interpolation."""
    c_curves, m_pillars = tenors.shape
    idx = np.clip(
        np.sum(tenors[:, None, :] <= targets[None, :, None], axis=2) - 1,
        0,
        m_pillars - 1,
    )
    c_idx = np.arange(c_curves)[:, None]
    return values[c_idx, idx, :]


@nb.njit(
    parallel=True,
    fastmath={"contract", "reassoc", "nsz"},
    nogil=True,
    boundscheck=False,
    error_model="numpy",
    cache=True,
)
def interp_step_left_flat_extrap_3d_numba_kernel(
    tenors: np.ndarray,
    values: np.ndarray,
    targets: np.ndarray,
    out: np.ndarray,
) -> None:
    """Zero-allocation 3D Step Left (LOCF) Interpolator."""
    c_curves, m_pillars = tenors.shape
    d_target_dates = targets.shape[0]
    s_scenarios = values.shape[2]

    for c in nb.prange(c_curves):
        for d in range(d_target_dates):
            target = targets[d]
            # side="right" ensures we find the first index > target.
            # Subtracting 1 gives the index <= target (Last Observation Carried Forward).
            idx = np.searchsorted(tenors[c], target, side="right") - 1
            idx = min(max(idx, 0), m_pillars - 1)

            for s in range(s_scenarios):
                out[c, d, s] = values[c, idx, s]


# ==============================================================================
# --- STEP LEFT (LOCF) INTERPOLATION WITH LINEAR EXTRAPOLATION ---
# ==============================================================================


def interp_step_left_linear_extrap_3d(
    tenors: np.ndarray,
    values: np.ndarray,
    targets: np.ndarray,
) -> np.ndarray:
    """Vectorized 3D Step Left (LOCF) Interpolation with Linear Extrapolation."""
    c_curves, m_pillars = tenors.shape
    idx = np.clip(
        np.sum(tenors[:, None, :] <= targets[None, :, None], axis=2) - 1,
        0,
        m_pillars - 1,
    )
    c_idx = np.arange(c_curves)[:, None]
    return values[c_idx, idx, :]


@nb.njit(
    parallel=True,
    fastmath={"contract", "reassoc", "nsz"},
    nogil=True,
    boundscheck=False,
    error_model="numpy",
    cache=True,
)
def interp_step_left_linear_extrap_3d_numba_kernel(
    tenors: np.ndarray,
    values: np.ndarray,
    targets: np.ndarray,
    out: np.ndarray,
) -> None:
    """
    Zero-allocation 3D Step Left (LOCF) Interpolator.
    Mathematically identical to flat extrapolation for step functions.
    """
    c_curves, m_pillars = tenors.shape
    d_target_dates = targets.shape[0]
    s_scenarios = values.shape[2]

    for c in nb.prange(c_curves):
        for d in range(d_target_dates):
            target = targets[d]
            idx = np.searchsorted(tenors[c], target, side="right") - 1
            idx = min(max(idx, 0), m_pillars - 1)

            for s in range(s_scenarios):
                out[c, d, s] = values[c, idx, s]

        0,
        m_pillars - 1,
    )
    c_idx = np.arange(c_curves)[:, None]

@nb.njit(

    nogil=True,

def interp_step_right_linear_extrap_3d_numba_kernel(

    """

            idx = np.searchsorted(tenors[c], target, side="left")
            idx = min(max(idx, 0), m_pillars - 1)

            for s in range(s_scenarios):
                out[c, d, s] = values[c, idx, s]


# ==============================================================================
# --- 2D SURFACE STRIKE INTERPOLATOR ---
# ==============================================================================


def interp_strike_linear_flat_extrap(
    pillars: np.ndarray,  # Shape: (N_trades, K_strikes)
    values: np.ndarray,  # Shape: (N_trades, K_strikes, S_scenarios)
    targets: np.ndarray,  # Shape: (N_trades, S_scenarios)
) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Vectorized 1D linear interpolation along the strike/Y-axis with flat extrapolation.
    Used dynamically inside Option Pricers to resolve exact strike vol across scenarios.
    """
    N, K_dim = pillars.shape
    S = targets.shape[1]

    mask = pillars[:, None, :] <= targets[:, :, None]
    idx1 = np.sum(mask, axis=2)
    idx1 = np.clip(idx1, 1, K_dim - 1)
    idx0 = idx1 - 1

    n_idx = np.arange(N)[:, None]
    s_idx = np.arange(S)[None, :]

    x0 = pillars[n_idx, idx0]
    x1 = pillars[n_idx, idx1]

    y0 = values[n_idx, idx0, s_idx]
    y1 = values[n_idx, idx1, s_idx]

    dx = np.where((x1 - x0) == 0, 1.0, x1 - x0)
    w = (targets - x0) / dx

    w = np.clip(w, 0.0, 1.0)
    return y0 + w * (y1 - y0)


@nb.njit(
    parallel=True,
    fastmath={"contract", "reassoc", "nsz"},
    nogil=True,
    boundscheck=False,
    error_model="numpy",
    cache=True,
)
def interp_strike_linear_flat_extrap_numba_kernel(
    pillars: np.ndarray,
    values: np.ndarray,
    targets: np.ndarray,
    out: np.ndarray,
) -> None:
    """
    Zero-allocation 2D Surface Strike Interpolator.
    Pre-allocates an (N_trades, S_scenarios) array and resolves exactly.
    """
    n_trades, k_strikes = pillars.shape
    s_scenarios = targets.shape[1]

    for n in nb.prange(n_trades):
        for s in range(s_scenarios):
            target = targets[n, s]

            idx = np.searchsorted(pillars[n], target, side="right")
            idx1 = min(max(idx, 1), k_strikes - 1)
            idx0 = idx1 - 1

            x0 = pillars[n, idx0]
            x1 = pillars[n, idx1]

            dx = x1 - x0
            if dx == 0.0:
                dx = 1.0

            w = (target - x0) / dx
            w = min(max(w, 0.0), 1.0)

            y0 = values[n, idx0, s]
            y1 = values[n, idx1, s]

            out[n, s] = y0 + w * (y1 - y0)
```

```python
from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Callable

import numpy as np

from riskquant.market_data.curves.extrap_methods import (
    EXTRAP_FLAT,
    EXTRAP_LINEAR,
)

# Import the fused Numba kernels
from riskquant.market_data.curves.interp_defs import (
    interp_linear_flat_extrap_3d_numba_kernel,
    interp_linear_linear_extrap_3d_numba_kernel,
    interp_log_linear_flat_extrap_3d_numba_kernel,
    interp_log_linear_linear_extrap_3d_numba_kernel,
    interp_step_left_flat_extrap_3d_numba_kernel,
    interp_step_left_linear_extrap_3d_numba_kernel,
    interp_step_right_flat_extrap_3d_numba_kernel,
    interp_step_right_linear_extrap_3d_numba_kernel,
)

# Import your enum constants
from riskquant.market_data.curves.interp_methods import (
    INTERP_LINEAR,
    INTERP_LOG_LINEAR,
    INTERP_STEP_LEFT,
    INTERP_STEP_RIGHT,
)

# ============================================================
# INTERPOLATION DISPATCH REGISTRY
# ============================================================

# Define the strict signature every curve kernel must follow.
# Signature: (tenors, values, targets, out) -> None
InterpKernelFn = Callable[[np.ndarray, np.ndarray, np.ndarray, np.ndarray], None]

# Map (Interp Method ID, Extrap Method ID) -> Math Kernel
# We cast the np.int8 enums to pure Python ints for safe, fast dictionary hashing.
CURVE_MATH_REGISTRY: dict[tuple[int, int], InterpKernelFn] = {
    (
        int(INTERP_LINEAR),
        int(EXTRAP_FLAT),
    ): interp_linear_flat_extrap_3d_numba_kernel,
    (
        int(INTERP_LINEAR),
        int(EXTRAP_LINEAR),
    ): interp_linear_linear_extrap_3d_numba_kernel,
    (
        int(INTERP_LOG_LINEAR),
        int(EXTRAP_FLAT),
    ): interp_log_linear_flat_extrap_3d_numba_kernel,
    (
        int(INTERP_LOG_LINEAR),
        int(EXTRAP_LINEAR),
    ): interp_log_linear_linear_extrap_3d_numba_kernel,
    (
        int(INTERP_STEP_LEFT),
        int(EXTRAP_FLAT),
    ): interp_step_left_flat_extrap_3d_numba_kernel,
    (
        int(INTERP_STEP_LEFT),
        int(EXTRAP_LINEAR),
    ): interp_step_left_linear_extrap_3d_numba_kernel,
    (
        int(INTERP_STEP_RIGHT),
        int(EXTRAP_FLAT),
    ): interp_step_right_flat_extrap_3d_numba_kernel,
    (
        int(INTERP_STEP_RIGHT),
        int(EXTRAP_LINEAR),
    ): interp_step_right_linear_extrap_3d_numba_kernel,
}
```

```python
# src\riskquant\market_data\curves\interp_methods.py

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Mapping
from enum import IntEnum

import numpy as np


class InterpMethod(IntEnum):
    LINEAR = 1
    LOG_LINEAR = 2
    STEP_LEFT = 3
    STEP_RIGHT = 4
    CUBIC_SPLINE = 5


# compiler-friendly int8 constants
INTERP_LINEAR: np.int8 = np.int8(InterpMethod.LINEAR)
INTERP_LOG_LINEAR: np.int8 = np.int8(InterpMethod.LOG_LINEAR)
INTERP_STEP_LEFT: np.int8 = np.int8(InterpMethod.STEP_LEFT)
INTERP_STEP_RIGHT: np.int8 = np.int8(InterpMethod.STEP_RIGHT)
INTERP_CUBIC_SPLINE: np.int8 = np.int8(InterpMethod.CUBIC_SPLINE)

# Interp methods
INTERP_METHOD_NAMES: tuple[str, ...] = tuple(
    method.name.upper() for method in InterpMethod
)

# Central registry
INTERP_METHOD_REGISTRY: dict[str, InterpMethod] = {
    "LINEAR": InterpMethod.LINEAR,
    "LOG_LINEAR": InterpMethod.LOG_LINEAR,
    "STEP_LEFT": InterpMethod.STEP_LEFT,
    "STEP_RIGHT": InterpMethod.STEP_RIGHT,
    "CUBIC_SPLINE": InterpMethod.CUBIC_SPLINE,
}


def validate_interpolation_methods_dict(
    interpolation_methods_dict: Mapping[str, int],
) -> None:
    expected = {name: int(member) for name, member in InterpMethod.__members__.items()}
    got = {str(k): int(v) for k, v in interpolation_methods_dict.items()}

    if got == expected:
        return

    missing = {k: expected[k] for k in expected.keys() - got.keys()}
    extra = {k: got[k] for k in got.keys() - expected.keys()}
    different = {
        k: {"expected": expected[k], "got": got[k]}
        for k in (expected.keys() & got.keys())
        if expected[k] != got[k]
    }

    raise ValueError(
        "interpolation_methods sheet does not match riskquant InterpolationMethod enum.\n"
        f"missing={missing}\n"
        f"extra={extra}\n"
        f"different={different}\n"
        "Fix the Excel interpolation_methods table or update riskquant.market_data.curves.interp_methods.InterpMethod."
    )
```

```python
# src\riskquant\market_data\risk_factors\builder_matrix.py
from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Mapping
from pathlib import Path

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
from pandas.api.types import is_datetime64_any_dtype

from riskquant.market_data.risk_factors.builder_utils import (
    _require_mapping_key,
    _validate_mapping_dict,
)
from riskquant.market_data.risk_factors.matrix import RiskFactorMatrix
from riskquant.utils.dates import (
    coerce_as_of_date_to_pd_timestamp,
    datetime64D_to_ord32,
)

# --- BUILD A RISK FACTOR MATRIX FROM A DATAFRAME


def build_risk_factor_matrix_from_df(
    *,
    df: pd.DataFrame,
    name_to_id_map: Mapping[str, int] | None = None,
    use_int32_dates: bool = True,
    float_dtype: type[np.floating] = np.float32,
) -> RiskFactorMatrix:
    """Construct a RiskFactorMatrix from a wide pandas DataFrame.

    Parameters
    ----------
    df : pd.DataFrame
        Wide DataFrame with dates on the index and one column per risk factor.
    name_to_id_map : Optional[Mapping[str, int]]
        Optional mapping from column name (string) to integer factor id. If
        omitted, columns are parsed as integers directly.
    use_int32_dates : bool
        If True convert dates to int32 ordinal representation; otherwise
        keep datetime64[D] dtype.
    float_dtype : numpy floating type
        dtype used for the `values` array (default: np.float32).

    Returns
    -------
    RiskFactorMatrix
        Canonicalized matrix with validated shapes, dtypes and contiguity.

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError
        If the index is not date-like, not monotonic increasing, or contains
        duplicates; or if factor id mapping contains errors.

    Notes
    -----
    The function intentionally does not auto-sort the DataFrame rows
    (dates). If the provided index is unsorted we raise an error because
    auto-sorting would require an expensive O(T*N) copy of the values.
    """
    if not isinstance(df, pd.DataFrame):
        raise ValueError("Expected a DataFrame!")

    # Accept a wider set of date-like index types; coerce if needed.
    if not is_datetime64_any_dtype(df.index):
        # Convert in-place where possible; this preserves row order and
        # avoids unnecessary copying of the data matrix.
        df = df.copy(deep=False)
        df.index = pd.to_datetime(df.index, errors="raise")

    # Enforce invariants early with clear errors (and avoid needless allocations)
    if df.index.has_duplicates:
        raise ValueError("DataFrame index (dates) must be unique!")
    if not df.index.is_monotonic_increasing:
        raise ValueError("DataFrame index (dates) must be sorted ascending!")

    # Check for duplicated column labels (factor names); this would cause silent data corruption
    if df.columns.has_duplicates:
        duplicated = df.columns[df.columns.duplicated(keep=False)]
        unique_dupes = duplicated.unique()
        sample = unique_dupes[:10]
        raise ValueError(
            f"DataFrame columns (factor names) must be unique.\n"
            f"Found {len(unique_dupes)} duplicated column(s).\n"
            f"Sample duplicates: {list(sample)}"
        )

    # Dates: obtain a datetime64[D] view and optionally convert to int32 ordinal
    dts_64D = df.index.to_numpy(dtype="datetime64[D]", copy=False)
    dates = datetime64D_to_ord32(dts_64D) if use_int32_dates else dts_64D

    # Columns -> factor_ids: either direct integer parsing or mapping lookup.
    cols = df.columns
    n = cols.size

    if name_to_id_map is None:
        # Parse column labels as integers; will raise on non-integer labels.
        factor_ids = np.fromiter(
            (int(c) for c in cols),
            dtype=np.int32,
            count=n,
        )
    else:
        # Validate mapping once (cheap for small config dicts) and then map.
        _validate_mapping_dict(name_to_id_map)
        factor_ids = np.fromiter(
            (_require_mapping_key(name_to_id_map, str(c)) for c in cols),
            dtype=np.int32,
            count=n,
        )

    # Canonicalize factor axis BEFORE allocating `values` (avoids extra copies).
    if n > 1 and np.any(factor_ids[1:] < factor_ids[:-1]):
        perm = np.argsort(factor_ids, kind="mergesort")
        factor_ids = factor_ids[perm]

        if np.any(factor_ids[1:] == factor_ids[:-1]):
            raise ValueError("factor_ids must be unique!")

        # Reorder the DataFrame columns to match the canonical factor order.
        df = df.iloc[:, perm]
    else:
        if n > 1 and np.any(factor_ids[1:] == factor_ids[:-1]):
            raise ValueError("factor_ids must be unique!")

    # Materialize the numeric matrix as a C-contiguous array with requested dtype.
    values = np.ascontiguousarray(
        df.to_numpy(dtype=float_dtype, copy=True, na_value=np.nan)
    )

    return RiskFactorMatrix(dates=dates, factor_ids=factor_ids, values=values)


# --- BUILD A RISK FACTOR MATRIX DIRECTLY FROM AN XLSX FILE


def build_risk_factor_matrix_from_xlsx(
    *,
    path: str | Path,
    sheet_name: str | int = 0,
    name_to_id_map: Mapping[str, int] | None = None,
    use_int32_dates: bool = True,
    float_dtype: type[np.floating] = np.float32,
    as_of_date: pd.Timestamp | None = None,
) -> RiskFactorMatrix:
    """Load a RiskFactorMatrix from an Excel workbook/sheet.

    Parameters
    ----------
    path : str | Path
        Path to the Excel file.
    sheet_name : str | int
        Sheet name or index to read (forwarded to pandas.read_excel).
    name_to_id_map : Optional[Mapping[str, int]]
        Optional mapping for column name -> factor id (same semantics as
        `from_df`).
    use_int32_dates : bool
        If True convert dates to int32 ordinal representation.
    float_dtype : numpy floating type
        dtype used for the `values` array.
    as_of_date : Optional[pd.Timestamp]
        If provided, rows with date > as_of_date are filtered out.

    Returns
    -------
    RiskFactorMatrix
        Canonicalized matrix constructed from the Excel sheet.
    """
    # Read sheet with the first column treated as the index (dates expected).
    df = pd.read_excel(
        str(path),
        sheet_name=sheet_name,
        index_col=0,
    )

    # If empty.
    if df.empty:
        raise ValueError("dataframe is empty - check: {path}")

    # Convert index to datetime if needed (common for Excel imports).
    if not is_datetime64_any_dtype(df.index):
        df.index = pd.to_datetime(df.index, errors="raise")

    # Optional filter by as_of_date; preserves monotonicity if present.
    if as_of_date is not None:
        cutoff = coerce_as_of_date_to_pd_timestamp(as_of_date)
        df = df.loc[df.index <= cutoff]

    # Enforce same sorted/unique contract as `from_df`.

    # Duplicated dates.
    if df.index.has_duplicates:
        duplicated = df.index[df.index.duplicated(keep=False)]
        unique_dupes = duplicated.unique()
        sample = unique_dupes[:10]
        raise ValueError(
            f"Excel sheet index (dates) must be unique.\n"
            f"Found {len(unique_dupes)} duplicated date(s).\n"
            f"Sample duplicates: {list(sample)}"
        )

    # Non-monotonic dates
    if not df.index.is_monotonic_increasing:
        raise ValueError("Excel sheet index (dates) must be sorted ascending!")

    # Reuse the robust `from_df` constructor for the remaining work.
    return build_risk_factor_matrix_from_df(
        df=df,
        name_to_id_map=name_to_id_map,
        use_int32_dates=use_int32_dates,
        float_dtype=float_dtype,
    )
```

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\market_data\risk_factors\builder_snapshot.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/market_data/risk_factors/builder_snapshot.py
from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Mapping
from datetime import date
from pathlib import Path

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

from riskquant.market_data.risk_factors.builder_utils import (
    _require_mapping_key,
    _validate_mapping_dict,
)
from riskquant.market_data.risk_factors.snapshot import RiskFactorLevelSnapshot


def build_risk_factor_level_snapshot_from_dict(
    *,
    as_of_date: date,
    levels_dict: Mapping[str, float],
    name_to_id_map: Mapping[str, int] | None = None,
    float_dtype: type[np.floating] = np.float32,
) -> RiskFactorLevelSnapshot:
    """
    Build a snapshot from a dictionary mapping risk factor names to their levels.
    """
    if not levels_dict:
        raise ValueError("levels_dict cannot be empty.")

    n = len(levels_dict)

    if name_to_id_map is None:
        # Assume the keys in the dictionary are already integer IDs formatted as strings.
        ids = np.fromiter((int(k) for k in levels_dict.keys()), dtype=np.int32, count=n)
    else:
        # Resolve names to IDs
        _validate_mapping_dict(name_to_id_map)
        ids = np.fromiter(
            (_require_mapping_key(name_to_id_map, str(k)) for k in levels_dict.keys()),
            dtype=np.int32,
            count=n,
        )

    # Extract levels into a contiguous array
    levels = np.fromiter(levels_dict.values(), dtype=float_dtype, count=n)

    return RiskFactorLevelSnapshot(
        as_of_date=as_of_date,
        factor_ids=ids,
        factor_levels=levels,
    )


def build_risk_factor_level_snapshot_from_df(
    *,
    as_of_date: date,
    df: pd.DataFrame,
    factor_col: str = "risk_factor_name",
    level_col: str = "level",
    name_to_id_map: Mapping[str, int] | None = None,
    float_dtype: type[np.floating] = np.float32,
) -> RiskFactorLevelSnapshot:
    """
    Build a snapshot from a vertical pandas DataFrame.
    """
    if df.empty:
        raise ValueError("DataFrame is empty.")

    if factor_col not in df.columns or level_col not in df.columns:
        raise ValueError(
            f"DataFrame must contain columns '{factor_col}' and '{level_col}'."
        )

    # Drop any rows where the level is NaN
    df_clean = df.dropna(subset=[level_col])

    # There must not be duplicate factor names after dropping NaNs, as this would lead to ambiguity in the snapshot.
    if df_clean[factor_col].duplicated().any():
        duplicated_factors = df_clean[factor_col][
            df_clean[factor_col].duplicated()
        ].unique()
        raise ValueError(
            f"Duplicate factor names found in column '{factor_col}' after dropping NaNs: {duplicated_factors}"
        )

    # Extract as a dictionary to reuse the highly-optimized dictionary builder
    # zip is faster than df.set_index().to_dict() or df.itertuples()
    levels_dict = dict(zip(df_clean[factor_col].astype(str), df_clean[level_col]))

    return build_risk_factor_level_snapshot_from_dict(
        as_of_date=as_of_date,
        levels_dict=levels_dict,
        name_to_id_map=name_to_id_map,
        float_dtype=float_dtype,
    )


def build_risk_factor_level_snapshot_from_xlsx(
    *,
    as_of_date: date,
    path: str | Path,
    sheet_name: str | int = 0,
    factor_col: str = "risk_factor_name",
    level_col: str = "level",
    name_to_id_map: Mapping[str, int] | None = None,
    float_dtype: type[np.floating] = np.float32,
) -> RiskFactorLevelSnapshot:
    """
    Build a snapshot from a two-column Excel sheet.
    """
    df = pd.read_excel(str(path), sheet_name=sheet_name)

    return build_risk_factor_level_snapshot_from_df(
        as_of_date=as_of_date,
        df=df,
        factor_col=factor_col,
        level_col=level_col,
        name_to_id_map=name_to_id_map,
        float_dtype=float_dtype,
    )
```
</file>

```python
# src\riskquant\market_data\risk_factors\builder_utils.py
from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Mapping
from typing import Any

import numpy as np


def _validate_mapping_dict(m: Mapping[str, Any]) -> None:
    """Validate a small static mapping of risk factor name -> integer id.

    Checks performed:
      - keys must be non-empty strings
      - values must be integer-like (reject bool, float, str)
      - no two keys map to the same integer id

    This helper is optimized for configuration-time validation (small dicts),
    not for per-row lookups in tight loops.
    """
    # Empty/blank key detection (single pass)
    for k in m.keys():
        if not isinstance(k, str) or not k.strip():
            raise ValueError(f"Mapping contains invalid/blank key: {k!r}")

    # Ensure values are integer-like and detect duplicates by tracking seen ids.
    seen: dict[int, str] = {}
    for k, v in m.items():
        # `bool` is a subclass of int in Python; explicitly reject it here because
        # booleans are not valid factor ids.
        if isinstance(v, (bool,)):
            raise ValueError(
                f"Mapping value for '{k}' must be an int id, got bool={v!r}"
            )

        # Accept Python ints and NumPy integer scalars; reject other types.
        if not isinstance(v, (int, np.integer)):
            raise ValueError(
                f"Mapping value for '{k}' must be an int id, got {type(v).__name__}={v!r}"
            )

        iv = int(v)
        prev = seen.get(iv)
        if prev is not None:
            # Clear, actionable error message when multiple names map to same id.
            raise ValueError(
                "Mapping contains duplicate ID assignment:\n"
                f"  '{prev}' and '{k}' both map to ID={iv}\n"
                "Each risk factor must have a unique risk_factor_name_id."
            )
        seen[iv] = k


def _require_mapping_key(
    m: Mapping[str, Any],
    key: str,
    *,
    preview_n: int = 25,
) -> Any:
    """Lookup `key` in mapping `m` and raise a detailed error if missing.

    This helper provides user-friendly diagnostics on configuration errors by
    showing a short preview of known keys and a suggested fix. Designed to be
    inexpensive on the success path (single hash lookup); the preview list is
    materialized only on error.

    Parameters
    ----------
    m : Mapping[str, Any]
        Mapping from risk factor name to id.
    key : str
        Key to lookup (column label converted to string prior to lookup).
    preview_n : int
        Maximum number of known keys to show in the error message.
    """
    try:
        return m[key]
    except KeyError:
        # Materialize a small preview of keys only when diagnosing a failure --
        # prevents huge error messages for very large config maps.
        keys = list(m.keys())
        n = len(keys)
        preview = keys[:preview_n]
        preview_str = ", ".join(preview) + (" ..." if n > preview_n else "")

        raise ValueError(
            "Risk factor ID mapping lookup failed.\n"
            f"Missing key: '{key}'\n\n"
            "Fix:\n"
            "  Add this risk factor to the STATIC config table that defines risk_factor_name_id.\n"
            "  Example row to add:\n"
            f"    risk_factor_name = {key}\n"
            "    risk_factor_name_id = <new unique integer>\n\n"
            f"Known keys preview ({min(preview_n, n)} of {n}):\n"
            f"  {preview_str}\n"
        ) from None
```
