<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\market_data\risk_factors\compile_snapshot.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/market_data/risk_factors/compile_snapshot.py
"""
Compile a flat `(rel_name, level)` levels dictionary into a typed
`RiskFactorLevelSnapshot`, with unit-scale conversion applied.

Public API mirror — analogous to `compile_risk_static_config_from_dict_of_dfs`:

  compile_risk_static_config_from_dict_of_dfs(dict_of_dfs) -> RiskStaticConfig
  compile_risk_factor_level_snapshot_from_levels_dict(
      *, as_of_date, levels_dict, static_config
  ) -> RiskFactorLevelSnapshot

Contract
--------
- Every key in `levels_dict` is a relative risk-factor name (e.g.
  "USD_SOFR_OIS[1Y]", "BRENT_CRUDE_VOL_SURF[M+0][50.00]") that
  `static_config.rel_name_to_rf_id` resolves.
- Each value is the level *in the unit_scale declared by the static
  config for that rf*. This compiler applies the per-rf
  `unit_scale_factor` so the emitted `factor_levels` are always in
  base DECIMAL — e.g. PERCENT inputs (5.30) become decimal (0.0530),
  and BP inputs (50.0) become decimal (0.0050).
- The resolved `factor_ids` array is sorted strictly ascending and
  C-contiguous; `RiskFactorLevelSnapshot.__post_init__` enforces this
  invariant downstream.

Why a separate compiler rather than reusing `build_*_from_dict`?
----------------------------------------------------------------
The existing `build_risk_factor_level_snapshot_from_dict` is layout-
agnostic and unit-agnostic — fine for already-canonical numeric inputs.
The compiler here adds two pieces the canonical fixture needs:
  1. Name resolution via the static config (rel_name -> rf_id).
  2. Unit-scale promotion to base DECIMAL.

Both belong to the ingest perimeter, not the hot-path snapshot
container, so they live here in a dedicated compile step.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Mapping
from datetime import date

import numpy as np

from riskquant.config.static_config.static_config import RiskStaticConfig
from riskquant.market_data.risk_factors.snapshot import RiskFactorLevelSnapshot


def compile_risk_factor_level_snapshot_from_levels_dict(
    *,
    as_of_date: date,
    levels_dict: Mapping[str, float],
    static_config: RiskStaticConfig,
    float_dtype: type[np.floating] = np.float64,
) -> RiskFactorLevelSnapshot:
    """
    Resolve relative-name -> rf_id, apply unit_scale, and return a snapshot.

    Parameters
    ----------
    as_of_date
        Calendar date the snapshot applies to. Pure metadata on the
        returned object — no arithmetic uses it.
    levels_dict
        Flat mapping of relative-name -> level. Levels are in each rf's
        declared `unit_scale` (PERCENT for IR, DECIMAL for prices /
        FX / vols, BP if anything declares it).
    static_config
        Compiled static config. Source of truth for the rel-name -> rf_id
        index and the per-rf unit_scale_factor.
    float_dtype
        Output dtype for `factor_levels`. Default `float64` to preserve
        precision through the unit-scale multiplication; downstream
        scenario buffers narrow to `float32` at their own perimeter.

    Returns
    -------
    RiskFactorLevelSnapshot
        `factor_ids` int32 sorted-ascending unique; `factor_levels`
        in base DECIMAL, parallel to `factor_ids`.

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError
        Empty `levels_dict`.
    KeyError
        A relative name in `levels_dict` does not resolve to any rf_id
        in the static config. The error names the offending key.
    """
    if not levels_dict:
        raise ValueError(
            "levels_dict cannot be empty; the snapshot must reference at "
            "least one rf_id."
        )

    n = len(levels_dict)
    rel_to_rf_id = static_config.rel_name_to_rf_id
    rf_id_to_unit_scale = static_config.rf_id_to_unit_scale_factor

    # Single-pass resolution: name -> rf_id + per-rf scale -> base level.
    # Pre-allocated int32 / float64 buffers; no Python list intermediates
    # past the dict iteration itself.
    ids_buf = np.empty(n, dtype=np.int32)
    levels_buf = np.empty(n, dtype=float_dtype)

    for i, (rel_name, raw_level) in enumerate(levels_dict.items()):
        rf_id = rel_to_rf_id.get(rel_name)
        if rf_id is None:
            raise KeyError(
                f"rel_name {rel_name!r} did not resolve through "
                f"static_config.rel_name_to_rf_id. The snapshot fixture is "
                f"out of sync with the static config."
            )
        ids_buf[i] = np.int32(rf_id)
        # rf_id_to_unit_scale_factor is keyed by rf_id (numpy array indexed
        # by id). float()-wrap because the result will be cast to
        # float_dtype anyway and we want native multiplication.
        levels_buf[i] = float_dtype(raw_level) * float_dtype(
            rf_id_to_unit_scale[int(rf_id)]
        )

    # The snapshot constructor enforces sorted-unique + contiguous; pass
    # the buffers in directly. If callers fed us duplicate rel-names that
    # map to the same rf_id, the snapshot constructor will raise.
    return RiskFactorLevelSnapshot(
        as_of_date=as_of_date,
        factor_ids=ids_buf,
        factor_levels=levels_buf,
    )
```
</file>

```python
# riskquant/market_data/risk_factors/matrix.py
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import date

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

from riskquant.market_data.risk_factors.snapshot import RiskFactorLevelSnapshot
from riskquant.utils.dates import ordinal32_to_datetime64D


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class RiskFactorMatrix:
    """
    Canonical RiskFactorMatrix with God-Tier memory layout for time-series operations.

    Invariants:
      - dates: 1D, sorted ascending, unique (int32 ordinal or datetime64[D])
      - factor_ids: 1D int32, sorted ascending, unique
      - values: 2D float32/64, shape (T, N), F-contiguous by default for column-major access
      - len(dates)=T, len(factor_ids)=N
    """

    # datetime64[D] or int32 ordinal
    dates: np.ndarray

    # int32
    factor_ids: np.ndarray

    # float32/64, shape (T, N) --> (observations, factors)
    values: np.ndarray

    # Default memory layout (F-contiguous for time-series)
    fortran_order: bool = True

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        # ---- Basic checks ----
        t_obs, n_factors = self.values.shape
        if self.dates.ndim != 1:
            raise ValueError("dates must be 1D")
        if self.factor_ids.ndim != 1:
            raise ValueError("factor_ids must be 1D")
        if self.values.ndim != 2:
            raise ValueError("values must be 2D")

        # ---- dtype checks ----
        if not np.issubdtype(self.values.dtype, np.floating):
            raise TypeError(f"values must be float dtype, got {self.values.dtype}")

        if np.issubdtype(self.dates.dtype, np.integer):
            if self.dates.dtype != np.int32:
                raise TypeError(f"int dates must be int32, got {self.dates.dtype}")
        elif self.dates.dtype != np.dtype("datetime64[D]"):
            raise TypeError(
                f"datetime dates must be datetime64[D], got {self.dates.dtype}"
            )

        if self.factor_ids.dtype != np.int32:
            raise TypeError(f"factor_ids must be int32, got {self.factor_ids.dtype}")

        # ---- shape checks ----
        if self.dates.size != t_obs:
            raise ValueError(
                f"len(dates) ({self.dates.size}) != values.shape[0] ({t_obs})"
            )
        if self.factor_ids.size != n_factors:
            raise ValueError(
                f"len(factor_ids) ({self.factor_ids.size}) != values.shape[1] ({n_factors})"
            )

        # ---- memory layout ----
        if self.fortran_order:
            if not self.values.flags["F_CONTIGUOUS"]:
                vals = np.asfortranarray(self.values)
                object.__setattr__(self, "values", vals)
        else:
            if not self.values.flags["C_CONTIGUOUS"]:
                vals = np.ascontiguousarray(self.values)
                object.__setattr__(self, "values", vals)

        # ---- dates sorted + unique ----
        if t_obs > 1:
            if np.any(self.dates[1:] < self.dates[:-1]):
                raise ValueError("dates must be sorted ascending")
            if np.any(self.dates[1:] == self.dates[:-1]):
                raise ValueError("dates must be unique")

        # ---- factor_ids sorted + unique ----
        if n_factors > 1 and np.any(self.factor_ids[1:] < self.factor_ids[:-1]):
            perm = np.argsort(self.factor_ids, kind="mergesort")
            sorted_ids = self.factor_ids[perm]
            if np.any(sorted_ids[1:] == sorted_ids[:-1]):
                raise ValueError("factor_ids must be unique")
            sorted_vals = (
                np.asfortranarray(self.values[:, perm])
                if self.fortran_order
                else np.ascontiguousarray(self.values[:, perm])
            )
            object.__setattr__(self, "factor_ids", sorted_ids)
            object.__setattr__(self, "values", sorted_vals)

    # ---------------------------- Convenience Methods ----------------------------
    def to_df(self) -> pd.DataFrame:
        index = pd.DatetimeIndex(
            ordinal32_to_datetime64D(self.dates)
            if np.issubdtype(self.dates.dtype, np.integer)
            else self.dates
        )
        df = pd.DataFrame(data=self.values, index=index, columns=self.factor_ids)
        df.index.name = "date"
        df.columns.name = "factor_id"
        return df

    def head(self, n: int = 5) -> pd.DataFrame:
        return self.to_df().head(n=n)

    def tail(self, n: int = 5) -> pd.DataFrame:
        return self.to_df().tail(n=n)

    def plot(self, **kwargs):
        return self.to_df().plot(**kwargs)

    # ---------------------------- Properties ----------------------------
    @property
    def index_dates(self) -> pd.DatetimeIndex:
        return pd.DatetimeIndex(
            ordinal32_to_datetime64D(self.dates)
            if np.issubdtype(self.dates.dtype, np.integer)
            else self.dates
        )

    @property
    def index_factors(self) -> pd.Index:
        return pd.Index(self.factor_ids, name="factor_id")

    @property
    def df(self) -> pd.DataFrame:
        return self.to_df()

    @property
    def shape(self) -> tuple[int, ...]:
        return self.values.shape

    @property
    def num_dates(self) -> int:
        return self.values.shape[0]

    @property
    def num_factors(self) -> int:
        return self.values.shape[1]

    # ---------------------------- Slicing Methods ----------------------------
    def slice_dates(
        self, start: int | None = None, end: int | None = None
    ) -> RiskFactorMatrix:
        start = 0 if start is None else start
        end = self.num_dates if end is None else end
        vals = self.values[start:end, :]
        if self.fortran_order and not vals.flags["F_CONTIGUOUS"]:
            vals = np.asfortranarray(vals)
        elif not self.fortran_order and not vals.flags["C_CONTIGUOUS"]:
            vals = np.ascontiguousarray(vals)
        return RiskFactorMatrix(
            dates=self.dates[start:end],
            factor_ids=self.factor_ids,
            values=vals,
            fortran_order=self.fortran_order,
        )

    def slice_factors(self, factor_indices: np.ndarray) -> RiskFactorMatrix:
        vals = self.values[:, factor_indices]
        if self.fortran_order and not vals.flags["F_CONTIGUOUS"]:
            vals = np.asfortranarray(vals)
        elif not self.fortran_order and not vals.flags["C_CONTIGUOUS"]:
            vals = np.ascontiguousarray(vals)
        return RiskFactorMatrix(
            dates=self.dates,
            factor_ids=self.factor_ids[factor_indices],
            values=vals,
            fortran_order=self.fortran_order,
        )

    # ---------------------------- Time-Series Optimization ----------------------------
    def to_time_series_optimized_array(self) -> np.ndarray:
        """Return F-contiguous array for column-major operations (Numba/Cython)."""
        return np.asfortranarray(self.values) if not self.fortran_order else self.values

    def get_factor_time_series(self, factor_id: int) -> np.ndarray:
        idx = np.searchsorted(self.factor_ids, factor_id)
        if idx >= self.factor_ids.size or self.factor_ids[idx] != factor_id:
            raise KeyError(f"factor_id {factor_id} not found")
        return np.ascontiguousarray(self.values[:, idx])

    # ---------------------------- Snapshot ----------------------------
    def get_snapshot(self, target_date: date) -> RiskFactorLevelSnapshot:
        from riskquant.utils.dates import date_to_ord32

        target_val = (
            date_to_ord32(target_date)
            if np.issubdtype(self.dates.dtype, np.integer)
            else np.datetime64(target_date, "D")
        )
        idx = np.searchsorted(self.dates, target_val)
        if idx >= self.dates.size or self.dates[idx] != target_val:
            raise KeyError(f"Date {target_date} not found in matrix")

        levels = self.values[idx, :]
        valid_mask = ~np.isnan(levels)
        return RiskFactorLevelSnapshot(
            as_of_date=target_date,
            factor_ids=self.factor_ids[valid_mask],
            factor_levels=levels[valid_mask],
        )

    @classmethod
    def from_snapshot(
        cls,
        snapshot: RiskFactorLevelSnapshot,
        *,
        fortran_order: bool = True,
    ) -> RiskFactorMatrix:
        """
        Elevate this snapshot into a 1-row RiskFactorMatrix.

        S-Tier mechanics:
        - factor_ids: Passed by reference (zero-copy).
        - values: Reshaped via np.newaxis (zero-copy view).
        - dates: Minimal 1-element array allocation aligned to datetime64[D].
        """

        # 1. Align dates invariant: 1D array of datetime64[D]
        dates_arr = np.array([snapshot.as_of_date], dtype="datetime64[D]")

        # 2. Align values invariant: 2D array (T, N) -> (1, N)
        # Using np.newaxis creates a fast, zero-copy view of the 1D C-contiguous array.
        values_2d = snapshot.factor_levels[np.newaxis, :]

        return RiskFactorMatrix(
            dates=dates_arr,
            factor_ids=snapshot.factor_ids,  # Already validated, sorted, and unique
            values=values_2d,
            fortran_order=fortran_order,
        )
```
</file>

```python
# riskquant/market_data/risk_factors/shift_types.py
from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Mapping
from enum import IntEnum

import numpy as np


class ShiftType(IntEnum):
    NO_SHIFT = -1
    LOG_RETURN = 1
    SIMPLE_RETURN = 2
    DIFFERENCE = 3


# Kernel-friendly int8 constants (avoid repeated casts in hot code)
SHIFT_NO_SHIFT_ID: np.int8 = np.int8(ShiftType.NO_SHIFT)
SHIFT_LOG_RETURN_ID: np.int8 = np.int8(ShiftType.LOG_RETURN)
SHIFT_SIMPLE_RETURN_ID: np.int8 = np.int8(ShiftType.SIMPLE_RETURN)
SHIFT_DIFFERENCE_ID: np.int8 = np.int8(ShiftType.DIFFERENCE)


# Names tuple
SHIFT_TYPE_NAMES: tuple[str, ...] = tuple(method.name.upper() for method in ShiftType)

# Registry
SHIFT_TYPE_NAME_TO_ID_REGISTRY: dict[str, np.int8] = {
    "NO_SHIFT": SHIFT_NO_SHIFT_ID,
    "LOG_RETURN": SHIFT_LOG_RETURN_ID,
    "SIMPLE_RETURN": SHIFT_SIMPLE_RETURN_ID,
    "DIFFERENCE": SHIFT_DIFFERENCE_ID,
}


def validate_shift_types_dict(shift_types_dict: Mapping[str, int]) -> None:
    """
    Validate that the Excel-provided mapping exactly matches the code enum.

    Rationale:
      - Code is the contract; Excel is data.
      - Kernels rely on stable integer codes.
    """
    # Expected mapping from enum
    expected: dict[str, int] = {
        name: int(member) for name, member in ShiftType.__members__.items()
    }

    # Normalize "got" mapping to plain ints for stable comparison
    got: dict[str, int] = {str(k): int(v) for k, v in shift_types_dict.items()}

    if got == expected:
        return

    missing = {k: expected[k] for k in expected.keys() - got.keys()}
    extra = {k: got[k] for k in got.keys() - expected.keys()}
    different = {
        k: {"expected": expected[k], "got": got[k]}
        for k in (expected.keys() & got.keys())
        if expected[k] != got[k]
    }

    raise ValueError(
        "shift_types sheet does not match riskquant ShiftType enum.\n"
        f"missing={missing}\n"
        f"extra={extra}\n"
        f"different={different}\n"
        "Fix the Excel shift_types table or update riskquant.market_data.risk_factors.shift_types.ShiftType."
    )
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\market_data\risk_factors\snapshot.py">
```python
# riskquant/market_data/risk_factors/snapshot.py

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import date

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class RiskFactorLevelSnapshot:
    """Immutable snapshot of risk factor levels at a single as-of date.

    Attributes
    ----------
    as_of_date : datetime.date
        The calendar date for which the snapshot applies.
    factor_ids : np.ndarray[int32]
        1-D array of unique risk-factor identifiers (dtype int32, C-contiguous).
        Must be sorted ascending; if not sorted, the constructor will re-order
        the ids and corresponding `factor_levels`.
    factor_levels : np.ndarray[float32|float64]
        1-D numeric array of factor levels aligned with `factor_ids`.
        Must be the same length as `factor_ids` and C-contiguous.

    Invariants (enforced on construction)
    -------------------------------------
    - `factor_ids` is 1D, dtype int32, C-contiguous, sorted ascending and unique
    - `factor_levels` is 1D, dtype float32 or float64, C-contiguous
    - lengths of `factor_ids` and `factor_levels` match

    The class is intentionally lightweight and validates eagerly so downstream
    numerical kernels can assume compact, contiguous, and deterministic
    memory layouts (fast gathers, stable hashing, etc.).
    """

    as_of_date: date
    factor_ids: np.ndarray  # (N0,) int32
    factor_levels: np.ndarray  # (N0,) float32/float64

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        """Validate and canonicalize snapshot arrays.

        The method performs cheap, early checks and performs the minimal amount
        of work required to restore canonical invariants (sorting + contiguity).
        It raises clear exceptions on invalid input rather than attempting to
        silently coerce data into a valid form.
        """
        # Local aliases to make subsequent checks concise and explicit.
        ids = self.factor_ids
        lv = self.factor_levels

        # ----- shape checks -----
        # Ensure both arrays are 1-D vectors.
        if ids.ndim != 1:
            raise ValueError(f"factor_ids must be 1D; got shape={ids.shape}")
        if lv.ndim != 1:
            raise ValueError(f"factor_levels must be 1D; got shape={lv.shape}")

        # Ensure arrays have the same length (alignment requirement).
        if ids.shape[0] != lv.shape[0]:
            raise ValueError(
                f"factor_ids and factor_levels length mismatch: "
                f"{ids.shape[0]} vs {lv.shape[0]}"
            )

        # ----- dtype checks -----
        # factor_ids must be 32-bit integers for compactness and predictable
        # behavior when used as indices or keys in mappings.
        if ids.dtype != np.int32:
            raise TypeError(f"factor_ids must be int32; got {ids.dtype}")

        # factor_levels must be floating point (single or double precision).
        if lv.dtype not in (np.float32, np.float64):
            raise TypeError(f"factor_levels must be float32 or float64; got {lv.dtype}")

        # ----- memory layout checks -----
        # Many numerical routines assume C-contiguous 1-D arrays; check early so
        # callers get an explicit error instead of subtle performance bugs.
        if not ids.flags["C_CONTIGUOUS"]:
            raise ValueError("factor_ids must be C-contiguous")
        if not lv.flags["C_CONTIGUOUS"]:
            raise ValueError("factor_levels must be C-contiguous")

        # Fast path: zero or one element requires no further sorting/uniqueness checks.
        n = ids.size
        if n <= 1:
            return

        # ----- sorted + unique enforcement -----
        # If ids are already non-decreasing, perform an adjacent-duplicate check
        # which is allocation-free. If not sorted, build a stable permutation and
        # reorder both arrays so the object is canonical afterwards.
        if not np.any(ids[1:] < ids[:-1]):
            # ids are non-decreasing — ensure strict uniqueness.
            if np.any(ids[1:] == ids[:-1]):
                raise ValueError("factor_ids must be unique")
            return

        # ids are unsorted: compute a stable sort permutation (mergesort).
        perm = np.argsort(ids, kind="mergesort")

        # Apply the permutation to ids (produces a new array view/copy).
        ids_sorted = ids[perm]

        # Check for duplicates after sorting (adjacent duplicates are easy to detect).
        if np.any(ids_sorted[1:] == ids_sorted[:-1]):
            raise ValueError("factor_ids must be unique")

        # Reorder factor levels to keep alignment with the now-sorted ids.
        lv_sorted = lv[perm]

        # Fancy indexing typically returns a contiguous array for 1-D, but
        # explicitly enforce C-contiguity to preserve the class invariant.
        if not lv_sorted.flags["C_CONTIGUOUS"]:
            lv_sorted = np.ascontiguousarray(lv_sorted)

        # Bypass dataclass immutability to install the canonicalized arrays.
        object.__setattr__(self, "factor_ids", ids_sorted)
        object.__setattr__(self, "factor_levels", lv_sorted)

    def to_df(self) -> pd.DataFrame:
        """Return the snapshot as a 1-row wide DataFrame."""
        # Reshape the 1D levels array into a 2D row vector (1, N)
        data_2d = self.factor_levels[np.newaxis, :]

        df = pd.DataFrame(
            data=data_2d,
            index=pd.DatetimeIndex([self.as_of_date], name="date"),
            columns=pd.Index(self.factor_ids, name="factor_id"),
        )
        return df

    def to_series(self) -> pd.Series:
        """Return the snapshot as a pandas Series."""
        return pd.Series(
            data=self.factor_levels,
            index=pd.Index(self.factor_ids, name="factor_id"),
            name=self.as_of_date,
        )

    @property
    def df(self) -> pd.DataFrame:
        """Alias for to_df()."""
        return self.to_df()

    @property
    def sr(self) -> pd.Series:
        """Alias for to_series()."""
        return self.to_series()

    @property
    def num_factors(self) -> int:
        return len(self.factor_ids)

    def summary(self) -> None:
        """Print a console-friendly overview of the snapshot."""
        lines = [
            "=======================================",
            "=== RiskFactorLevelSnapshot Summary ===",
            "=======================================",
            f"  As of date          : {self.as_of_date}",
            f"  Number of factors   : {self.num_factors}",
            f"  Factor ID range     : {self.factor_ids.min()} to {self.factor_ids.max()}",
            f"  Factor level range  : {self.factor_levels.min()} to {self.factor_levels.max()}",
            "=======================================",
        ]
        print("\n".join(lines))
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\market_data\state.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/market_data/state.py
"""
Vectorized market data container for the valuation engine.

`MarketDataMatrix` is the frozen, valuation-ready SoA representation of all
market data required to price a `CompiledPortfolio` across `num_scenarios`
parallel scenarios.

Axis conventions
----------------
* Dimension 0 always corresponds to a `_uix` (price / fx_effective / vol /
  discount universe).
* For 2-D scalar grids the final dimension is the scenario index.
* 3-D grids (`discount_factor_grid`, `price_curve_grid`) carry a middle
  date-uix axis aligned with `CompiledPortfolio.unique_dates_ord32`, and
  scenario in the trailing axis.
* `vol_surfaces` is 4-D and arranged as `[surface, expiry, scenario, pillar]`
  so that the strike-axis pillars for one trade-scenario are a single
  contiguous run of memory — see Vol-surface layout below.

FX semantics
------------
`fx_spots_effective` holds the deduplicated, fully-resolved FX transform
matrix. Row `k` is the spot transform identified by row `k` of the
portfolio's `UixMapper.fx_effective_pairs`. Trades that need no FX carry
`INVALID_UIX` (< 0) in their per-trade `fx_*_effective_uix` field and skip
the FX gather entirely via a hoisted branch in the pricer.

Pricer contract for FX:
    out[i, s] *= fx_spots_effective[fx_effective_uix[i], s]

No inversion math, no `1.0 / rate`, no scenario-level branches. The raw
`fx_spots` matrix is producer-side only and never reaches the pricers.

Vol-surface layout (CRITICAL)
-----------------------------
`vol_surfaces` is `(num_vol_surfaces, num_unique_dates, num_scenarios,
max_vol_strikes)` — pillar in the trailing axis. This guarantees that
the strike pillars for one (surface, expiry, scenario) tuple form one
C-contiguous run, which is exactly what the strike-axis interpolation
in the option-curve kernel needs for SIMD-friendly reads.

The market-data builder is responsible for emitting this layout; the
kernels rely on it for `v_plane[s]` to be a contiguous 1-D pillar row.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass, field

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class MarketDataMatrix:
    """Frozen, validated SoA container of market data for vectorized valuation."""

    num_scenarios: int

    # ========== 1. SCALARS / NODES ==========

    # (num_fx_effective_pairs, num_scenarios) — deduplicated, pre-resolved FX
    # transforms. Pricers gather one row per leg per trade and multiply.
    fx_spots_effective: npt.NDArray[np.float64]

    # (num_price_factors, num_scenarios)
    prices: npt.NDArray[np.float64]

    # (num_fwd_nodes, num_scenarios)
    forward_nodes: npt.NDArray[np.float64] = field(
        default_factory=lambda: np.empty((0, 0), dtype=np.float64),
    )

    # (num_vol_nodes, num_scenarios)
    vol_nodes: npt.NDArray[np.float64] = field(
        default_factory=lambda: np.empty((0, 0), dtype=np.float64),
    )

    # ========== 2. DISCOUNT GRID ==========
    # (num_discount_curves, num_unique_dates, num_scenarios)
    discount_factor_grid: npt.NDArray[np.float64] = field(
        default_factory=lambda: np.empty((0, 0, 0), dtype=np.float64),
    )

    # ========== 3. CURVE-BASED DATA ==========
    # (num_price_curves, num_unique_dates, num_scenarios)
    price_curve_grid: npt.NDArray[np.float64] = field(
        default_factory=lambda: np.empty((0, 0, 0), dtype=np.float64),
    )

    # ========== 4. TIME GRIDS ==========
    # (num_unique_dates,) — year fractions from as_of to each unique date.
    tte_grid: npt.NDArray[np.float64] = field(
        default_factory=lambda: np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64),
    )

    sqrt_tte_grid: npt.NDArray[np.float64] = field(init=False)

    half_tte_grid: npt.NDArray[np.float64] = field(init=False)

    # ========== 5. SURFACES ==========
    # (num_vol_surfaces, max_vol_strikes) — strike pillars per surface.
    vol_surface_strikes: npt.NDArray[np.float64] = field(
        default_factory=lambda: np.empty((0, 0), dtype=np.float64),
    )

    # (num_vol_surfaces, num_unique_dates, num_scenarios, max_vol_strikes)
    # Pillar-in-trailing-axis layout — see module docstring.
    vol_surfaces: npt.NDArray[np.float64] = field(
        default_factory=lambda: np.empty((0, 0, 0, 0), dtype=np.float64),
    )

    def __post_init__(self):

        object.__setattr__(
            self,
            "sqrt_tte_grid",
            np.sqrt(self.tte_grid),
        )

        object.__setattr__(
            self,
            "half_tte_grid",
            0.5 * self.tte_grid,
        )
```
</file>

```python
from enum import IntEnum

import numpy as np


class SurfaceYAxisType(IntEnum):
    STRIKE = 1
    LOG_STRIKE = 2
    MONEYNESS_SPOT = 3
    MONEYNESS_FWD = 4
    LOG_MONEYNESS_SPOT = 5
    LOG_MONEYNESS_FWD = 6
    DELTA_CALL = 7


# compiler-friendly int8 constants
SURFACE_Y_AXIS_TYPE_STRIKE: np.int8 = np.int8(
    SurfaceYAxisType.STRIKE,
)
SURFACE_Y_AXIS_TYPE_LOG_STRIKE: np.int8 = np.int8(
    SurfaceYAxisType.LOG_STRIKE,
)
SURFACE_Y_AXIS_TYPE_MONEYNESS_SPOT: np.int8 = np.int8(
    SurfaceYAxisType.MONEYNESS_SPOT,
)
SURFACE_Y_AXIS_TYPE_MONEYNESS_FWD: np.int8 = np.int8(
    SurfaceYAxisType.MONEYNESS_FWD,
)
SURFACE_Y_AXIS_TYPE_LOG_MONEYNESS_SPOT: np.int8 = np.int8(
    SurfaceYAxisType.LOG_MONEYNESS_SPOT,
)
SURFACE_Y_AXIS_TYPE_LOG_MONEYNESS_FWD: np.int8 = np.int8(
    SurfaceYAxisType.LOG_MONEYNESS_FWD,
)
SURFACE_Y_AXIS_TYPE_DELTA_CALL: np.int8 = np.int8(
    SurfaceYAxisType.DELTA_CALL,
)

# Names tuple
SURFACE_Y_AXIS_TYPE_NAMES: tuple[str, ...] = tuple(
    method.name.upper() for method in SurfaceYAxisType
)

# Central registry
SURFACE_Y_AXIS_TYPE_REGISTRY: dict[str, SurfaceYAxisType] = {
    "STRIKE": SurfaceYAxisType.STRIKE,
    "LOG_STRIKE": SurfaceYAxisType.LOG_STRIKE,
    "MONEYNESS_SPOT": SurfaceYAxisType.MONEYNESS_SPOT,
    "MONEYNESS_FWD": SurfaceYAxisType.MONEYNESS_FWD,
    "LOG_MONEYNESS_SPOT": SurfaceYAxisType.LOG_MONEYNESS_SPOT,
    "LOG_MONEYNESS_FWD": SurfaceYAxisType.LOG_MONEYNESS_FWD,
    "DELTA_CALL": SurfaceYAxisType.DELTA_CALL,
}
```

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\portfolio\blocks\base_block.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/portfolio/blocks/base_block.py

"""
Base class for all Struct-of-Arrays (SoA) trade blocks.

`Block` carries only what is universal to every compiled block: the flat int64
`trade_uids` array and the `INSTRUMENT_ID` dispatch tag. All economic SoA arrays
live on the concrete subclasses.

`INSTRUMENT_ID` is the single integer the valuation engine dispatches on: it
indexes directly into `BLOCK_EVALUATORS`, so no `type()` calls, no isinstance
ladders, no string keys. The base value is the `_UNSET` sentinel; every
concrete block MUST override it with its `Instrument` enum id. The override is
enforced at class-creation time by `__init_subclass__`, which fails loudly the
moment a new block type is added without wiring its id — a compile-time guard
rather than a runtime surprise.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass, fields
from typing import Any, ClassVar

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

# Sentinel for "concrete subclass forgot to set INSTRUMENT_ID". Negative so it
# can never collide with a real Instrument enum id or index a dispatch table.
_UNSET_INSTRUMENT_ID: int = -999


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class Block:
    """Universal base for compiled SoA trade blocks."""

    # Dispatch tag. ClassVar => not a dataclass field, zero per-instance cost.
    INSTRUMENT_ID: ClassVar[int] = _UNSET_INSTRUMENT_ID

    trade_uids: npt.NDArray[np.int64]

    def __init_subclass__(cls, **kwargs: object) -> None:
        """
        Enforce the dispatch contract for *concrete* block subclasses.

        Intermediate abstract bases (names starting with '_') are exempt: they
        legitimately leave `INSTRUMENT_ID` unset for their own subclasses to
        define. Any concrete block that ships without an id is a wiring bug and
        is rejected here, at import time.
        """
        # super().__init_subclass__(**kwargs)
        is_abstract_base = cls.__name__.startswith("_")
        if not is_abstract_base and cls.INSTRUMENT_ID == _UNSET_INSTRUMENT_ID:
            raise TypeError(
                f"Block subclass '{cls.__name__}' must set INSTRUMENT_ID "
                f"(its riskquant.portfolio.instruments id) for engine dispatch."
            )

    @property
    def num_trades(self) -> int:
        """Number of trades packed into this block."""
        return self.trade_uids.shape[0]

    def print_block(
        self,
        *,
        max_items: int | None = 10,
        float_precision: int = 4,
        sep: str = ", ",
        pad: int = 15,  # Increased default pad slightly to account for the new '_' characters
        thousand_sep: str = "_",
    ) -> None:
        """Print every SoA field with shape, dtype, and a value preview."""

        print(f"{type(self).__name__}:")
        for f in fields(self):
            v = getattr(self, f.name)
            if isinstance(v, np.ndarray):
                # Replace lambdas with typed inner functions to satisfy Pyright/MyPy
                def fmt_float(x: Any) -> str:
                    return f"{x:>{pad}{thousand_sep}.{float_precision}f}"

                def fmt_int(x: Any) -> str:
                    # Use 'd' (decimal integer) instead of a hacked float format
                    return f"{x:>{pad}{thousand_sep}d}"

                # Format the array preview cleanly
                val_preview = np.array2string(
                    v[:max_items],
                    separator=sep,
                    max_line_width=100_000,
                    formatter={
                        "float_kind": fmt_float,
                        "int_kind": fmt_int,
                    },
                )

                print(
                    f"  {f.name:<33} | shape: {v.shape!s:>10} | "
                    f"dtype: {v.dtype!s:>10} | values: {val_preview}"
                )
            else:
                print(f"  {f.name:<33} {v}")
        print()
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\portfolio\blocks\spot_block.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/portfolio/blocks/spot_block.py
"""
Compiled, memory-efficient container for spot positions.

`SpotBlock` is the struct-of-arrays representation of a set of spot trades.
The block is intentionally compact (NumPy arrays, primitive numeric dtypes)
so it can be held efficiently in memory and passed between processes in
high-throughput valuation workflows.

Architectural contract
----------------------
* Row `i` of every array corresponds to the trade with UID `trade_uids[i]`.
* Every `_uix` field is a zero-based, contiguous index produced by the
  portfolio engine's `UixMapper` — NOT a global static-config ID. The block
  carries execution coordinates only.
* `fx_spots_effective_uix` indexes into `MarketDataMatrix.fx_spots_effective`,
  the deduplicated effective FX-transform matrix. No invert flag is carried;
  the inversion (if any) has been folded into the effective matrix at the
  builder edge. Pricer kernels do one unconditional gather + multiply.
* `INVALID_UIX (< 0)` in any `_uix` field signals "no factor required for
  this trade" and is the only branch the pricer kernel performs (hoisted
  outside the scenario loop).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import ClassVar

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.portfolio.blocks.base_block import Block
from riskquant.portfolio.instruments import SPOT_ID


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class SpotBlock(Block):
    """Struct-of-arrays for compiled spot trades."""

    INSTRUMENT_ID: ClassVar[int] = int(SPOT_ID)

    # ========== TRADE IDENTITY ==========
    # Unique trade identifiers, one per row.
    trade_uids: npt.NDArray[np.int64]

    # ========== ECONOMIC TERMS ==========
    # Signed quantities / notionals for the spot positions.
    qty: npt.NDArray[np.float64]

    # ========== MARKET LINKAGES (EXECUTION UIX) ==========
    # Index into `MarketDataMatrix.prices` for this row.
    price_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32]

    # Index into `MarketDataMatrix.fx_spots_effective`, or INVALID_UIX
    # (< 0) when no FX conversion is required for this trade.
    fx_spots_effective_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32]
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\portfolio\blocks\vanilla_european_option_block.py">

```python
# src/riskquant/portfolio/blocks/vanilla_european_option_block.py
"""
Compiled blocks for vanilla European option trades.

Architectural contract
----------------------
* Row `i` of every array corresponds to the trade with UID `trade_uids[i]`.
* Every `_uix` field is a zero-based, contiguous execution index produced by
  the portfolio engine's `UixMapper` — NOT a global static-config ID.
* FX legs are carried as `fx_*_to_settle_effective_uix` indexing
  `MarketDataMatrix.fx_spots_effective`. No invert flags survive into the
  block layer.
* `cp_mult` packs `is_call` into `+1` / `-1` for branchless Black-76 payoff.
* `INVALID_UIX (< 0)` in any optional `_uix` field signals "no factor
  required for this trade" and is the only branch the pricer kernel
  performs (hoisted outside the scenario loop).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import ClassVar

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.portfolio.blocks.base_block import Block
from riskquant.portfolio.instruments import (
    VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_CURVE_ID,
    VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_NODE_ID,
)


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class _VanillaEuropeanOptionBaseBlock(Block):
    """Common SoA fields shared by all vanilla European option blocks."""

    # ========== TRADE IDENTITY ==========
    trade_uids: npt.NDArray[np.int64]

    # ========== ECONOMIC TERMS ==========
    qty: npt.NDArray[np.float64]
    strike: npt.NDArray[np.float64]
    # +1 for call, -1 for put, precomputed for branchless payoff sign.
    cp_mult: npt.NDArray[np.int8]

    # ========== DATES (DATE UIX) ==========
    # Indices into `unique_dates_ord32`.
    expiry_date_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32]
    settlement_date_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32]

    # ========== FX LINKAGES (EFFECTIVE UIX) ==========
    # Indices into `MarketDataMatrix.fx_spots_effective`. INVALID_UIX (< 0)
    # signals "no FX conversion required" for that leg.
    fx_under_to_settle_effective_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32]
    fx_strike_to_settle_effective_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32]

    # ========== DISCOUNT LINKAGES (CURVE UIX) ==========
    # Indices into `MarketDataMatrix.discount_factor_grid` axis 0.
    # INVALID_UIX (< 0) when no discount applies on that leg.
    under_discount_curve_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32]
    strike_discount_curve_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32]


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class VanillaEuropeanOptionNodeBlock(_VanillaEuropeanOptionBaseBlock):
    """European option whose underlying is sourced from a scalar node."""

    INSTRUMENT_ID: ClassVar[int] = int(VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_NODE_ID)

    # ========== MARKET LINKAGES ==========
    # Index into `MarketDataMatrix.forward_nodes`.
    underlying_price_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32]
    # Index into `MarketDataMatrix.vol_nodes`.
    vol_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32]


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class VanillaEuropeanOptionCurveBlock(_VanillaEuropeanOptionBaseBlock):
    """European option whose underlying and vol come from curves/surfaces."""

    INSTRUMENT_ID: ClassVar[int] = int(VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_CURVE_ID)

    # ========== DELIVERY DATE (DATE UIX) ==========
    delivery_date_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32]

    # ========== MARKET LINKAGES ==========
    # Index into `MarketDataMatrix.price_curve_grid` axis 0.
    underlying_price_curve_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32]
    # Index into `MarketDataMatrix.vol_surfaces` axis 0.
    vol_surface_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32]

    # Y-axis kind for the vol surface (e.g., strike, log-moneyness).
    vol_surface_y_axis_type_id: npt.NDArray[np.int8]
```

```python
# src/riskquant/portfolio/blocks/vanilla_forward_block.py
"""
Compiled blocks for vanilla forward trades.

Architectural contract
----------------------
* Row `i` of every array corresponds to the trade with UID `trade_uids[i]`.
* Every `_uix` field is a zero-based, contiguous index produced by the
  portfolio engine's `UixMapper` — NOT a global static-config ID.
* FX legs are carried as `fx_*_to_settle_effective_uix` indexing the
  deduplicated `MarketDataMatrix.fx_spots_effective` matrix. No invert
  flags survive into the block layer — inversion is folded into the
  effective matrix at the builder edge.
* `INVALID_UIX (< 0)` in any optional `_uix` field signals "no factor
  required for this trade" and is the only branch the pricer kernel
  performs (hoisted outside the scenario loop).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import ClassVar

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.portfolio.blocks.base_block import Block
from riskquant.portfolio.instruments import (
    VANILLA_FORWARD_CURVE_ID,
    VANILLA_FORWARD_NODE_ID,
)


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class _VanillaForwardBaseBlock(Block):
    """Common SoA fields shared by all vanilla forward blocks."""

    # ========== TRADE IDENTITY ==========
    trade_uids: npt.NDArray[np.int64]

    # ========== ECONOMIC TERMS ==========
    qty: npt.NDArray[np.float64]
    strike: npt.NDArray[np.float64]

    # ========== SETTLEMENT DATE (DATE UIX) ==========
    # Index into `unique_dates_ord32` for cashflow settlement.
    settlement_date_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32]

    # ========== FX LINKAGES (EFFECTIVE UIX) ==========
    # Index into `MarketDataMatrix.fx_spots_effective` for the under->settle
    # transform. INVALID_UIX (< 0) when no FX needed for this leg.
    fx_under_to_settle_effective_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32]

    # Index into `MarketDataMatrix.fx_spots_effective` for the strike->settle
    # transform. INVALID_UIX (< 0) when no FX needed for this leg.
    fx_strike_to_settle_effective_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32]

    # ========== DISCOUNT LINKAGES (CURVE UIX) ==========
    # Indices into `MarketDataMatrix.discount_factor_grid` axis 0.
    # INVALID_UIX (< 0) when no discount applies on that leg.
    under_discount_curve_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32]
    strike_discount_curve_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32]


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class VanillaForwardNodeBlock(_VanillaForwardBaseBlock):
    """Forward priced directly off a scalar node."""

    INSTRUMENT_ID: ClassVar[int] = int(VANILLA_FORWARD_NODE_ID)

    # ========== UNDERLYING MARKET LINKAGE ==========
    # Index into `MarketDataMatrix.forward_nodes` axis 0.
    price_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32]


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class VanillaForwardCurveBlock(_VanillaForwardBaseBlock):
    """Forward priced off a curve requiring delivery-date interpolation."""

    INSTRUMENT_ID: ClassVar[int] = int(VANILLA_FORWARD_CURVE_ID)

    # ========== DELIVERY DATE (DATE UIX) ==========
    delivery_date_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32]

    # ========== UNDERLYING MARKET LINKAGE ==========
    # Index into `MarketDataMatrix.price_curve_grid` axis 0.
    price_curve_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32]
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\portfolio\compiled.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/portfolio/compiled.py

"""
Compiled portfolio container.

`CompiledPortfolio` is a pure portfolio-layer artifact: the frozen, valuation-
ready output of block compilation. It holds compiled SoA blocks plus the
metadata the engine needs to walk them — and nothing from the valuation layer.

Layering
--------
This module imports only `portfolio.blocks`. It deliberately does NOT import
pricers, risk evaluators, or the `Evaluator` protocol. Evaluator resolution is
the valuation engine's responsibility: it dispatches on each block's
`INSTRUMENT_ID` class constant via its own `BLOCK_EVALUATORS` table.

The dependency therefore points one way only:

    valuation  ──▶  portfolio        (allowed)
    portfolio  ──▶  valuation        (never)

The previous design bound pricer callables into `_evaluators` inside
`__post_init__`, which made a data container reach up into the valuation layer
and created the `compiled <-> valuation.engine` import cycle. Removing those
references eliminates the cycle by construction — there is no edge left to
break, deferred-import trick or otherwise.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Iterator
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import TypeAlias

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.portfolio.blocks.spot_block import SpotBlock
from riskquant.portfolio.blocks.vanilla_european_option_block import (
    VanillaEuropeanOptionCurveBlock,
    VanillaEuropeanOptionNodeBlock,
)
from riskquant.portfolio.blocks.vanilla_forward_block import (
    VanillaForwardCurveBlock,
    VanillaForwardNodeBlock,
)

BlockT: TypeAlias = (
    # --- Market Risk ---
    SpotBlock
    | VanillaForwardNodeBlock
    | VanillaForwardCurveBlock
    | VanillaEuropeanOptionNodeBlock
    | VanillaEuropeanOptionCurveBlock
)


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class CompiledPortfolio:
    """
    Valuation-ready portfolio: an ordered set of compiled SoA blocks.

    Each populated block contributes one contiguous row-band to the eventual
    (n_trades x n_scenarios) valuation matrix; `block_offsets[i]` is the first
    row of block `i`. `trade_uids` aggregates every block's uids into one flat
    int64 array in the same order, so a portfolio-row index maps straight to a
    trade uid without per-block bookkeeping.
    """

    # Unique dates across the portfolio, as ordinals truncated to int32 for
    # engine-friendly storage and fast comparisons. Sorted ascending, unique.
    unique_dates_ord32: npt.NDArray[np.int32] = field(
        default_factory=lambda: np.empty(0, dtype=np.int32)
    )

    # Instrument blocks (SoA arrays); None blocks are compacted in __post_init__.
    spot: SpotBlock | None = None
    vanilla_forward_node: VanillaForwardNodeBlock | None = None
    vanilla_forward_curve: VanillaForwardCurveBlock | None = None
    vanilla_european_option_node: VanillaEuropeanOptionNodeBlock | None = None
    vanilla_european_option_curve: VanillaEuropeanOptionCurveBlock | None = None

    # Derived, set in __post_init__.
    _blocks: tuple[BlockT, ...] = field(init=False, repr=False)
    _block_offsets: npt.NDArray[np.int64] = field(init=False, repr=False)
    _total_blocks: int = field(init=False, repr=False)
    _total_trades: int = field(init=False, repr=False)
    _trade_uids: npt.NDArray[np.int64] = field(init=False, repr=False)

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        # Construction-ordered slots; None slots are compacted out below. This
        # tuple order defines block ordering and therefore output-matrix bands.
        ordered: tuple[BlockT | None, ...] = (
            self.spot,
            self.vanilla_forward_node,
            self.vanilla_forward_curve,
            self.vanilla_european_option_node,
            self.vanilla_european_option_curve,
        )
        blocks: tuple[BlockT, ...] = tuple(b for b in ordered if b is not None)
        total_blocks: int = len(blocks)

        # Offsets via exclusive prefix-sum of per-block trade counts.
        block_offsets = np.zeros(total_blocks, dtype=np.int64)
        trade_counts = np.fromiter(
            (b.num_trades for b in blocks), dtype=np.int64, count=total_blocks
        )
        total_trades = int(trade_counts.sum())
        if total_blocks > 1:
            np.cumsum(trade_counts[:-1], out=block_offsets[1:])

        # Aggregate every block's uids into one flat array in portfolio order.
        trade_uids = np.empty(total_trades, dtype=np.int64)
        offset = 0
        for block in blocks:
            n = block.num_trades
            trade_uids[offset : offset + n] = block.trade_uids
            offset += n

        object.__setattr__(self, "_blocks", blocks)
        object.__setattr__(self, "_block_offsets", block_offsets)
        object.__setattr__(self, "_total_blocks", total_blocks)
        object.__setattr__(self, "_total_trades", total_trades)
        object.__setattr__(self, "_trade_uids", trade_uids)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ #
    # Read-only accessors
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ #

    @property
    def blocks(self) -> tuple[BlockT, ...]:
        return self._blocks

    @property
    def block_offsets(self) -> npt.NDArray[np.int64]:
        return self._block_offsets

    @property
    def total_blocks(self) -> int:
        return self._total_blocks

    @property
    def total_trades(self) -> int:
        return self._total_trades

    @property
    def trade_uids(self) -> npt.NDArray[np.int64]:
        return self._trade_uids

    def iter_blocks(self) -> Iterator[BlockT]:
        yield from self._blocks

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ #
    # Diagnostics
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ #

    def summary(self) -> None:
        """Print a console-friendly overview of block layout and trade counts."""
        print("=" * 90)
        print(f"{'COMPILED PORTFOLIO SUMMARY':^90}")
        print("=" * 90)
        print(f"Total Trades: {self._total_trades:,}")
        print(f"Total Blocks: {self._total_blocks}")
        print(f"Unique Dates: {len(self.unique_dates_ord32)}")
        print("-" * 90)
        print(f"{'Block Type':<32} | {'Trades':<10} | {'Offset'}")
        print("-" * 90)

        if self._total_blocks == 0:
            print(f"{'Empty Portfolio':^90}")
        else:
            for i, block in enumerate(self._blocks):
                print(
                    f"{type(block).__name__:<32} | "
                    f"{block.num_trades:<10,} | {self._block_offsets[i]}"
                )
        print("=" * 90 + "\n")

    def print_block_details(
        self,
        *,
        max_items: int | None = 10,
        float_precision: int = 0,
        sep: str = ", ",
        pad: int = 12,
        thousand_sep: str = "_",
    ) -> None:
        """Cascade into each block and print its raw SoA arrays."""
        print("=" * 90)
        print(f"{f'BLOCK DETAILS (MAX {max_items} ITEMS PER BLOCK)':^90}")
        print("=" * 90 + "\n")
        for block in self._blocks:
            title = f"Block: {type(block).__name__}"
            print("-" * len(title))
            print(title)
            print("-" * len(title))
            block.print_block(
                max_items=max_items,
                float_precision=float_precision,
                sep=sep,
                pad=pad,
                thousand_sep=thousand_sep,
            )
        print("=" * 90 + "\n")
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/portfolio/compilers/instruments/spot.py
"""
Spot block compiler.

Translates the spot-instrument sub-range of an `IngestBatch` (global IDs) into
a `SpotBlock` (execution UIXs) via the run's `UixMapper`. The only fields
that need translation are:
  - `price_uix`            : underlying_ids        -> market_id_universe
  - `fx_spots_effective_uix`: (fx_u2s_ids, fx_u2s_inv) -> fx_effective_pairs

`qty` and `trade_uids` are pure economics — no translation needed; slicing
returns NumPy views (zero copy).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import TYPE_CHECKING

from riskquant.portfolio.blocks.spot_block import SpotBlock
from riskquant.portfolio.ingest.batch import IngestBatch

if TYPE_CHECKING:
    from riskquant.portfolio.engine import UixMapper


def compile_spot(
    batch: IngestBatch,
    start: int,
    end: int,
    uix_mapper: UixMapper,
) -> SpotBlock | None:
    """Slice + UIX-translate a spot run from `batch[start:end]`."""
    if start == end:
        return None

    # Underlying price IDs -> market-universe UIXs.
    price_uix = uix_mapper.map_market_ids_to_uix(batch.underlying_ids[start:end])

    # FX leg (only the under->settle leg matters for spot).
    fx_spots_effective_uix = uix_mapper.map_fx_legs_to_effective_uix(
        raw_fx_ids=batch.fx_u2s_ids[start:end],
        raw_fx_inv=batch.fx_u2s_inv[start:end],
    )

    return SpotBlock(
        trade_uids=batch.uids[start:end],
        qty=batch.qty[start:end],
        price_uix=price_uix,
        fx_spots_effective_uix=fx_spots_effective_uix,
    )
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/portfolio/compilers/block_compilers/vanilla_european_option.py
"""
Vanilla European option block compilers (node and curve variants).

Translate a sub-range of an `IngestBatch` into a
`VanillaEuropeanOption*Block` via the run's `UixMapper`:
  - Underlying market ID  -> market_id_universe (price_uix / price_curve_uix)
  - Volatility market ID  -> market_id_universe (vol_uix / vol_surface_uix)
  - Discount curve IDs    -> discount_curve_id_universe
  - FX leg pairs          -> fx_effective_pairs
  - Expiry / settle / delivery ordinals -> date_ord32_universe

`qty`, `strike`, `cp_mult`, `trade_uids`, `vol_surface_y_axis_type_id` are
pure economic / typed fields — zero-copy slicing only.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import TYPE_CHECKING

from riskquant.portfolio.blocks.vanilla_european_option_block import (
    VanillaEuropeanOptionCurveBlock,
    VanillaEuropeanOptionNodeBlock,
)
from riskquant.portfolio.ingest.batch import IngestBatch

if TYPE_CHECKING:
    from riskquant.portfolio.engine import UixMapper


def compile_vanilla_euro_option_node(
    batch: IngestBatch,
    start: int,
    end: int,
    uix_mapper: UixMapper,
) -> VanillaEuropeanOptionNodeBlock | None:
    """Slice + UIX-translate a vanilla-european-option-node run."""
    if start == end:
        return None

    underlying_price_uix = uix_mapper.map_market_ids_to_uix(
        batch.underlying_ids[start:end]
    )
    vol_uix = uix_mapper.map_market_ids_to_uix(batch.volatility_ids[start:end])

    expiry_date_uix = uix_mapper.map_dates_to_uix(batch.expiry_ord[start:end])
    settlement_date_uix = uix_mapper.map_dates_to_uix(batch.settle_ord[start:end])

    fx_u2s_eff = uix_mapper.map_fx_legs_to_effective_uix(
        raw_fx_ids=batch.fx_u2s_ids[start:end],
        raw_fx_inv=batch.fx_u2s_inv[start:end],
    )
    fx_k2s_eff = uix_mapper.map_fx_legs_to_effective_uix(
        raw_fx_ids=batch.fx_k2s_ids[start:end],
        raw_fx_inv=batch.fx_k2s_inv[start:end],
    )

    under_df_uix = uix_mapper.map_discount_ids_to_uix(
        batch.under_discount_curve_ids[start:end]
    )
    strike_df_uix = uix_mapper.map_discount_ids_to_uix(
        batch.strike_discount_curve_ids[start:end]
    )

    return VanillaEuropeanOptionNodeBlock(
        trade_uids=batch.uids[start:end],
        qty=batch.qty[start:end],
        strike=batch.strike_price[start:end],
        cp_mult=batch.cp_mult[start:end],
        expiry_date_uix=expiry_date_uix,
        settlement_date_uix=settlement_date_uix,
        fx_under_to_settle_effective_uix=fx_u2s_eff,
        fx_strike_to_settle_effective_uix=fx_k2s_eff,
        under_discount_curve_uix=under_df_uix,
        strike_discount_curve_uix=strike_df_uix,
        underlying_price_uix=underlying_price_uix,
        vol_uix=vol_uix,
    )


def compile_vanilla_euro_option_curve(
    batch: IngestBatch,
    start: int,
    end: int,
    uix_mapper: UixMapper,
) -> VanillaEuropeanOptionCurveBlock | None:
    """Slice + UIX-translate a vanilla-european-option-curve run."""
    if start == end:
        return None

    underlying_price_curve_uix = uix_mapper.map_market_ids_to_uix(
        batch.underlying_ids[start:end]
    )
    vol_surface_uix = uix_mapper.map_market_ids_to_uix(batch.volatility_ids[start:end])

    expiry_date_uix = uix_mapper.map_dates_to_uix(batch.expiry_ord[start:end])
    settlement_date_uix = uix_mapper.map_dates_to_uix(batch.settle_ord[start:end])
    delivery_date_uix = uix_mapper.map_dates_to_uix(batch.delivery_ord[start:end])

    fx_u2s_eff = uix_mapper.map_fx_legs_to_effective_uix(
        raw_fx_ids=batch.fx_u2s_ids[start:end],
        raw_fx_inv=batch.fx_u2s_inv[start:end],
    )
    fx_k2s_eff = uix_mapper.map_fx_legs_to_effective_uix(
        raw_fx_ids=batch.fx_k2s_ids[start:end],
        raw_fx_inv=batch.fx_k2s_inv[start:end],
    )

    under_df_uix = uix_mapper.map_discount_ids_to_uix(
        batch.under_discount_curve_ids[start:end]
    )
    strike_df_uix = uix_mapper.map_discount_ids_to_uix(
        batch.strike_discount_curve_ids[start:end]
    )

    return VanillaEuropeanOptionCurveBlock(
        trade_uids=batch.uids[start:end],
        qty=batch.qty[start:end],
        strike=batch.strike_price[start:end],
        cp_mult=batch.cp_mult[start:end],
        expiry_date_uix=expiry_date_uix,
        settlement_date_uix=settlement_date_uix,
        delivery_date_uix=delivery_date_uix,
        fx_under_to_settle_effective_uix=fx_u2s_eff,
        fx_strike_to_settle_effective_uix=fx_k2s_eff,
        under_discount_curve_uix=under_df_uix,
        strike_discount_curve_uix=strike_df_uix,
        underlying_price_curve_uix=underlying_price_curve_uix,
        vol_surface_uix=vol_surface_uix,
        vol_surface_y_axis_type_id=batch.vol_surface_y_axis_type_ids[start:end],
    )
```

```python
# src/riskquant/portfolio/compilers/block_compilers/vanilla_forward.py
"""
Vanilla forward block compilers (node and curve variants).

Translate a sub-range of an `IngestBatch` into a `VanillaForward*Block` via
the run's `UixMapper`:
  - Underlying market ID  -> market_id_universe        (price_uix / price_curve_uix)
  - Discount curve IDs    -> discount_curve_id_universe
  - FX leg pairs          -> fx_effective_pairs
  - Settlement / delivery ordinals -> date_ord32_universe

    VanillaForwardCurveBlock,
    VanillaForwardNodeBlock,
)

    from riskquant.portfolio.engine import UixMapper

    batch: IngestBatch,

        raw_fx_ids=batch.fx_u2s_ids[start:end],
        raw_fx_inv=batch.fx_u2s_inv[start:end],
    )
    fx_k2s_eff = uix_mapper.map_fx_legs_to_effective_uix(
        raw_fx_ids=batch.fx_k2s_ids[start:end],
        raw_fx_inv=batch.fx_k2s_inv[start:end],

    )

    batch: IngestBatch,
    start: int,
    end: int,

) -> VanillaForwardCurveBlock | None:

        return None

    delivery_date_uix = uix_mapper.map_dates_to_uix(batch.delivery_ord[start:end])

    fx_u2s_eff = uix_mapper.map_fx_legs_to_effective_uix(

    fx_k2s_eff = uix_mapper.map_fx_legs_to_effective_uix(
        raw_fx_ids=batch.fx_k2s_ids[start:end],
        raw_fx_inv=batch.fx_k2s_inv[start:end],

    under_df_uix = uix_mapper.map_discount_ids_to_uix(
        batch.under_discount_curve_ids[start:end]

        batch.strike_discount_curve_ids[start:end]
```
