```python
# src/riskquant/portfolio/ingest/pipeline.py

"""
Top-level ingest orchestrator.This wires the full
edge pipeline so a raw, human-facing frame is normalized and validated before
any SoA payload is produced.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import pandas as pd

from riskquant.config.static_config.static_config import RiskStaticConfig
from riskquant.portfolio.ingest.batch import IngestBatch
from riskquant.portfolio.ingest.canonicalize import canonicalize_raw_ingest_df
from riskquant.portfolio.ingest.compile import (
    compile_ingest_soa_payload_from_df,
)
from riskquant.portfolio.ingest.dispatcher import dispatch_ingest_validation
from riskquant.portfolio.ingest.materialize import (
    materialize_canonicalized_ingest_df,
)


def compile_raw_df_into_ingest_batch(
    *,
    raw_df: pd.DataFrame,
    static_config: RiskStaticConfig,
    copy: bool = False,
) -> IngestBatch:
    """
    Run the full ingest pipeline: raw frame -> validated SoA `IngestBatch`.

    Stages
    ------
    1. canonicalize : column names, ordering, string normalization (in place)
    2. materialize  : strict dtype coercion (in place)
    3. dispatch     : table-driven per-instrument validation; returns int32 ids
    4. compile      : DataFrame -> flat SoA `CompiledIngestSoA`
    5. pack         : wrap into the immutable `IngestBatch` contract

    Parameters
    ----------
    raw_df
        Human-facing positions frame (market-risk and/or JTD credit rows).
    static_config
        The full `RiskStaticConfig` is required to validate the raw frame and
        compile the SoA arrays.
    copy
        When True (default) the caller's frame is left untouched: stages 1-2
        mutate a private copy. Set False only when the caller already owns a
        throwaway frame and wants to avoid the edge allocation.
    """
    # Canonicalize / materialize mutate in place; copy once at the edge so the
    # caller's frame is never clobbered.
    df = raw_df.copy() if copy else raw_df

    canonicalize_raw_ingest_df(df)
    materialize_canonicalized_ingest_df(df)

    # Validation routing also yields the int32 instrument ids, so the
    # `instrument` strings are hash-mapped exactly once for the whole pipeline.
    instrument_ids = dispatch_ingest_validation(df)

    # Compile the canonicalized, validated frame into the flat SoA payloads expected
    # by the portfolio engine. This is the last step before packing into the immutable
    # `IngestBatch` contract.
    compiled_ingest_soa = compile_ingest_soa_payload_from_df(
        df=df,
        instrument_ids=instrument_ids,
        static_config=static_config,
    )
    # compiled_ingest_soa.print_soa()

    return IngestBatch.from_compiled_ingest_soa(compiled_ingest_soa)
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\portfolio\ingest\rf_resolver.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/portfolio/ingest/rf_resolver.py

"""
Vectorized resolution of human-facing risk-factor name strings into the int32
Universe Indices (UIX) consumed by the compiled blocks and JIT kernels.

Sibling of `fx_resolver.py`: that module resolves FX-pair strings; this one
resolves single-name risk factors (underlyings, discount curves, price/vol
factors). Together they are the layer where "strings die early" — every name
column is collapsed to a flat C-contiguous np.int32 array before any data
leaves the ingest edge.

All resolution is performed through pandas `Series.map` (a C-level hash join).
There are no Python row loops, no `.apply`, and no regex matching.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Mapping
from typing import cast

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt
import pandas as pd

from riskquant.exceptions import PrettyKeyError

# A canonicalized string column uses the empty string as its missing sentinel
# (canonicalize.py maps NA -> "" on extraction). "" is never a valid RF name.
_BLANK: str = ""


def _detect_unmapped(
    names: npt.NDArray[np.str_],
    mapped: pd.Series,
    blank_mask: npt.NDArray[np.bool_],
    *,
    ctx: str,
) -> None:
    """Raise on names that are populated but absent from the lookup table."""
    unmapped = mapped.isna().to_numpy(copy=False) & ~blank_mask
    if not unmapped.any():
        return

    bad = sorted({str(v) for v in names[unmapped]})[:10]
    raise PrettyKeyError(
        f"{ctx}: {int(unmapped.sum())} name(s) could not be resolved to a "
        f"risk-factor universe index.\nUnresolved names:\n{bad}"
    )


def resolve_required_ids(
    names: npt.NDArray[np.str_],
    mapping: dict[str, int],
    *,
    ctx: str,
) -> npt.NDArray[np.int32]:
    """
    Resolve a name column where every row MUST carry a mappable name.

    Parameters
    ----------
    names
        Flat string array (blanks are treated as errors here).
    mapping
        Name -> universe-index lookup table.
    ctx
        Human-readable column label for diagnostics.
    """
    blank_mask = names == _BLANK
    mapped = pd.Series(names).map(mapping)
    _detect_unmapped(names, mapped, blank_mask, ctx=ctx)

    if blank_mask.any():
        bad = np.flatnonzero(blank_mask)[:10].tolist()
        raise PrettyKeyError(f"{ctx}: required name missing at rows {bad}.")
    # No NaNs survive past the guards above -> direct int32 narrowing is safe.
    arr = np.ascontiguousarray(mapped.to_numpy(dtype=np.int32))
    return cast(npt.NDArray[np.int32], arr)


def resolve_optional_ids(
    names: npt.NDArray[np.str_],
    mapping: Mapping[str, int | np.int32],
    *,
    sentinel: int | np.integer,
    ctx: str,
) -> npt.NDArray[np.int32]:
    """
    Resolve a name column where blank rows are legitimately absent.

    Blank rows collapse to `sentinel`. A populated-but-unmapped name is still a
    hard error: it signals a typo or a stale universe rather than absence.

    Parameters
    ----------
    names
        Flat string array; blank rows ("") map to `sentinel`.
    mapping
        Name -> universe-index lookup table.
    sentinel
        Typed sentinel (from riskquant.constants) for "no linkage".
    ctx
        Human-readable column label for diagnostics.
    """
    n = names.shape[0]
    blank_mask = names == _BLANK
    mapped = pd.Series(names).map(mapping)

    # Edge allocation; downstream kernels never touch this array directly.
    out = np.empty(n, dtype=np.int32)
    out.fill(np.int32(sentinel))

    populated = ~blank_mask
    if populated.any():
        out[populated] = mapped.to_numpy(dtype=np.float64)[populated].astype(np.int32)

    return out  # np.empty is already C-contiguous
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\portfolio\ingest\schema.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/portfolio/ingest/schema.py

"""
Structural ingest schema definitions.

This module defines the canonical structural ingest contract for RiskQuant
portfolio ingestion. It validates ONLY structural invariants (column presence,
uniqueness, allowed universe). Semantic invariants (value ranges, cross-column
logic, instrument correctness) belong in the validation dispatcher.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Final

# ==============================================================================
# CORE REQUIRED SCHEMA CONTRACT (REQUIRED FOR ALL INGEST BATCHES)
# ==============================================================================

UNIVERSAL_REQUIRED_COLS: Final[tuple[str, ...]] = (
    "uid",
    "instrument",
)

# ==============================================================================
# DOMAIN COLUMN REGISTRIES (REFLECTING DIFFERENT RISK DOMAINS)
# ==============================================================================

# Market-risk-related columns. May not be required for all instruments.
MARKET_DOMAIN_COLS: Final[tuple[str, ...]] = (
    "quantity",
    "underlying",
    "settlement_ccy",
)

# Credit-exposure linkage columns. Optional for all instruments.
CREDIT_EXPOSURE_DOMAIN_COLS: Final[tuple[str, ...]] = ("netting_set_id",)

# # JTD loss model-related columns. May not be required for all instruments.
# JTD_LOSS_MODEL_DOMAIN_COLS: Final[tuple[str, ...]] = (
#     "ead",
#     "pd",
#     "lgd",
# )

# ==============================================================================
# INSTRUMENT EXTENSION COLS
# ==============================================================================

MARKET_DOMAIN_EXTENSION_COLS: Final[tuple[str, ...]] = (
    "strike_price",
    "strike_ccy",
    "is_call",
    "expiry_date",
    "delivery_date",
    "settlement_date",
    "under_discount_curve",
    "strike_discount_curve",
    # Container-name pointer for the option's implied-vol source. Optional
    # at the structural layer (SPOT / FORWARD rows leave it None); the
    # OPTION family validator enforces non-null.
    "volatility_container_name",
)

# # `beta` is the single-factor copula loading consumed by the JTD block compilers.
# JTD_LOSS_MODEL_DOMAIN_EXTENSION_COLS: Final[tuple[str, ...]] = (
#     "beta",
#     "systemic_factor",
#     "idiosyncratic_factor",
#     "chi_squared_factor",
# )

# ==============================================================================
# GLOBAL STRUCTURAL REGISTRY
# ==============================================================================

ALL_ALLOWED_COLS_SET: Final[frozenset[str]] = frozenset(
    UNIVERSAL_REQUIRED_COLS
    + MARKET_DOMAIN_COLS
    # + JTD_LOSS_MODEL_DOMAIN_COLS
    + CREDIT_EXPOSURE_DOMAIN_COLS
    + MARKET_DOMAIN_EXTENSION_COLS
    # + JTD_LOSS_MODEL_DOMAIN_EXTENSION_COLS
)

CANONICAL_COLUMN_ORDER: Final[tuple[str, ...]] = (
    UNIVERSAL_REQUIRED_COLS
    + MARKET_DOMAIN_COLS
    # + JTD_LOSS_MODEL_DOMAIN_COLS
    + CREDIT_EXPOSURE_DOMAIN_COLS
    + MARKET_DOMAIN_EXTENSION_COLS
    # + JTD_LOSS_MODEL_DOMAIN_EXTENSION_COLS
)

# ==============================================================================
# TYPE REGISTRIES
# ==============================================================================

STRING_COLS: Final[tuple[str, ...]] = (
    # Market-specific string columns
    "instrument",
    "underlying",
    "strike_ccy",
    "settlement_ccy",
    "under_discount_curve",
    "strike_discount_curve",
    "volatility_container_name",
    # # JTD loss model-specific string columns
    # "systemic_factor",
    # "idiosyncratic_factor",
    # "chi_squared_factor",
)

FLOAT_COLS: Final[tuple[str, ...]] = (
    # Market-specific float columns
    "quantity",
    "strike_price",
    # # JTD loss model-specific float columns
    # "ead",
    # "pd",
    # "lgd",
    # "beta",
)

INT_COLS: Final[tuple[str, ...]] = ("uid",)

# Nullable integer columns: exact integers, NA permitted (NA -> typed sentinel
# downstream). Distinct from INT_COLS, which are strictly non-nullable.
NULLABLE_INT_COLS: Final[tuple[str, ...]] = ("netting_set_id",)

FLAG_COLS: Final[tuple[str, ...]] = ("is_call",)

DATE_COLS: Final[tuple[str, ...]] = (
    "delivery_date",
    "settlement_date",
    "expiry_date",
)

DATETIME_COLS: Final[tuple[str, ...]] = ()

ALL_TYPED_COLS: Final[tuple[str, ...]] = (
    STRING_COLS
    + FLOAT_COLS
    + INT_COLS
    + NULLABLE_INT_COLS
    + FLAG_COLS
    + DATE_COLS
    + DATETIME_COLS
)

if len(ALL_TYPED_COLS) != len(set(ALL_TYPED_COLS)):
    raise RuntimeError("Column appears in multiple dtype registries.")

ALL_TYPED_COLS_SET: Final[frozenset[str]] = frozenset(ALL_TYPED_COLS)

typed_missing = ALL_ALLOWED_COLS_SET - ALL_TYPED_COLS_SET
if typed_missing:
    raise RuntimeError(
        f"Allowed columns missing dtype registry:\n{sorted(typed_missing)}"
    )

illegal_typed = ALL_TYPED_COLS_SET - ALL_ALLOWED_COLS_SET
if illegal_typed:
    raise RuntimeError(
        f"Typed registry contains unknown columns:\n{sorted(illegal_typed)}"
    )

# ==============================================================================
# NON-NULLABLE REGISTRY
# ==============================================================================

NON_NULLABLE_COLS: Final[tuple[str, ...]] = UNIVERSAL_REQUIRED_COLS

NON_NULLABLE_COLS_SET: Final[frozenset[str]] = frozenset(NON_NULLABLE_COLS)

unknown_non_nullable = NON_NULLABLE_COLS_SET - ALL_ALLOWED_COLS_SET
if unknown_non_nullable:
    raise RuntimeError(
        f"NON_NULLABLE_COLS contains unknown cols:\n{sorted(unknown_non_nullable)}"
    )

if __name__ == "__main__":
    print(ALL_TYPED_COLS)
```

```python
# src/riskquant/portfolio/ingest/utils.py

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd


def is_na_helper(x: object) -> bool:
    """Check if a scalar value is missing or invalid (None, NaN, pd.NA, ±inf)."""
    if x is None:
        # None is considered missing.
        return True

    if x is pd.NA:
        # pd.NA is considered missing.
        return True

    if isinstance(x, (float, np.floating)):
        # Check for NaN or infinite values.
        # np.isfinite returns False for NaN and ±inf, so we return True for those cases.
        # NaNs and infinite values are considered missing/invalid.
        return not np.isfinite(x)

    # For all other types (e.g., int, str), we consider them valid and not missing.
    return False
```

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\portfolio\ingest\validate.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/portfolio/ingest/validate.py

from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt
import pandas as pd

from riskquant.constants import POSITION_DUST_TOLERANCE
from riskquant.portfolio.ingest.schema import STRING_COLS
from riskquant.portfolio.instruments import (
    INSTRUMENT_NAME_TO_ID_REGISTRY,
    INSTRUMENT_TO_FAMILY_ARRAY,
    InstrumentFamily,
)
from riskquant.utils.dataframes import validate_string_cols

# ==============================================================================
# INTERNAL HELPERS
# ==============================================================================


def _require_cols(df: pd.DataFrame, cols: tuple[str, ...]) -> None:
    missing = [c for c in cols if c not in df.columns]
    if missing:
        raise ValueError(f"Missing required columns:\n{missing}")


def _validate_non_null_masked(
    df: pd.DataFrame,
    mask: npt.NDArray[np.bool_],
    cols: tuple[str, ...],
    ctx: str,
) -> None:
    for col in cols:
        arr = df[col].isna().to_numpy(copy=False)
        bad = mask & arr
        if np.any(bad):
            idx = df.index[bad].tolist()[:10]
            raise ValueError(f"{ctx} missing '{col}' at rows: {idx}")


def _validate_ccy_series(
    df: pd.DataFrame,
    col: str,
    mask: npt.NDArray[np.bool_],
    ctx: str,
) -> None:
    ser = df[col]
    valid_idx = np.flatnonzero(mask & ser.notna().to_numpy(copy=False))

    if len(valid_idx) == 0:
        return

    subset = ser.iloc[valid_idx]

    alpha = subset.str.isalpha()
    if not alpha.all():
        idx = subset.index[~alpha].tolist()[:10]
        raise ValueError(f"{ctx} invalid currency (non-alpha) at rows: {idx}")

    length = subset.str.len().eq(3)
    if not length.all():
        idx = subset.index[~length].tolist()[:10]
        raise ValueError(f"{ctx} invalid currency (len!=3) at rows: {idx}")


def _validate_non_zero_quantity(
    df: pd.DataFrame,
    mask: npt.NDArray[np.bool_],
    ctx: str,
) -> None:
    qty = df["quantity"].to_numpy(copy=False)

    with np.errstate(invalid="ignore"):
        dust = np.abs(qty) < POSITION_DUST_TOLERANCE

    bad = mask & dust
    if np.any(bad):
        idx = df.index[bad].tolist()[:25]
        raise ValueError(
            f"{ctx} contain zero / dust quantities.\n"
            f"Tolerance={POSITION_DUST_TOLERANCE}\nRows: {idx}"
        )


# ==============================================================================
# FAST CORE: INSTRUMENT → ID → FAMILY
# ==============================================================================


def _extract_instrument_id_array(
    df: pd.DataFrame,
) -> npt.NDArray[np.int32]:
    ser = df["instrument"]

    id_arr = ser.map(INSTRUMENT_NAME_TO_ID_REGISTRY).to_numpy()

    if np.any(pd.isna(id_arr)):
        bad_mask = pd.isna(id_arr)
        idx = ser.index[bad_mask].tolist()[:10]
        vals = sorted(ser[bad_mask].dropna().unique().tolist())

        raise ValueError(f"Invalid instruments:\n{vals}\nRows: {idx}")

    return id_arr.astype(np.int32, copy=False)


def _derive_family_array(
    inst_id_arr: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
) -> npt.NDArray[np.int8]:
    return np.take(INSTRUMENT_TO_FAMILY_ARRAY, inst_id_arr)


def _mask_eq(
    arr: npt.NDArray,
    val: int,
) -> npt.NDArray[np.bool_]:
    return arr == val


def _mask_in(
    arr: npt.NDArray,
    vals: tuple[int, ...],
) -> npt.NDArray[np.bool_]:
    mask = np.zeros_like(arr, dtype=bool)
    for v in vals:
        mask |= arr == v
    return mask


# ==============================================================================
# FAMILY-LEVEL VALIDATION
# ==============================================================================


def validate_spot_family(
    df: pd.DataFrame,
    mask: npt.NDArray[np.bool_],
) -> None:
    if not np.any(mask):
        return

    cols = (
        "quantity",
        "underlying",
        "settlement_ccy",
    )

    _require_cols(df, cols)
    _validate_non_null_masked(df, mask, cols, "Spot")
    _validate_ccy_series(df, "settlement_ccy", mask, "Spot")
    _validate_non_zero_quantity(df, mask, "Spot")


def validate_forward_family(
    df: pd.DataFrame,
    mask: npt.NDArray[np.bool_],
) -> None:
    if not np.any(mask):
        return

    cols = (
        "quantity",
        "underlying",
        "settlement_ccy",
        "strike_price",
        "strike_ccy",
    )

    _require_cols(df, cols)
    _validate_non_null_masked(df, mask, cols, "Forward")
    _validate_ccy_series(df, "settlement_ccy", mask, "Forward")
    _validate_ccy_series(df, "strike_ccy", mask, "Forward")
    _validate_non_zero_quantity(df, mask, "Forward")


def validate_option_family(
    df: pd.DataFrame,
    mask: npt.NDArray[np.bool_],
) -> None:
    if not np.any(mask):
        return

    cols = (
        "quantity",
        "underlying",
        "settlement_ccy",
        "strike_price",
        "strike_ccy",
        "expiry_date",
        "is_call",
        # OPTION rows MUST point at a vol container; non-option rows
        # may leave the column None. The compiler resolves the name to
        # an int32 container id and derives the y-axis-type id off
        # `surface_meta_dict[id].y_axis_kind`.
        "volatility_container_name",
    )

    _require_cols(df, cols)
    _validate_non_null_masked(df, mask, cols, "Option")
    _validate_ccy_series(df, "settlement_ccy", mask, "Option")
    _validate_ccy_series(df, "strike_ccy", mask, "Option")
    _validate_non_zero_quantity(df, mask, "Option")


# ==============================================================================
# INSTRUMENT-LEVEL VALIDATION (SECONDARY DISPATCH)
# ==============================================================================

# ---> SPOT: N/A
# ---> VANILLA FORWARD NODE / CURVE: TODO
# ---> VANILLA EUROPEAN OPTION NODE / CURVE: TODO


# ==============================================================================
# GLOBAL VALIDATORS
# ==============================================================================


def validate_unique_uids(df: pd.DataFrame) -> None:
    ser = df["uid"]
    if not ser.is_unique:
        dupes = ser[ser.duplicated()].unique().tolist()
        raise ValueError(f"Duplicate UIDs:\n{dupes[:10]}")


# ==============================================================================
# MAIN ENTRYPOINT
# ==============================================================================


def validate_materialized_ingest_df(df: pd.DataFrame) -> None:
    """
    High-performance semantic validation.
    """

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # 1. Global
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    validate_unique_uids(df)
    validate_string_cols(df, STRING_COLS)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # 2. Instrument → ID
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    inst_id = _extract_instrument_id_array(df)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # 3. ID → Family
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    family = _derive_family_array(inst_id)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # 4. Masks (pure NumPy)
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    # --- FAMILY MASKS (PRIMARY DISPATCH) ---
    spot_mask = _mask_eq(family, InstrumentFamily.SPOT.value)
    fwd_mask = _mask_eq(family, InstrumentFamily.FORWARD.value)
    opt_mask = _mask_eq(family, InstrumentFamily.OPTION.value)

    # --- INSTRUMENT MASKS (SECONDARY DISPATCH) ---
    # (none yet, but this is where they'd go once we have any instrument-level validators)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # 5. Validation passes
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    # --- FAMILY LAYER (PRIMARY DISPATCH) ---

    # Family layer
    validate_spot_family(df, spot_mask)
    validate_forward_family(df, fwd_mask)
    validate_option_family(df, opt_mask)

    # --- INSTRUMENT REFINEMENT (SECONDARY DISPATCH) ---
```

```python
# src/riskquant/portfolio/instruments.py

from __future__ import annotations

from enum import IntEnum
from typing import Final

# ============================================================
# INSTRUMENT (ABI-STABLE IDENTIFIERS)
# ============================================================


class Instrument(IntEnum):
    """
    Instrument = dispatch identifier for evaluation units.

    This includes:
    - tradable contracts (spot, forwards, options,bonds, swaps, etc)
    - stochastic loss processes (credit JTD)

    Behavior is defined by EvaluationTopology, NOT by instrument name.
    """

    # --- Market Risk ---
    SPOT = 0

    VANILLA_FORWARD_NODE = 1
    VANILLA_FORWARD_CURVE = 2

    VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_NODE = 3
    VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_CURVE = 4


NUM_INSTRUMENTS: Final[int] = len(Instrument)


INSTRUMENT_NAMES = frozenset(inst.name for inst in Instrument)

# ============================================================
# IDS FOR DISPATCH (SINGLE SOURCE OF TRUTH)
# ============================================================

SPOT_ID: Final[int] = Instrument.SPOT.value
VANILLA_FORWARD_NODE_ID: Final[int] = Instrument.VANILLA_FORWARD_NODE.value
VANILLA_FORWARD_CURVE_ID: Final[int] = Instrument.VANILLA_FORWARD_CURVE.value
VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_NODE_ID: Final[int] = (
    Instrument.VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_NODE.value
)
VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_CURVE_ID: Final[int] = (
    Instrument.VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_CURVE.value
)


# ============================================================
# EVALUATION TOPOLOGY (PRIMARY DISPATCH AXIS)
# ============================================================


class EvaluationTopology(IntEnum):
    """
    Defines HOW an object is evaluated.
    This is the primary driver of execution strategy.
    """

    MTM = 0  # direct evaluation (spot, forwards)
    PATH_DEPENDENT = 1  # depends on full path
    OPTIMAL_STOPPING = 2  # requires backward induction


# ============================================================
# OPTIONAL: INSTRUMENT FAMILY (NON-DISPATCH)
# ============================================================


class InstrumentFamily(IntEnum):
    SPOT = 0
    FORWARD = 1
    OPTION = 2
    CREDIT = 3


# ============================================================
# CORE REGISTRIES (SINGLE SOURCE OF TRUTH)
# ============================================================


INSTRUMENT_TO_TOPOLOGY: Final[dict[Instrument, EvaluationTopology]] = {
    # --- MTM ---
    Instrument.SPOT: EvaluationTopology.MTM,
    Instrument.VANILLA_FORWARD_NODE: EvaluationTopology.MTM,
    Instrument.VANILLA_FORWARD_CURVE: EvaluationTopology.MTM,
    # --- Options ---
    Instrument.VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_NODE: EvaluationTopology.MTM,
    Instrument.VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_CURVE: EvaluationTopology.MTM,
}


INSTRUMENT_TO_FAMILY: Final[dict[Instrument, InstrumentFamily]] = {
    Instrument.SPOT: InstrumentFamily.SPOT,
    Instrument.VANILLA_FORWARD_NODE: InstrumentFamily.FORWARD,
    Instrument.VANILLA_FORWARD_CURVE: InstrumentFamily.FORWARD,
    Instrument.VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_NODE: InstrumentFamily.OPTION,
    Instrument.VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_CURVE: InstrumentFamily.OPTION,
}


# ============================================================
# VALIDATION (HARD FAIL ON DRIFT)
# ============================================================


_ALL = frozenset(Instrument)

if _ALL - frozenset(INSTRUMENT_TO_TOPOLOGY):
    raise RuntimeError("Missing topology mappings")

if _ALL - frozenset(INSTRUMENT_TO_FAMILY):
    raise RuntimeError("Missing family mappings")


# ============================================================
# FAST DISPATCH ARRAYS (CRITICAL FOR PERFORMANCE)
# ============================================================


# Dense arrays for O(1) lookup without dicts
INSTRUMENT_TO_TOPOLOGY_ARRAY: Final[list[int]] = [
    INSTRUMENT_TO_TOPOLOGY[Instrument(i)] for i in range(0, NUM_INSTRUMENTS)
]

INSTRUMENT_TO_FAMILY_ARRAY: Final[list[int]] = [
    INSTRUMENT_TO_FAMILY[Instrument(i)] for i in range(0, NUM_INSTRUMENTS)
]


# ============================================================
# EDGE (STRING) REGISTRIES — ONLY FOR I/O
# ============================================================


INSTRUMENT_NAME_TO_INSTRUMENT_REGISTRY: Final[dict[str, Instrument]] = {
    inst.name: inst for inst in Instrument
}

INSTRUMENT_NAME_TO_ID_REGISTRY: Final[dict[str, int]] = {
    inst.name: inst.value for inst in Instrument
}

INSTRUMENT_ID_TO_NAME_REGISTRY: Final[list[str]] = [
    Instrument(i).name for i in range(0, NUM_INSTRUMENTS)
]


# ============================================================
# INSTRUMENT FAMILY TO INSTRUMENT NAME REGISTRIES
# ============================================================


# Family → list of instrument names (all instruments in a family)
FAMILY_TO_INSTRUMENT_NAMES: Final[dict[InstrumentFamily, list[str]]] = {
    family: [inst.name for inst, fam in INSTRUMENT_TO_FAMILY.items() if fam == family]
    for family in InstrumentFamily
}

# Family → list of instrument IDs
FAMILY_TO_INSTRUMENT_IDS: Final[dict[InstrumentFamily, list[int]]] = {
    family: [inst.value for inst, fam in INSTRUMENT_TO_FAMILY.items() if fam == family]
    for family in InstrumentFamily
}

# Family name (string) → list of instrument names
FAMILY_NAME_TO_INSTRUMENT_NAMES: Final[dict[str, list[str]]] = {
    family.name: [
        inst.name for inst, fam in INSTRUMENT_TO_FAMILY.items() if fam == family
    ]
    for family in InstrumentFamily
}


if __name__ == "__main__":
    # Instument.
    print("\nInstruments:")
    for inst in Instrument:
        print(f"  {inst.name} (ID={inst.value})")

    # Sanity check: print all registries
    print("\nINSTRUMENT_TO_TOPOLOGY:")
    for inst, topo in INSTRUMENT_TO_TOPOLOGY.items():
        print(f"  {inst.name} → {topo.name}")

    print("\nINSTRUMENT_TO_FAMILY:")
    for inst, fam in INSTRUMENT_TO_FAMILY.items():
        print(f"  {inst.name} → {fam.name}")

    print("\nFAMILY_TO_INSTRUMENT_NAMES:")
    for fam, insts in FAMILY_TO_INSTRUMENT_NAMES.items():
        print(f"  {fam.name} → {insts}")

    print("\n --- END: ALL CHECKS PASSED --- \n")
```

```python
# src/riskquant/portfolio/validation.py
"""
Pre-compile validation of `IngestBatch` global-ID references against the
`UixMapper` universes.

Runs ONCE between universe discovery (`build_uix_mapper`) and block
compilation. Catches any global ID or ordinal that the upstream resolver
emitted but the universe-builder didn't see — i.e. a referential-integrity
bug somewhere in the ingest pipeline. By doing this before compile, the
hot-path `map_*_to_uix` calls can stay branch-light and trust their input.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Iterable, Sequence

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.portfolio.engine import UixMapper
from riskquant.portfolio.ingest.batch import IngestBatch


def validate_ingest_batches_against_uix_universe(
    *,
    ingest_batches: Sequence[IngestBatch],
    uix_mapper: UixMapper,
) -> None:
    """
    Validate that every non-sentinel ID and ordinal referenced by the
    ingest batches is present in the corresponding `UixMapper` universe.

    Raises `ValueError` with the first batch of offending values it finds,
    grouped by universe.
    """
    # --- MARKET IDs -------------------------------------------------------
    _check_arrays_against_universe(
        arrays=(
            arr
            for b in ingest_batches
            for arr in (
                b.underlying_ids,
                b.volatility_ids,
                b.fx_u2s_ids,
                b.fx_k2s_ids,
            )
        ),
        universe=uix_mapper.market_id_universe,
        label="market IDs",
    )

    # --- DISCOUNT CURVE IDs -----------------------------------------------
    _check_arrays_against_universe(
        arrays=(
            arr
            for b in ingest_batches
            for arr in (
                b.under_discount_curve_ids,
                b.strike_discount_curve_ids,
            )
        ),
        universe=uix_mapper.discount_curve_id_universe,
        label="discount-curve IDs",
    )

    # --- DATES ------------------------------------------------------------
    _check_arrays_against_universe(
        arrays=(
            arr
            for b in ingest_batches
            for arr in (b.settle_ord, b.delivery_ord, b.expiry_ord)
        ),
        universe=uix_mapper.date_ord32_universe,
        label="ordinal dates",
    )


def _check_arrays_against_universe(
    *,
    arrays: Iterable[npt.NDArray[np.int32]],
    universe: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
    label: str,
) -> None:
    """
    For every input array, assert that every value >= 0 also appears in
    `universe`. Vectorized: one `searchsorted` per input array, no Python
    loop over rows.
    """
    for arr in arrays:
        populated = arr[arr >= 0]
        if populated.size == 0:
            continue

        idx = np.searchsorted(universe, populated)
        in_bounds = idx < universe.size

        matches = np.zeros(populated.shape[0], dtype=bool)
        matches[in_bounds] = universe[idx[in_bounds]] == populated[in_bounds]

        if not matches.all():
            missing = np.unique(populated[~matches])
            raise ValueError(
                f"Unknown {label} encountered during UIX validation: {missing}"
            )
```

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\simlab\builder\shock_universe.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/simlab/builder/shock_universe.py
"""
Canonical shock-universe builder.

Deduplicates `(source_factor_id, shift_type_id)` pairs into K unique
shocks. Each unique shock can be computed once and broadcast to every
required exposure that maps onto it.

Hot-path discipline
-------------------
- No Python loop over per-required ids — dedup is one `np.unique` pass.
- First-seen ordering preserved across reruns of identical inputs so
  downstream hashes are deterministic.
- Bit-packed int64 keys: high 56 bits = `source_factor_id` (int32,
  sign-extended into int64), low 8 bits = `shift_type_id` (uint8 cast
  from int8). The pack/unpack invariant requires
  `0 <= shift_type_id < 128`; enforced at input.
- `available_factor_ids` arrives as a sorted-ascending int32 array
  (the `RiskFactorMatrix` invariant), so the downstream compute-plan
  resolution is one `np.searchsorted`.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.simlab.shock_plan.plan import (
    ShockComputePlan,
    build_shock_compute_plan,
)


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class ShockUniverse:
    """
    Canonical definition of the shock space.

    Deterministic and reusable; zero-allocation at runtime once built.
    """

    # --- UNIQUE SHOCK KEYS (length K) ---
    shock_source_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int32]
    shock_shift_type_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int8]

    # --- REQUIRED -> SHOCK MAPPING (length Nreq) ---
    required_to_shock_uix: npt.NDArray[np.int32]

    # --- COMPUTE PLAN ---
    compute_plan: ShockComputePlan

    # --- RAW PACKED KEYS (debug / hashing; length K) ---
    shock_keys: npt.NDArray[np.int64]


# ============================================================
# Key packing
# ============================================================


_SHIFT_TYPE_BITS: int = 8
_SHIFT_TYPE_MASK: int = (1 << _SHIFT_TYPE_BITS) - 1  # 0xFF


def _pack_keys(
    source_factor_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
    shift_type_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int8],
) -> npt.NDArray[np.int64]:
    """
    Pack `(source_id, shift_type_id)` into int64.

    `source_id` fits in 32 bits but we promote to int64 so the left-shift
    doesn't lose the high bit. `shift_type_id` is masked to its low 8
    bits so a future sign-bit ambiguity can't poison the key.
    """
    src64 = source_factor_ids.astype(np.int64, copy=False)
    st64 = shift_type_ids.astype(np.int64, copy=False) & np.int64(_SHIFT_TYPE_MASK)
    return (src64 << _SHIFT_TYPE_BITS) | st64


# ============================================================
# First-seen dedup
# ============================================================


def _unique_first_seen_int64(
    arr: npt.NDArray[np.int64],
) -> tuple[npt.NDArray[np.int64], npt.NDArray[np.int32]]:
    """
    Vectorised first-seen dedup.

    Returns
    -------
    unique_first_seen
        (K,) int64 — unique values in first-seen order.
    inverse_first_seen
        (N,) int32 — for each input position, the index in
        `unique_first_seen`.
    """
    if arr.size == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.int64), np.empty(0, dtype=np.int32)

    _, first_idx, inverse_sorted = np.unique(
        arr, return_index=True, return_inverse=True
    )
    order = np.argsort(first_idx, kind="mergesort")
    unique_first_seen = arr[first_idx[order]]
    sorted_to_first_seen = np.empty(order.size, dtype=np.int32)
    sorted_to_first_seen[order] = np.arange(order.size, dtype=np.int32)
    inverse_first_seen = sorted_to_first_seen[inverse_sorted].astype(
        np.int32, copy=False
    )
    return unique_first_seen, inverse_first_seen


# ============================================================
# Public API
# ============================================================


def build_shock_universe(
    *,
    required_shift_type_id: npt.NDArray[np.int8],
    source_factor_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
    available_factor_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
) -> ShockUniverse:
    """
    Build a canonical `ShockUniverse` from proxy-resolution outputs.

    Parameters
    ----------
    required_shift_type_id
        (Nreq,) int8 — shift type id per required exposure.
    source_factor_ids
        (Nreq,) int32 — resolved source rf per required (from
        `ProxyResolution.source_factor_ids`).
    available_factor_ids
        (Ntotal,) int32 — sorted-ascending unique factor ids exposed
        by the generator's risk-factor matrix.
    """
    _smoke_test_inputs(required_shift_type_id, source_factor_ids)

    # --- BIT-PACK KEYS ---
    keys = _pack_keys(source_factor_ids, required_shift_type_id)

    # --- DEDUPLICATE (first-seen) ---
    unique_keys, req_to_uix = _unique_first_seen_int64(keys)

    # Unpack unique keys back to int32 / int8 components.
    shock_source_ids = (unique_keys >> _SHIFT_TYPE_BITS).astype(np.int32, copy=False)
    shift_low = (unique_keys & np.int64(_SHIFT_TYPE_MASK)).astype(np.uint8, copy=False)
    shock_shift_type_ids = shift_low.astype(np.int8, copy=False)

    # --- BUILD COMPUTE PLAN ---
    compute_plan = build_shock_compute_plan(
        shock_source_ids=shock_source_ids,
        shock_shift_type_ids=shock_shift_type_ids,
        factor_ids=available_factor_ids,
    )

    return ShockUniverse(
        shock_source_ids=shock_source_ids,
        shock_shift_type_ids=shock_shift_type_ids,
        required_to_shock_uix=req_to_uix,
        compute_plan=compute_plan,
        shock_keys=unique_keys,
    )


# ============================================================
# Input validation
# ============================================================


def _smoke_test_inputs(
    required_shift_type_id: npt.NDArray[np.int8],
    source_factor_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
) -> None:
    if required_shift_type_id.ndim != 1:
        raise TypeError("required_shift_type_id must be 1D.")
    if source_factor_ids.ndim != 1:
        raise TypeError("source_factor_ids must be 1D.")
    if required_shift_type_id.dtype != np.int8:
        raise TypeError(
            f"required_shift_type_id must be int8; got {required_shift_type_id.dtype}"
        )
    if source_factor_ids.dtype != np.int32:
        raise TypeError(
            f"source_factor_ids must be int32; got {source_factor_ids.dtype}"
        )
    if required_shift_type_id.shape != source_factor_ids.shape:
        raise ValueError(
            "required_shift_type_id and source_factor_ids must have the same shape; "
            f"got {required_shift_type_id.shape} and {source_factor_ids.shape}"
        )
    if required_shift_type_id.size == 0:
        return
    # The bit-pack uses the low 8 bits and assumes the shift_type_id is
    # non-negative. int8 can represent up to 127.
    if required_shift_type_id.min() < 0:
        raise ValueError(
            "required_shift_type_id must be >= 0 for the bit-packed dedup key."
        )
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\simlab\runner.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/simlab/runner.py
"""
SIMLAB orchestrator.

Streaming VaR engine: holds the compiled portfolio, the UIX universe,
the universe-to-flat-buffer mapping, and a scenario generator. Each
`run(snapshot)` call:

  1. Extracts the t0 base-levels vector from the injected snapshot.
  2. Prices the base portfolio once (single-scenario MD matrix).
  3. Streams scenario batches from the generator, applies shocks to
     base levels into a pre-allocated `(N_req, batch_size)` buffer,
     hydrates a `MarketDataMatrix` view over that buffer, prices the
     batch, and writes the PVs into the master scenario-PV grid.
  4. Subtracts base PVs in place and returns a float32 PnL matrix.

DOD properties
--------------
- All hot-path buffers pre-allocated in `run()` exactly once.
- Apply-shocks buffer validation runs ONCE at first batch, not per
  batch.
- `scenario_pvs` is laid out as `(n_trades, n_scenarios)` C-contiguous
  so the per-batch PV writes are unit-stride along the inner axis.
- The terminal `np.float32` cast writes into a pre-allocated buffer
  to avoid the peak-memory spike of `.astype(np.float32)`.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from datetime import date

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

# Config & Resolution
from riskquant.config.proxy_config.proxy_topology import ProxyTopology
from riskquant.config.static_config.static_config import RiskStaticConfig

# State & Valuation
from riskquant.market_data.builder import build_market_data_matrix
from riskquant.market_data.risk_factors.snapshot import RiskFactorLevelSnapshot
from riskquant.market_data.state import MarketDataMatrix
from riskquant.pnl.results import PnlMatrix
from riskquant.portfolio.compiled import CompiledPortfolio
from riskquant.portfolio.engine import UixMapper
from riskquant.proxy_resolver.proxy import (
    ProxyResolution,
    resolve_required_factor_ids_to_market_data_factor_ids,
)
from riskquant.simlab.scenarios.base import ScenarioGenerator
from riskquant.simlab.shocks.apply import (
    apply_shocks_to_base_levels,
    validate_apply_shocks_buffers,
)
from riskquant.valuation.engine import run_valuation

# =========================================================================
# UNIVERSE MAPPING
# =========================================================================


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class UniverseMapping:
    """
    Slicing plan to extract the structured market-data arrays from one
    flat `(N_req, B)` levels matrix.

    The `idx_*` slices index the leading factor axis; reshape information
    for curves and surfaces lives in the `shape_*` triples.

    All `np.ndarray` fields hold per-config metadata pinned to the run's
    universe. Validation lives at build time; the runner trusts the
    contract.
    """

    required_factor_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int32]
    required_shift_type_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int8]

    # Scalars
    idx_fx_spots: slice
    idx_prices: slice
    idx_fwd_nodes: slice
    idx_vol_nodes: slice

    # Curves / surfaces
    idx_discount_curves: slice
    shape_discount_curves: tuple[int, int]  # (C_curves, M_tenors)

    idx_price_curves: slice
    shape_price_curves: tuple[int, int]

    idx_vol_surfaces: slice
    shape_vol_surfaces: tuple[int, int, int]  # (C_surfaces, M_tenors, K_strikes)

    # 1-D curve configs
    curve_interp_methods: npt.NDArray[np.int8]
    curve_extrap_methods: npt.NDArray[np.int8]
    raw_discount_tenors: npt.NDArray[np.float64]
    raw_price_curve_tenors: npt.NDArray[np.float64]

    # 2-D surface configs
    surface_interp_x_methods: npt.NDArray[np.int8]
    surface_extrap_x_methods: npt.NDArray[np.int8]
    surface_y_axis_type_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int8]
    raw_vol_surface_tenors: npt.NDArray[np.float64]
    raw_vol_surface_strikes: npt.NDArray[np.float64]


# =========================================================================
# ORCHESTRATOR
# =========================================================================


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class SimlabRunner:
    """
    Immutable orchestrator for the SIMLAB risk engine.

    Acts as a compiled compute topology: once constructed, every call
    to `run(snapshot)` reuses the same proxy-resolution, universe map,
    UIX mapper, and pre-allocated hot-path buffers (allocated inside
    `run` and reused for the duration of that call).
    """

    config: RiskStaticConfig
    proxy_topology: ProxyTopology
    portfolio: CompiledPortfolio
    uix_mapper: UixMapper
    universe_map: UniverseMapping
    generator: ScenarioGenerator
    as_of_date: date
    batch_size: int = 500

    # Cache topology mapping, NOT state.
    _proxy_resolution: ProxyResolution = field(init=False, repr=False)

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if self.batch_size <= 0:
            raise ValueError("batch_size must be positive")

        # Single proxy resolution per runner lifetime.
        proxy_res = resolve_required_factor_ids_to_market_data_factor_ids(
            static_config=self.config,
            proxy_topology=self.proxy_topology,
            required_factor_ids=self.universe_map.required_factor_ids,
            market_data_factor_ids=self.generator.available_factor_ids,
        )
        object.__setattr__(self, "_proxy_resolution", proxy_res)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Hot path
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def run(self, snapshot: RiskFactorLevelSnapshot) -> PnlMatrix:
        """
        Run the streaming VaR loop against an injected market snapshot.

        Returns a `PnlMatrix` whose `pnl` field is float32
        `(n_trades, n_scenarios)`.
        """
        n_trades = self.portfolio.total_trades
        n_scenarios = self.generator.total_scenarios

        # ---- 1. t0 base levels ----
        base_levels = self._extract_base_levels(
            required_ids=self.universe_map.required_factor_ids,
            snapshot=snapshot,
        )
        n_req_factors = base_levels.shape[0]

        # ---- 2. Pre-allocate hot-path buffers ----
        # `scenario_pvs` is C-contig: per-batch writes hit unit-stride
        # along the inner scenario axis.
        scenario_pvs = np.empty(
            (n_trades, n_scenarios),
            dtype=np.float64,
            order="C",
        )
        shocked_levels_buf = np.empty(
            (n_req_factors, self.batch_size),
            dtype=np.float64,
            order="C",
        )

        # ---- 3. Base valuation (t0) ----
        base_state = self._hydrate_state(
            flat_levels=base_levels[:, None],
            num_scenarios=1,
        )
        base_result = run_valuation(
            portfolio=self.portfolio,
            market_data_mtx=base_state,
        )
        base_pvs = base_result.values  # (n_trades, 1)

        # ---- 4. Streaming hot loop ----
        first_batch = True
        for batch in self.generator.iter_batches(
            required_shift_type_id=self.universe_map.required_shift_type_ids,
            source_factor_ids=self._proxy_resolution.source_factor_ids,
            shock_scale=self._proxy_resolution.shock_scale,
            batch_size=self.batch_size,
        ):
            start = batch.scenario_offset
            current_b = batch.shocks.shape[0]
            end = start + current_b

            out_view = shocked_levels_buf[:, :current_b]

            # One-shot layout/dtype validation at first batch. Hot
            # path trusts the contract afterwards.
            if first_batch:
                validate_apply_shocks_buffers(
                    base_levels=base_levels,
                    shocks=batch.shocks,
                    out=out_view,
                )
                first_batch = False

            # Step A — apply shocks (zero allocation).
            apply_shocks_to_base_levels(
                base_levels=base_levels,
                batch=batch,
                out=out_view,
            )

            # Step B — hydrate market state (zero-copy slicing).
            market_state = self._hydrate_state(
                flat_levels=out_view,
                num_scenarios=current_b,
            )

            # Step C — price the batch.
            batch_result = run_valuation(
                portfolio=self.portfolio,
                market_data_mtx=market_state,
            )

            # Step D — write PVs into the master grid.
            scenario_pvs[:, start:end] = batch_result.values

        # ---- 5. PnL = scenario PV - base PV, in place ----
        # Broadcast subtraction against the (n_trades, 1) base PVs.
        scenario_pvs -= base_pvs

        # ---- 6. Narrow-cast into a fresh float32 buffer ----
        # Allocating the result up front and using np.copyto with the
        # cast is equivalent to .astype() but lets us drop scenario_pvs
        # immediately after — peak-memory wise we still own both
        # buffers across the copy; the caller's GC reclaims scenario_pvs
        # at function exit.
        scenario_pnl_32 = np.empty(scenario_pvs.shape, dtype=np.float32)
        np.copyto(scenario_pnl_32, scenario_pvs, casting="same_kind")

        return PnlMatrix(
            trade_uids=self.portfolio.trade_uids,
            pnl=scenario_pnl_32,
        )

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Helpers
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def _extract_base_levels(
        self,
        *,
        required_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
        snapshot: RiskFactorLevelSnapshot,
    ) -> npt.NDArray[np.float64]:
        """
        Vectorised O(N log K) gather of base levels by required-id.

        `snapshot.factor_ids` is sorted ascending (the Snapshot class
        invariant), enabling one-shot `searchsorted` membership check
        and gather.
        """
        idx = np.searchsorted(snapshot.factor_ids, required_ids)
        idx = np.clip(idx, 0, snapshot.factor_ids.shape[0] - 1)
        if np.any(snapshot.factor_ids[idx] != required_ids):
            missing = required_ids[snapshot.factor_ids[idx] != required_ids]
            raise ValueError(
                f"Compiled portfolio requires risk factors missing from the "
                f"snapshot: {np.unique(missing).tolist()}"
            )
        return np.ascontiguousarray(snapshot.factor_levels[idx]).astype(
            np.float64, copy=False
        )

    def _hydrate_state(
        self,
        *,
        flat_levels: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
        num_scenarios: int,
    ) -> MarketDataMatrix:
        """
        Slice the flat `(N_req, B)` levels matrix into the dimensional
        grids expected by the valuation engine, and call the builder.

        Slicing produces views, not copies. The builder owns its own
        per-build allocations (FX-effective gather, curve grids).
        """
        u = self.universe_map

        # ---- Scalars ----
        raw_fx_spots = flat_levels[u.idx_fx_spots, :]
        raw_prices = flat_levels[u.idx_prices, :]
        raw_forward_nodes = flat_levels[u.idx_fwd_nodes, :]
        raw_vol_nodes = flat_levels[u.idx_vol_nodes, :]

        # ---- Curves ----
        raw_discount_ln_dfs = flat_levels[u.idx_discount_curves, :].reshape(
            u.shape_discount_curves[0],
            u.shape_discount_curves[1],
            num_scenarios,
        )
        raw_price_curve_values = flat_levels[u.idx_price_curves, :].reshape(
            u.shape_price_curves[0],
            u.shape_price_curves[1],
            num_scenarios,
        )

        # ---- Surfaces ----
        raw_vol_surface_values = flat_levels[u.idx_vol_surfaces, :].reshape(
            u.shape_vol_surfaces[0],
            u.shape_vol_surfaces[1],
            u.shape_vol_surfaces[2],
            num_scenarios,
        )

        return build_market_data_matrix(
            as_of_date=self.as_of_date,
            uix_mapper=self.uix_mapper,
            num_scenarios=num_scenarios,
            raw_fx_spots=raw_fx_spots,
            raw_prices=raw_prices,
            raw_forward_nodes=raw_forward_nodes,
            raw_vol_nodes=raw_vol_nodes,
            raw_discount_tenors=u.raw_discount_tenors,
            raw_discount_ln_dfs=raw_discount_ln_dfs,
            raw_price_curve_tenors=u.raw_price_curve_tenors,
            raw_price_curve_values=raw_price_curve_values,
            curve_interp_methods=u.curve_interp_methods,
            curve_extrap_methods=u.curve_extrap_methods,
            raw_vol_surface_tenors=u.raw_vol_surface_tenors,
            raw_vol_surface_strikes=u.raw_vol_surface_strikes,
            raw_vol_surface_values=raw_vol_surface_values,
            surface_interp_x_methods=u.surface_interp_x_methods,
            surface_extrap_x_methods=u.surface_extrap_x_methods,
            surface_y_axis_type_ids=u.surface_y_axis_type_ids,
        )
```

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\simlab\scenarios\base.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/simlab/scenarios/base.py
"""
Scenario-generator protocol, batch container, and the one canonical entry
point for resolving a required-factor universe into a ShockUniverse +
ShockComputePlan.

Design
------
`ScenarioGenerator` is a streaming protocol: generators yield
`ScenarioSetBatch` chunks of fixed `batch_size` instead of materialising
the full (T, K) shock matrix. The runner holds one pre-allocated
shocked-levels buffer and consumes each batch in place.

`resolve_shock_universe` replaces the old dict-based
`build_factor_id_to_col_index` + `build_shock_key_plan` helpers. It
takes `RiskFactorMatrix.factor_ids` (sorted ascending int32) directly
and uses `np.searchsorted` for O(K log N) vectorised resolution.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Iterator
from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Protocol

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.simlab.builder.shock_universe import (
    ShockUniverse,
    build_shock_universe,
)

# ============================================================
# Streaming-batch payload
# ============================================================


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class ScenarioSetBatch:
    """
    One streaming chunk of scenarios in shock-space.

    `shocks` is F-contiguous `(B, K_req)` so the apply-shocks kernel
    reads down the scenario axis with unit stride. `required_to_shock_uix`
    maps required-exposure rows back to columns of `shocks`.
    """

    shocks: np.ndarray
    required_to_shock_uix: np.ndarray
    shock_scale: np.ndarray
    required_shift_type_id: np.ndarray
    scenario_offset: int
    scenario_count_total: int | None = None


# ============================================================
# Generator protocol
# ============================================================


class ScenarioGenerator(Protocol):
    """Streaming-first scenario generation."""

    total_scenarios: int
    available_factor_ids: np.ndarray

    def iter_batches(
        self,
        *,
        required_shift_type_id: np.ndarray,  # (Nreq,) int8
        source_factor_ids: np.ndarray,  # (Nreq,) int32
        shock_scale: np.ndarray,  # (Nreq,) float32
        batch_size: int,
    ) -> Iterator[ScenarioSetBatch]: ...


# ============================================================
# Vectorised universe resolution
# ============================================================


def resolve_shock_universe(
    *,
    required_shift_type_id: npt.NDArray[np.int8],
    source_factor_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
    available_factor_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
) -> ShockUniverse:
    """
    Build a `ShockUniverse` from the run's resolved-source universe and
    the generator's available-factor index.

    `available_factor_ids` MUST be sorted ascending and unique (the
    `RiskFactorMatrix` invariant). This lets us replace the dict-based
    lookup with one vectorised `np.searchsorted` inside
    `build_shock_universe`.

    The previous two-stage helper pair (`build_factor_id_to_col_index`
    + `build_shock_key_plan`) is gone — both were Python-loop with
    dict-lookup anti-patterns. This is the single entry point all
    generators use.
    """
    return build_shock_universe(
        required_shift_type_id=required_shift_type_id,
        source_factor_ids=source_factor_ids,
        available_factor_ids=available_factor_ids,
    )
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\simlab\scenarios\bootstrap_fhs.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/simlab/scenarios/bootstrap_fhs.py
"""
Bootstrapped Filtered Historical Simulation generator.

Methodology
-----------
1. AR(1) residualisation of historical returns.
2. EWMA volatility ratios Z_t = sigma_today / sigma_t.
3. Pure i.i.d. bootstrap of single days drawn uniformly from
   [1, T-1] to construct h-day paths.

Why no block bootstrap: the upstream AR(1) + EWMA pipeline has already
whitened the series, so block-stitching reintroduces artificial seam
artefacts. Per-day i.i.d. is the cleaner aggregator on filtered data.

Implementation
--------------
- AR(1) + EWMA scratch buffers cached lazily on the generator instance
  (same scheme as `FilteredHistoricalGenerator`).
- All MC path indices drawn upfront from one `rng.integers` call.
- Streaming `shocks` buffer pre-allocated once at `batch_size`.
- Kernel pinned, branch-hoisted, called positionally.
- Shocks are always float32.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Iterator
from dataclasses import dataclass, field

import numba as nb  # type: ignore[import-untyped]
import numpy as np

from riskquant._njit import parallel_njit
from riskquant.constants import EWMA_DECAY_FACTOR, MAX_EWMA_VOL_RATIO
from riskquant.market_data.risk_factors.matrix import RiskFactorMatrix
from riskquant.market_data.risk_factors.shift_types import (
    SHIFT_DIFFERENCE_ID,
    SHIFT_LOG_RETURN_ID,
    SHIFT_SIMPLE_RETURN_ID,
)
from riskquant.simlab.scenarios.base import (
    ScenarioGenerator,
    ScenarioSetBatch,
    resolve_shock_universe,
)
from riskquant.simlab.scenarios.core_math.ar1_filter import kernel_ar1_residuals
from riskquant.simlab.scenarios.core_math.ewma_volatility import (
    kernel_ewma_vol_ratios_from_residuals,
)

_F64_2D_F = nb.float64[::1, :]
_F32_2D_F = nb.float32[::1, :]
_I32_1D = nb.int32[::1]
_I32_2D_C = nb.int32[:, ::1]
_I8_1D = nb.int8[::1]


# =========================================================================
# ZERO-ALLOCATION KERNEL
# =========================================================================


@parallel_njit(
    nb.void(
        _F64_2D_F,  # residuals          (T, K_req) F-contig
        _F64_2D_F,  # vol_ratios         (T, K_req) F-contig
        _I32_1D,  # cols               (K_req,)  — debug parity
        _I8_1D,  # shift_types        (K_req,)
        _I32_1D,  # inv_perm           (K_req,)
        _I32_2D_C,  # random_t_indices   (B, h) C-contig
        nb.int64,  # h
        nb.int64,  # B
        _F32_2D_F,  # out                (B, K_req) F-contig
    ),
)
def _kernel_fused_filtered_bootstrap_h_day_shock(
    residuals,
    vol_ratios,
    cols,
    shift_types,
    inv_perm,
    random_t_indices,
    h,
    batch_len,
    out,
) -> None:
    """
    Aggregate vol-scaled residuals over a synthetic i.i.d. path.
    """
    k_cols = cols.shape[0]
    for k in nb.prange(k_cols):
        st = shift_types[k]
        out_col = inv_perm[k]

        col_res = residuals[:, k]
        col_vols = vol_ratios[:, k]
        out_col_vals = out[:, out_col]

        if st == SHIFT_LOG_RETURN_ID:
            for b in range(batch_len):
                cum_shock = 0.0
                for step in range(h):
                    t1 = random_t_indices[b, step]
                    cum_shock += col_res[t1] * col_vols[t1]
                out_col_vals[b] = np.float32(cum_shock)

        elif st == SHIFT_SIMPLE_RETURN_ID:
            for b in range(batch_len):
                cum_ret = 1.0
                for step in range(h):
                    t1 = random_t_indices[b, step]
                    cum_ret *= 1.0 + col_res[t1] * col_vols[t1]
                out_col_vals[b] = np.float32(cum_ret - 1.0)

        elif st == SHIFT_DIFFERENCE_ID:
            for b in range(batch_len):
                cum_diff = 0.0
                for step in range(h):
                    t1 = random_t_indices[b, step]
                    cum_diff += col_res[t1] * col_vols[t1]
                out_col_vals[b] = np.float32(cum_diff)


# =========================================================================
# GENERATOR
# =========================================================================


@dataclass(slots=True, kw_only=True)
class FilteredBootstrapGenerator(ScenarioGenerator):
    """
    Bootstrapped FHS generator. Shocks are always float32.

    Not `frozen=True` because `ar1_residuals` and `vol_ratios` are
    populated lazily on first `iter_batches()` call.
    """

    matrix: RiskFactorMatrix
    total_scenarios: int
    horizon: int = field(default=1)
    decay_factor: float = field(default=EWMA_DECAY_FACTOR)
    max_vol_ratio: float = field(default=MAX_EWMA_VOL_RATIO)
    seed: int | None = field(default=None)

    # Derived in __post_init__.
    available_factor_ids: np.ndarray = field(init=False, repr=False)

    # Lazy caches.
    _ar1_residuals: np.ndarray | None = field(default=None, init=False, repr=False)
    _vol_ratios: np.ndarray | None = field(default=None, init=False, repr=False)
    _cache_signature: tuple | None = field(default=None, init=False, repr=False)

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if self.matrix.values.shape[0] < 3:
            raise ValueError("History too short for EWMA calculation.")
        if self.total_scenarios <= 0:
            raise ValueError("total_scenarios must be positive")
        if self.horizon < 1:
            raise ValueError("horizon must be >= 1")
        self.available_factor_ids = self.matrix.factor_ids

    def reset_caches(self) -> None:
        """Drop the AR(1) + EWMA scratch caches."""
        self._ar1_residuals = None
        self._vol_ratios = None
        self._cache_signature = None

    def _ensure_filter_caches(
        self, plan_cols: np.ndarray, plan_shifts: np.ndarray
    ) -> None:
        sig = (
            plan_cols.tobytes(),
            plan_shifts.tobytes(),
            float(self.decay_factor),
            float(self.max_vol_ratio),
        )
        if self._cache_signature == sig:
            return

        t_max = self.matrix.values.shape[0]
        k_req = plan_cols.shape[0]

        ar1 = np.empty((t_max, k_req), dtype=np.float64, order="F")
        kernel_ar1_residuals(
            self.matrix.values,
            plan_cols,
            plan_shifts,
            ar1,
        )

        vols = np.empty((t_max, k_req), dtype=np.float64, order="F")
        kernel_ewma_vol_ratios_from_residuals(
            ar1,
            float(self.decay_factor),
            float(self.max_vol_ratio),
            vols,
        )

        self._ar1_residuals = ar1
        self._vol_ratios = vols
        self._cache_signature = sig

    def iter_batches(
        self,
        *,
        required_shift_type_id: np.ndarray,
        source_factor_ids: np.ndarray,
        shock_scale: np.ndarray,
        batch_size: int,
    ) -> Iterator[ScenarioSetBatch]:
        t_max = self.matrix.values.shape[0]
        h = int(self.horizon)

        shock_universe = resolve_shock_universe(
            required_shift_type_id=required_shift_type_id,
            source_factor_ids=source_factor_ids,
            available_factor_ids=self.matrix.factor_ids,
        )
        plan = shock_universe.compute_plan
        k_req = plan.cols_sorted.shape[0]

        self._ensure_filter_caches(plan.cols_sorted, plan.shift_types_sorted)
        assert self._ar1_residuals is not None
        assert self._vol_ratios is not None

        # Single MC draw for all paths upfront. Valid residual indices
        # are [1, T-1] (t=0 has no return).
        rng = np.random.default_rng(self.seed)
        master_t_indices = rng.integers(
            low=1,
            high=t_max,
            size=(self.total_scenarios, h),
            dtype=np.int32,
        )

        shocks_buf = np.empty((batch_size, k_req), dtype=np.float32, order="F")

        scenario_offset = 0
        while scenario_offset < self.total_scenarios:
            batch_len = min(batch_size, self.total_scenarios - scenario_offset)
            shocks_view = shocks_buf[:batch_len, :]
            batch_t_indices = master_t_indices[
                scenario_offset : scenario_offset + batch_len
            ]

            _kernel_fused_filtered_bootstrap_h_day_shock(
                self._ar1_residuals,
                self._vol_ratios,
                plan.cols_sorted,
                plan.shift_types_sorted,
                plan.inverse_permutation,
                np.ascontiguousarray(batch_t_indices),
                np.int64(h),
                np.int64(batch_len),
                shocks_view,
            )

            yield ScenarioSetBatch(
                shocks=shocks_view,
                required_to_shock_uix=shock_universe.required_to_shock_uix,
                shock_scale=shock_scale,
                required_shift_type_id=required_shift_type_id,
                scenario_offset=scenario_offset,
                scenario_count_total=self.total_scenarios,
            )

            scenario_offset += batch_len
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\simlab\scenarios\bootstrap_sbb.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/simlab/scenarios/bootstrap_sbb.py
"""
Stationary Block Bootstrap (SBB) scenario generator.

Constructs synthetic h-day paths by stitching contiguous historical
blocks whose lengths are drawn from a Geometric distribution
(parametrised by `expected_block_size`). Per-column aggregation is
log-product, simple-compound, or sum-of-differences according to the
column's shift type.

Implementation
--------------
- All RNG-driven path indices are generated upfront via
  `generate_sbb_path_indices` (RNG draws in NumPy, stitch in a njit
  kernel — see core_math/sbb_block_generator.py).
- Streaming `shocks` buffer is pre-allocated once at `batch_size`.
- Kernel uses a pinned signature with positional args; branch ladder
  hoisted around the B-loop.

References
----------
Politis & Romano (1994); Politis (2003).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import math
from collections.abc import Iterator
from dataclasses import dataclass, field

import numba as nb  # type: ignore[import-untyped]
import numpy as np

from riskquant._njit import parallel_njit
from riskquant.market_data.risk_factors.matrix import RiskFactorMatrix
from riskquant.market_data.risk_factors.shift_types import (
    SHIFT_DIFFERENCE_ID,
    SHIFT_LOG_RETURN_ID,
    SHIFT_SIMPLE_RETURN_ID,
)
from riskquant.simlab.scenarios.base import (
    ScenarioGenerator,
    ScenarioSetBatch,
    resolve_shock_universe,
)
from riskquant.simlab.scenarios.core_math.sbb_block_generator import (
    generate_sbb_path_indices,
)

_F64_2D_F = nb.float64[::1, :]
_F32_2D_F = nb.float32[::1, :]
_I32_1D = nb.int32[::1]
_I32_2D_C = nb.int32[:, ::1]
_I8_1D = nb.int8[::1]


@parallel_njit(
    nb.void(
        _F64_2D_F,  # values            (T, K_total) F-contig
        _I32_1D,  # cols              (K_req,)
        _I8_1D,  # shift_types       (K_req,)
        _I32_1D,  # inv_perm          (K_req,)
        _I32_2D_C,  # random_t_indices  (B, h) C-contig
        nb.int64,  # h
        nb.int64,  # B
        _F32_2D_F,  # out               (B, K_req) F-contig
    ),
)
def _kernel_fused_sbb_h_day_shock(
    values,
    cols,
    shift_types,
    inv_perm,
    random_t_indices,
    h,
    batch_len,
    out,
) -> None:
    """
    h-day aggregation of 1-day returns along the SBB-synthetic path.
    Parallelised across columns for F-contig cache-line usage.
    """
    k_cols = cols.shape[0]
    for k in nb.prange(k_cols):
        col = cols[k]
        st = shift_types[k]
        out_col = inv_perm[k]

        col_vals = values[:, col]
        out_col_vals = out[:, out_col]

        if st == SHIFT_LOG_RETURN_ID:
            for b in range(batch_len):
                cum_ratio = 1.0
                for step in range(h):
                    t1 = random_t_indices[b, step]
                    cum_ratio *= col_vals[t1] / col_vals[t1 - 1]
                out_col_vals[b] = np.float32(math.log(cum_ratio))

        elif st == SHIFT_SIMPLE_RETURN_ID:
            for b in range(batch_len):
                cum_ret = 1.0
                for step in range(h):
                    t1 = random_t_indices[b, step]
                    cum_ret *= col_vals[t1] / col_vals[t1 - 1]
                out_col_vals[b] = np.float32(cum_ret - 1.0)

        elif st == SHIFT_DIFFERENCE_ID:
            for b in range(batch_len):
                cum_diff = 0.0
                for step in range(h):
                    t1 = random_t_indices[b, step]
                    cum_diff += col_vals[t1] - col_vals[t1 - 1]
                out_col_vals[b] = np.float32(cum_diff)


# =========================================================================
# GENERATOR
# =========================================================================


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class StationaryBlockBootstrapGenerator(ScenarioGenerator):
    """SBB generator. Shocks are always float32."""

    matrix: RiskFactorMatrix
    total_scenarios: int
    expected_block_size: float = field(default=1.0)
    horizon: int = field(default=1)
    seed: int | None = field(default=None)

    # Derived in __post_init__.
    available_factor_ids: np.ndarray = field(init=False, repr=False)

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if self.total_scenarios <= 0:
            raise ValueError("total_scenarios must be positive")
        if self.expected_block_size < 1.0:
            raise ValueError("expected_block_size must be >= 1.0")
        if self.horizon < 1:
            raise ValueError("horizon must be >= 1")
        if self.matrix.values.shape[0] < 2:
            raise ValueError(
                f"Matrix history (T={self.matrix.values.shape[0]}) too short "
                "to compute 1-day returns."
            )
        object.__setattr__(self, "available_factor_ids", self.matrix.factor_ids)

    def iter_batches(
        self,
        *,
        required_shift_type_id: np.ndarray,
        source_factor_ids: np.ndarray,
        shock_scale: np.ndarray,
        batch_size: int,
    ) -> Iterator[ScenarioSetBatch]:
        t_max = self.matrix.values.shape[0]
        h = int(self.horizon)

        shock_universe = resolve_shock_universe(
            required_shift_type_id=required_shift_type_id,
            source_factor_ids=source_factor_ids,
            available_factor_ids=self.matrix.factor_ids,
        )
        plan = shock_universe.compute_plan
        k_req = plan.cols_sorted.shape[0]

        # All paths upfront in one njit + RNG burst.
        rng = np.random.default_rng(self.seed)
        master_t_indices = generate_sbb_path_indices(
            t_max=t_max,
            h=h,
            total_scenarios=self.total_scenarios,
            expected_block_size=self.expected_block_size,
            rng=rng,
        )

        shocks_buf = np.empty((batch_size, k_req), dtype=np.float32, order="F")

        scenario_offset = 0
        while scenario_offset < self.total_scenarios:
            batch_len = min(batch_size, self.total_scenarios - scenario_offset)
            shocks_view = shocks_buf[:batch_len, :]
            batch_t_indices = master_t_indices[
                scenario_offset : scenario_offset + batch_len
            ]

            _kernel_fused_sbb_h_day_shock(
                self.matrix.values,
                plan.cols_sorted,
                plan.shift_types_sorted,
                plan.inverse_permutation,
                np.ascontiguousarray(batch_t_indices),
                np.int64(h),
                np.int64(batch_len),
                shocks_view,
            )

            yield ScenarioSetBatch(
                shocks=shocks_view,
                required_to_shock_uix=shock_universe.required_to_shock_uix,
                shock_scale=shock_scale,
                required_shift_type_id=required_shift_type_id,
                scenario_offset=scenario_offset,
                scenario_count_total=self.total_scenarios,
            )

            scenario_offset += batch_len
```
